Chapter 204: The Festival Ritual

Tens of thousands of visitors crowded Shamouti Island's long main street. Most had come from other islands to see the festival. In local costumes they had rented or bought, they were almost indistinguishable from the islanders.

Vendors had sprung up along both sides of the street and called out their wares. By the time Ethan's group arrived, the other people in bird costumes had dispersed. Only Carol stayed with them. Melody had vanished too.

The crush of people slowed them to a crawl. It took five minutes to cover barely a hundred meters.

"Come and look! These pearls are a Shamouti specialty. Every one is full and round!"

"Sakura shells, white conches, yellow ring shells! Jewelry made from every kind of shell!"

"Orange cakes! Try our local treat!"

The voices ran together into a noisy din. Night fell while they walked, and lights across the island made the celebration seem even brighter.

Ethan looked around in amazement. "What a crowd. There are nearly as many people here as at the Indigo League Conference."

Carol chuckled. "It's Shamouti Island's biggest festival. Nothing else all year matters quite as much. You won't find a celebration this grand anywhere in the Orange Islands, not even in Trovitopolis on Mandarin Island South!"

She was proud that her home could host such an event. Ethan could hardly blame her. Everyone wanted to see their hometown thrive.

At one stall, he bought several bird masks and put them on himself, Sabrina, and Misty. They had stumbled into a festival, so they might as well join in. Ash and Tracey could buy their own masks if they wanted them.

A long lantern procession opened a path through the crowd ahead of them. People carried lanterns of many shapes and danced toward the end of the street. The most striking was a huge Gyarados lantern. Several performers worked it up and down until it seemed that a fierce, living Gyarados was swimming past the spectators.

The group took in the sights as they made their slow way forward. When they finally left the main street, the crowd thinned at once. Carol led them farther on to a banquet hall built of straw and stone, with something of a Maya style to it.

Torches filled the hall with warm light. A lavish dinner had been laid out across more than a dozen long tables.

"Come and rest," Carol said. "Please help yourselves to the food. I'll arrange rooms for you after the banquet."

She gave Ethan's group a table of their own, then sat with Maren at another. After five years apart, the two friends surely had plenty to discuss.

Once no one else was close enough to hear, Tracey lowered his voice. "I wonder why they're treating us like this."

"Who cares?" Misty said. "The food and rooms are free. Let's enjoy them."

Ash was more interested in something else. He looked around the hall, eyes wide. "Where did that girl go?"

"Hold on." Misty stared at him. His interest was obvious. "You don't have a crush on her, do you?"

Ash's face turned as red as Misty's had earlier. "No! I'm just curious..."

That was what he said, but he was a ten-year-old boy who had just been kissed by a pretty girl. Of course it had stirred something in him.

Ethan left their teasing alone. He sat quietly, trying to recall the story of Shamouti Island and the three legendary birds. A crazed collector had attacked and captured the birds to obtain Lugia, the God of the Sea. Ash had stopped him in the end. Ethan could no longer remember the details, though, or how the collector was defeated.

No point forcing it. I'll deal with things as they happen.

Sabrina noticed his distant expression and glanced at him. "What are you thinking about? Don't tell me you're thinking about that girl too."

Ethan spread his hands. "Come on. I'm not a lovestruck kid like Ash. Why would I be thinking about a young girl?"

He was privately surprised. Sabrina rarely showed such concern. Was she jealous?

She turned away again. Ethan picked up a pear-shaped fruit from the table and took a bite. It was tender, juicy, and remarkably sweet. A region named for oranges knew how to grow fruit; it tasted better than what he usually found elsewhere.

"Try this. It's delicious!"

At his recommendation, Sabrina and the others each tried one. He was right.

A lovely flute melody drifted through the hall. Everyone turned toward the stage. Melody had changed into a long white dress, with a wreath of flowers on her head and a white veil over it. Moving in a gentle dance, she stepped onto the stage like a fairy.

She held an unusual instrument, and the sweet notes came from her playing it.

"Melody?"

The entire hall fell silent, Carol and Maren included, as everyone watched her dance and listened. Ethan's group had not expected a change of clothes to transform her manner so completely. The willful girl now seemed demure.

The tune was beautiful, soft and lingering, yet it left Ethan with a sense of loss he could not explain. He could appreciate it without liking it.

When the piece ended, Melody stepped down from the stage and approached their long table. With the grand gestures of an actor, she proclaimed:

"When the heavens and earth rage and the world stands at the edge of destruction, the God of the Sea will appear. Together with an exceptional Trainer, it will calm the gods' wrath!"

She knelt between Ethan and Ash and gazed earnestly at them. "Lord Ethan, Lord Ash, if either of you is that exceptional Trainer, please prove it to me!"

Ash looked completely bewildered. Melody found his expression funny. She leaned in and whispered, "Don't get the wrong idea. It's a ritual we do every year. Just play along!"

Chapter 205: Setting Out for Fire Island

"Melody!"

From the next table, Carol gave her sister a helpless look. Melody had always been the darling of the family, and their parents spoiled her. As the older sister, Carol had little hope of keeping her in line.

Melody waved off Carol's objection and turned back to Ethan and Ash.

"Proving yourself isn't hard. There are three little islands nearby: Fire Island, Ice Island, and Lightning Island. Bring back one treasure from the shrine on each island, place all three on the altar here on Shamouti Island, and I'll play the flute of offering to the gods. That's the whole ceremony!"

"Where do we find the treasures?" Ash asked.

"In the shrines, of course!" When she saw him frown, Melody thought he was worried about reaching them. "Don't worry. There's a boat to take you to the islands, and the shrines even have stairs. It's easy."

Ethan exchanged glances with Tracey and the others. The islanders had plainly turned an old legend into an attraction for visitors. They must have placed the treasures in the shrines beforehand. It sounded like an easy task.

Ash was not afraid it would be difficult. He hesitated because he knew he was an ordinary person, not some commander from a prophecy. But Melody's reassurance made him feel as if she had underestimated him. He stood at once.

"Got it. I'll go and see for myself right now!"

Melody's closeness had something to do with his sudden enthusiasm. Looking at her face brought back the kiss she had given him when they first landed.

"There's no hurry," Melody said. "The festival lasts until tomorrow. You can go in the morning. Relax." She caught Ash by the arm and pushed him back into his seat.

Once Ash made up his mind, though, it was hard to change it. He pulled free and stood again.

"If something can be done today, it should be done today. That's how Ino, that's how this humble servant lives!"

Ethan, Misty, and Tracey stared at him. This humble servant? Even his choice of words was affected. Was this really the Ash who was usually so oblivious to romance? A pretty face had gone straight to his head.

His sudden conviction impressed Melody despite herself. It also won Maren over.

"All right, I'll support you!" Maren came over with a smile and tapped her chest. "I'll pilot the boat and take you to Fire Island. It's the closest one."

"Thank you!" Ash said. Then he looked at Ethan. "Are you coming too?"

"Sure." Ethan shrugged. He had nothing else to do here, and staying near Ash seemed the surest way to find himself in the middle of the story.

Sabrina and Misty wanted to come once they heard Ethan was going, but he stopped them.

"It isn't far, so we should be back soon. It's late, and there won't be much to see out there. Wait here and get comfortable."

They did not insist. Sabrina and Misty stayed in the banquet hall with Tracey.

Ethan picked up an apple and ate it in a few bites. Then he left with Ash and Maren for the harbor.

Instead of taking a boat from Shamouti Island, they set out in Maren's speedboat again. As she put it, another person's boat might be better in every way, but her own was the one she knew how to handle.

The engine roared, and the speedboat cut through the waves, racing south from the harbor.

A chill night wind raised goose bumps on their skin. Even within the reach of the boat's lights, the sea around them looked dark. Ethan glanced up. He could see neither moon nor stars; clouds had covered the sky again.

That isn't a good sign.

Before long, thunder sounded overhead. A bright flash of lightning split the sky, and rain began to pour. Heavy drops pelted the boat, driving Ethan and Ash into the cramped wheelhouse to stay dry.

At least the waves had not risen this time. They were spared the violent rocking and the danger of capsizing.

When the rain showed no sign of stopping, Ethan asked, "Miss Maren, how far is Fire Island?"

Maren thought for a moment. "The round trip should take about half an hour, if you're only counting travel time."

Half an hour? Ethan nodded. It was close, then.

Thump!

A faint sound came from the bow. The boat gave a small jolt, as though it had struck something and ridden straight over it.

"Did you hear that?" Ethan asked.

"Hear what?" Maren looked around. "All I heard was the rain."

"I didn't hear anything strange either," Ash said. "Are you sure you didn't imagine it?"

Had he? Neither of them had noticed anything, but Ethan was certain the boat had run over something. He thought he had even heard a few cries. He looked back into the darkness. He could see nothing where they had just passed.

I hope I was mistaken.

The speedboat continued south, leaving a long wake behind it.

Not long after it disappeared, a man, a woman, and a Meowth struggled onto a floating board. It was Team Rocket's trio, still following Ash. Their Magikarp submarine had been smashed to pieces.

Jessie glared after the departing boat and cursed. "Are they blind? They ran right over our submarine!"

James spat out a mouthful of seawater. "Does it matter? It was leaking already and we couldn't use it. If it's broken, it's broken."

"Idiot!" Jessie snapped. "Broken or not, we worked and saved for that submarine, then put it together ourselves! How are we supposed to get across the sea without it?"

"Will you two stop arguing?!" Meowth raked them with his claws. Then he narrowed his eyes and lowered his voice. "I think that was the twerp and his Pikachu on the boat. And the man called Ethan was standing beside him!"

Jessie and James, their faces scratched, stared at him. "Those two met up again? And they're traveling together? Does that mean something big is about to happen?"

Chapter 206: The Stone Shrine

The thing you worried about most always seemed to happen.

If something could go wrong, it would, however small the chance. Engineer Edward Murphy had put forward that rule in 1949. Ethan did not know whether it applied in the Pokemon world, or whether anyone here had ever expressed a similar idea. But after setting out, they had been grateful to face only heavy rain. Now the sea had turned fierce again.

Waves nearly two meters high struck the speedboat one after another, making it impossible to stand steadily. Thunder cracked overhead as lightning tore across the night sky.

Crack! Boom!

Maren had grown up by the sea, but even Ethan could tell they had sailed into a terrible storm.

"What rotten luck. I hope lightning doesn't hit the boat." Maren looked up with a rueful smile. "We went through a storm before reaching Shamouti Island. I thought this trip might be calm, but..."

She sighed to herself. They should have stayed on Shamouti and waited until morning. If she had not offered to take Ash, perhaps they would have avoided this storm altogether.

Back on Shamouti Island, Melody watched the rain through a window of the banquet hall, her brow furrowed. Wind stirred the torches, making shadows writhe around the room as uneasily as her thoughts. Rain blew onto her bangs and white veil, but she paid it no attention. She kept looking in the direction Ethan and the others had gone.

They were bound to be in danger at sea in weather like this. Melody made up her mind.

"Carol, lend me a boat!"

"What?" Carol stared at her. "What are you planning to do at this hour?"

"If I hadn't said those things, they wouldn't have gone out in the rain. I put them in danger over a silly tradition. It's my responsibility!"

Sabrina and Misty looked at Melody with new respect. Their brief acquaintance had made her seem willful, but she knew when something mattered.

As Melody prepared to go into the storm, Sabrina stood. "You aren't to blame. None of us expected the weather to change like this."

Coming from Ethan's traveling companion, the reassurance helped Melody feel a little better. Sabrina had not tried to stop her, either. From the way she rose and spoke, Melody could tell she intended to come along.

"Me too!" Misty said. "I'm worried about them. If you're going to look for them, take me with you."

Tracey looked from one to the other, a chicken leg in one hand and roasted meat in the other. He silently got to his feet to go with Misty.

"You all..." Melody hesitated. She knew she should not ask more people to risk going to sea, but they were determined. "All right. If anything happens, though, don't say I didn't warn you!"

...

Bang!

A huge wave lifted Maren's speedboat and smashed it against a submerged reef off Fire Island. The impact flung it upward before it crashed onto the shore.

Maren and Ash were left dazed by the landing. Only Ethan reached the ground safely, protected by Haunter.

They were on Fire Island, their destination, but the darkness kept them from seeing much of it.

"Ow, my back. I think it nearly broke..." Maren groaned. Despite the pain, she examined her boat. "Some parts of the hull are damaged, but at least we made it ashore in one piece."

Pikachu suddenly cried out, jumped from the boat, and ran into the darkness.

"Pikachu! Where are you going?" Ash shouted. Pikachu did not look back. Something on the island seemed to be drawing it away.

"Pikachu!" Ash had no choice but to run after it.

"Come back!" Maren called as he left the shore. "In this weather, you should stay here!"

Ash had already vanished. Her voice echoed across the pebble-strewn beach.

"Never mind," Ethan said with a smile. "Thank you for bringing us here, Miss Maren. We can handle what comes next. Stay and see what the boat needs; we'll be counting on it to get back."

"All right." Maren shook her head. "You're the Trainers. It's your call. Good luck out there."

Ethan switched on a flashlight and left the shore.

Fire Island, as he had expected, had formed from a volcanic eruption. It was home to the Orange Islands' God of Fire. Almost nothing grew on it; bare rock stretched all around him.

Just as Melody had promised, a man-made stone staircase led deep into the volcano. Ethan sent out Gardevoir and Jolteon to walk beside him as a precaution.

Haunter floated out of his shadow. "Be careful," it said close to his ear. "I feel something strange, just like that Pikachu did. It really does seem as though something is about to happen."

"I understand. Keep watch, all of you."

Ethan began climbing the staircase, whose end he could not see. The closer he came to the volcano, the quieter it grew. Apart from his and his Pokemon's footsteps and the occasional gust of wind, he heard nothing. At least the rain had finally stopped.

He climbed for a long time without incident, but saw no sign of Ash or Pikachu. Only when he reached the crater did he find them on a platform.

"Ash, you got up here fast. Did you find anything?"

Ash turned at the sound of his voice and stepped aside. "Ethan, look at this."

By flashlight, Ethan saw a shrine built of massive stones. Set within it was an oddly shaped bronze statue of a bird.

Chapter 207: Reunion and the God of Thunder

Ethan stepped closer to the stone shrine. A red orb sat in the bronze bird's beak, casting a warm glow into the darkness.

"That must be the treasure Melody told us about. Ash, take it out."

"Huh?" Ash looked at him in surprise. "Aren't you going to do it?"

Ethan shook his head. "I have no interest in being the commander. You got here first, anyway. This part is yours."

Ash hesitated. He admired Ethan's strength and character so much that he had already decided that, if either of them was the commander in the legend, it had to be Ethan. But Ethan made no move toward the shrine.

Ash reached into the bird's beak. The orb was wedged in tightly, and he had to pull hard before it came free.

Only then did Ethan lean in for a better look. The orb was not red after all. It resembled a glass ball about the size of a fist. Something like a flame burned inside it, bathing the glass in red light.

Aside from the storm, the trip had been as easy as Melody had promised. The climb to the shrine had felt almost like sightseeing.

"Put the orb away," Ethan said. "It's time to head back."

They turned toward the stone staircase and found several figures standing on it. Team Rocket's trio had followed them and watched from hiding. Now that Ethan and Ash were leaving, they had finally shown themselves.

It was too dark for Ash to make out their faces at first. "Who are you?"

Jessie gave a cold smile. "Prepare for trouble!"

James folded his arms. "And make it double!"

"To protect the world from"

"All right, enough," Ethan cut in. "I've heard your motto too many times. We all know each other by now, so skip the introduction. Tell us what you want."

"Exactly!" Ash said with a frown. "The weather's awful, and we're busy. Don't waste our time."

Jessie, James, and Meowth fell silent. Having their routine interrupted left them feeling as if the words were stuck in their throats.

Jessie coughed to hide her embarrassment and pretended she did not care. "Fine. Before the weather gets any worse, hand over Pikachu and that thing you just picked up!"

Team Rocket's determination to catch Pikachu ran through the entire animated series. Ethan could almost admire their persistence, however selfish their motives. They had failed countless times and only grown more stubborn. Their great ambition was still bound to fail.

"Keep dreaming," Ash said. "I'm not giving you Pikachu or the treasure!"

"Oh?" Jessie grinned at the slip. "So that thing really is a treasure. Then don't blame us for taking it by force!"

She and James reached for their Poke Balls. Before they could send out their Pokemon, a deep scraping sound came from below, as though something heavy were dragging across the stone steps.

They looked back. A huge dark shape came rushing up the staircase.

"What is that?!"

Team Rocket barely had time to cry out. The shape leaped high and bore down on them. They threw themselves aside to avoid being crushed.

Bang!

It landed and slid toward Ethan and Ash. Now they could see it was a small sailboat.

A boat? How had a boat reached the top of the island?

It stopped in front of Ethan. Before anyone could work out what had happened, several heads emerged from its cabin.

"Ethan! Ash! We've come to get you!"

That was Misty's voice. In the beam of Ethan's flashlight, he saw Melody, Tracey, and Sabrina with her.

"What are you doing here?"

"We were worried about you," Misty said with a smile. "So we all came."

"Honestly!" Melody said. "In weather like this, you should have gone straight back to Shamouti Island. Why would you sail out in such heavy rain?"

Ash scratched his head and gave her an apologetic grin. His impulsive decision had worried them.

"We're here now, so there's no point arguing about it," Ethan said. He looked over the sailboat. "How did you get it up here, though?"

They were at the highest part of Fire Island, at least several hundred meters from the shore in a straight line. Sailing a boat all the way up the stone steps without water seemed impossible.

"That was Melody," Misty said admiringly. "She's amazing with a boat. She caught the strong wind in the sails and steered us right up the staircase!"

Melody lifted her chin at the praise. "For someone who grew up on Shamouti Island, a boat is practically a cradle. It wasn't difficult."

The tension between Misty and Melody had faded. Ethan wondered what had happened after he left the banquet hall, or on their way here. Melody had also changed into an islander's outfit, which gave her a wilder look.

Team Rocket noticed the growing crowd and prepared to slip away. Before they could move, a brilliantly glowing Pokemon swept across the black sky beside the island.

Everyone recognized it at once.

"Z-Zapdos?!"

"The God of Thunder!" Melody exclaimed. Her surprise gave way to a frown. This was Fire Island, the God of Fire's territory. Why would the God of Thunder come here?

At the same time, the three Great Balls at Ethan's left hip began to tremble. Their occupants seemed to sense another of their kind and could barely wait to come out and fight.

Chapter 208: The Airship

Lightning fell in every direction each time the God of Thunder beat its wings.

Ash and Team Rocket instinctively ducked beneath the sailboat. Ethan stood where he was. As a bolt flashed toward him, Gardevoir raised an arm and caught it on a psychic barrier. The lightning struck with a bang, scattering sparks.

The Great Balls at Ethan's waist shook harder. His three legendary birds seemed ready to burst out at any moment.

"Easy," he murmured, touching the balls. "It isn't trying to attack me. Besides, the main actor in this drama hasn't appeared yet. It's not your turn. Be patient a little longer."

The Great Balls let his birds sense the God of Thunder without letting it sense them. That spared Ethan an unexpected complication.

Still, this Zapdos was larger than his own, though it looked otherwise much the same. It was probably stronger too.

The God of Thunder circled above the group a few times, as if searching for something. Then it folded its wings and landed atop Fire Island's shrine. Electricity burst from its body and spread outward. A strange blue mist began to fill the crater.

Footsteps approached. Sabrina, Misty, Melody, and Tracey came to Ethan's side.

Sabrina watched the bird. "What is it doing?"

Ethan did not answer at once. He touched his hair. Like everyone else's, it had gone dry and was lifting of its own accord. Even the fine hairs on his skin stood up.

Static electricity?

"It might be changing the magnetic field here with its own power," Ethan guessed. "I think it wants to turn this island into its territory too."

Melody looked around in alarm. "But this is Fire Island. If the God of Thunder came here so openly, the God of Fire should have driven it off by now!"

"Something must have happened," Ethan said. He was already certain the collector had captured Moltres. Otherwise Zapdos would not have invaded its territory.

Pikachu, which had been behaving strangely since they reached the island, sent a thin thread of electricity toward the God of Thunder.

Jessie huddled nearby, trembling. "What's it doing? An attack that weak could never beat Zapdos!"

"Idiot," Meowth said. "It's talking, not attacking. Pikachu wants to know why Zapdos came to Moltres's island."

"You can talk with electric shocks? That's amazing!"

Ethan glanced at Meowth. "He's right. Electric-type Pokemon can communicate through electrical waves, much as Psychic-type Pokemon use telepathy."

"So that's how it works! What a handy ability!"

Pikachu's signal drew no response at first. Zapdos did not even look its way. Perhaps the questions finally irritated it: the bird beat its wings and sent a current to meet Pikachu's.

The others looked lost, so Meowth translated. "Zapdos says the owner of this island has disappeared. Since it's gone, Zapdos will rule here. It says the island belongs to it now!"

"What? The God of Fire is missing? How?" Melody cried.

Her shock was understandable. In the hundreds of years since the legends of the three sacred birds began, nothing like this had happened.

Then another disturbance caught everyone's attention. The currents from Zapdos and Pikachu suddenly flew upward, beyond either Pokemon's control.

The thick clouds parted. A vast airship was approaching Fire Island, its long, lightning rod-like spikes drawing in the electricity.

Whether it sensed an enemy or resented the affront to its power, Zapdos unleashed a much stronger attack at the vessel. Every bolt vanished into the spikes, no matter how powerful it was. As the airship closed in, it began actively pulling electricity from Zapdos.

In mere seconds, the bird lost an enormous amount of power. It screamed in anger and stopped attacking. Now it was panting, as though the strength had been drained from its body.

Instinct told it to flee. Zapdos launched itself into the air, but a hatch opened in the airship's underside. Several square metal rings flew out and encircled it.

"What... what is that thing?" Melody stared.

Only Ethan knew the answer.

Zapdos struggled, forcing the rings back for a moment with an electric shock. It swept over the group's heads. Some rings continued after it; others stopped around Ethan and his companions.

Sabrina's hair lifted without wind as psychic power stirred within her. She was ready to strike, but first she leaned toward Ethan. "Do we fight?"

"No." Ethan tried to piece together his scattered memories of the story. "Let's watch for now. If I rememif I'm right, we'll meet the person behind this very soon."

He recalled Gardevoir and Jolteon into their Poke Balls.

The rings around them released strange currents. In a moment, the whole group and the sailboat were trapped inside.

After their first stunned pause, Ash and the others struck at the rings, only to be shocked and left covered in burns and scrapes. Whatever this technology was, even Pikachu's attacks could not damage it.

Magnetism lifted the rings, carrying the captives and the sailboat slowly toward the airship.

"Aaah!" Misty and Melody cried out in fright.

As they entered the vessel, Ethan looked back at the God of Thunder. It had managed to fly only a short distance before the pursuing rings caught and bound it too. They were bringing Zapdos aboard.

Chapter 209: The Collector

The captives hung inside a sturdy iron cage in the airship's hold. They were packed together so tightly that none of them had room to move.

Ash rubbed his forehead where he had struck it and winced. "Ow... Is everyone all right?"

"I'm fine!" Tracey answered first. His face was pressed against the bars, badly squashed by the crowd. He was plainly trying to sound braver than he felt.

"Ethan?" Ash called.

"I'm fine too," Ethan said evenly. He was less battered than Tracey, but his position brought its own difficulty. Sabrina was crowded against him in front, Misty and Melody on either side, and Jessie behind him. He had ended up there by accident when the cage lurched. Whatever it looked like, he had not arranged it.

Sabrina shifted and glanced back. "What have you got in your pocket? Something's pressing against me."

"Wait, Sabrina. Please don't move for a moment," Ethan said, embarrassed. "There's barely room to breathe in here."

She gave him an exasperated look. "I still don't understand why you stopped me earlier. If you'd let me act, we wouldn't be in this cage."

"Don't worry." Ethan tried to ease his cramped posture. "They caught us for a reason. Whether they mean to let us go or keep us, we'll find out what they want. This gives us a chance to see what the people aboard this airship are planning."

Misty and Melody shifted too, leaving Ethan even less room. He cleared his throat and searched the hold for something else to focus on.

His eyes found the God of Thunder, bound nearby. Not far from it, Moltres was imprisoned in another cage.

"The God of Fire! It's here too!" Melody exclaimed. She leaned across Ethan for a closer look, making the cramped space more awkward still.

While everyone watched Moltres, Ethan noticed a low display case on the floor. Beneath its glass cover lay an ancient stone tablet.

"What's that?"

He nudged Melody with his elbow to draw her attention to it. In the crowded cage, the gesture came out awkwardly, but she was too intent on the tablet to notice.

"...The God of Fire, the God of Thunder, and the God of Ice must not be touched..." Melody read. "Those are the words of the prophecy! What are they doing here?"

"That isn't hard to explain," Ethan said, forcing his attention onto the tablet. "It's because of those words that the God of Fire and the God of Thunder were captured."

Melody understood at once, then turned to him in disbelief. "Fire, Thunder, Ice... Are the people on this airship after the God of the SeaLugia?"

Only then did she realize how closely she was leaning against Ethan. She gave a small cry, pulled away, and blushed.

Misty watched with a teasing smile. She looped her arm through Ethan's, lifted her chin at Melody, and stayed exactly where she was. Ethan pretended not to notice either girl's reaction.

"I suppose we'll have to hear their plans from them," he said.

As if on cue, the hold shuddered. A lift descended from the ceiling. Beneath a roof painted with angels, a man with a faintly sinister air reclined on a sofa, examining the prisoners as he came down.

"You're the first people to come aboard without an invitation." When the lift reached the floor, he stood and let his cold gaze pass over them. "What do you think? The God of Fire, Moltres, and the God of Thunder, Zapdos. My newest acquisitions."

Misty and the others were stunned.

"Hold on, mister!" Misty shouted. "If you want to catch Pokemon, why not battle them fairly? Isn't this an insult to them?"

"I'm no Pokemon Trainer," the man replied in a low voice. "I'm a collector. Capturing rare Pokemon and displaying them for my enjoymentthat is collecting. Please feel free to admire my collection. As a courtesy, don't touch anything."

He snapped his fingers. Gears turned, and the iron cage holding the group split in two. Everyone lost their footing and tumbled from the air.

Ethan grabbed Sabrina before they fell. He turned and held her above him, partly to protect her and partly so she would not have to reveal her psychic power. A barely perceptible breeze caught them, slowing their landing.

The collector gave them a glance and noticed nothing unusual.

Ethan had considered overpowering him immediately. But if he did, he might never see Lugia appear. He chose to wait and see how events unfolded.

A synthetic female voice sounded beside the collector.

"Articuno detected. Tracking... Beep. Target acquired."

"Excellent." The man smiled and returned to his sofa as the lift carried him toward the upper control room. "The God of Ice, the last of the legendary birds, has shown itself. My plan will soon be complete."

Chapter 210: Two Ways Forward

Ash and the others had seen the legendary Zapdos and Moltres, been hauled aboard an airship for no reason they understood, and met the man who claimed to own both birds. Too much had happened too quickly for them to think clearly.

Only after the collector vanished into the ceiling again did they begin to recover. He had barely acknowledged them. Perhaps he saw them as ordinary bystanders caught up in his plans, and that was why he had left them loose in the hold.

The room fell quiet. Ash looked around and scratched his head. "What do we do now? Should we free them?"

Melody was more concerned with the stone tablet. She stepped up to its case and studied it closely.

"It says exactly what our prophecy has said for generations. I don't know where he found it, but we have to stop him. If he breaks the balance between the three gods, the fury of the heavens and earth could destroy the world!"

"Destroy the world?" Misty frowned. She had heard the warning more than once now. "Aren't you overstating it? They're three legendary Pokemon. Even if someone captures them, how much could that affect the whole world?"

"Don't underestimate this!" Melody said. "The prophecy has been passed down on Shamouti Island for hundreds of years. If its conditions are met, disaster will follow!"

Misty shook her head, unconvinced. She trusted Ethan's judgment more than an old tale.

"What do you think, Ethan?" she asked.

Instead of answering, Ethan asked, "Have any of you heard of the butterfly effect?"

"The butterfly effect? What's that?"

Everyone turned toward him. Ethan gathered his thoughts.

"A small change in the starting conditions of a complex system can cause a much larger chain of effects over time. That's the butterfly effect."

Their blank faces told him he needed an example. "Imagine a Butterfree in Kanto flapping its wings a few times. Weeks later, those small changes might contribute to a huge storm in Johto."

"Really?" Ash's eyes widened. "A Butterfree could make a hurricane just by flapping its wings?"

"It sounds far-fetched, but the idea has a scientific basis." Ethan turned to Melody. "Shamouti Island lies near the center of the Orange Islands. Is there a major ocean current nearby?"

"Yes. A current runs through the deep water near Shamouti. It splits into three around the islands of the gods, then flows toward different seas."

"That's what I suspected," Ethan said. "The God of Thunder, the God of Fire, and the God of Ice could affect their surroundings the way that Butterfree affects the air. If they fought, storms and lightning from the battle could spread through the region and affect other places within hours or weeks. Shamouti would be hit first. If the fighting devastated the island people live on, wouldn't that feel almost like the end of the world to them?"

Shamouti Island was small. There was little room to escape a tsunami or another disaster. A single catastrophe could kill its inhabitants.

"I... I see," Ash said. He had not followed every part of the explanation, but he understood what mattered. He clenched a fist. "Then we need to free these two and let them return to their islands. That should keep them from fighting, right?"

"Exactly!" Melody said. "We have to restore things to how they were. That's how we'll avoid a disaster."

They had agreed, but Ethan rubbed his chin and said nothing at first. There was another way to look at it. If the collector captured all three birds, none would remain on its island to fight the others. In that sense, captivity could remove the immediate danger to people for good.

These were the legendary Pokemon of Shamouti and the wider Orange Islands, though. The choice belonged to the people who lived here. After a moment's hesitation, Ethan laid out both possibilities for Melody.

The others fell silent. They had not thought of his second approach, and all eyes turned to the festival maiden. Melody's brow tightened as she weighed the choice.

At last she set her jaw. "No. I can't stand by while the God of Thunder and the God of Fire fall into a villain's hands. The three gods have lived in peace for hundreds of years. I believe they can continue to do so!"

"Good choice," Ethan said. "I'd make the same one. Let's get these two out of their cages."

Ash was the first to rush toward Moltres. He grabbed the bars, intending to bend them apart by force, but a burst of energy threw him backward the instant he touched them.

"Idiot," Misty sighed, covering her face. "If those cages can hold legendary Pokemon, they must have powerful defenses. You weren't going to break one with your hands."

Ash blushed. "I knew that! I was testing how strong it was. Pikachu, use Thunderbolt!"

Pikachu fired a fierce electric attack into the cage. The glare faded. The bars were untouched.

"It's that strong?" Ash stared.

The airship shuddered. Alarmed, the group hurried to a window and looked out. After a short flight, the vessel had reached another island and was battling a large bird Pokemon. Shells shot from its cannons while the bird dodged and answered with blasts of ice.

Melody recognized it at once. "Oh no! The God of Ice has appeared too!"

Chapter 211: Rescue and Fall

The God of Ice was losing its fight outside. Melody bit her lip. "We can't waste any more time! We have to free the God of Fire and the God of Thunder before the collector catches the last one!"

The others knew she was right. They left the window and hurried back to the cages.

Misty and Tracey sent out their Pokemon to help Pikachu attack the bars around Moltres. Even Jessie and James understood what was at stake for once. Rather than interfere, they sent out Weezing and Arbok to help free the two legendary birds.

Bang! Bang! Boom!

Move after move struck the cage, raising thick smoke. When it cleared, the bars were untouched. A transparent shield protected them from every attack.

"What is that thing made of?" Tracey demanded. "How can it be so strong?"

"Maybe that's why the collector felt safe leaving us here," Ethan said. "He must trust his defenses completely."

Ash refused to give up. "I don't care what it's made of. If we keep attacking, even a shield this tough has to break eventually!"

He released Charizard, Squirtle, and Bulbasaur to add their attacks to the barrage.

That was Ash's way: meet a difficult problem head-on. Ethan remembered a member of Hoenn's Elite Four once describing Ash's tactics as full of force but short on calculation. Constant attacks might work, but they would take time and exhaust their Pokemon.

There had to be another way. Ethan studied the cage and the space around it. His gaze settled on the point where it met the floor.

"What are you thinking?" Misty asked.

"I don't know how the cage works," Ethan said, directing Haunter toward the floor, "but it needs power to hold a Pokemon inside and repel attacks from outside. Something must be feeding energy into it."

Haunter fired a Shadow Ball. It blew a large hole through the airship's floor. Even the hard alloy plating could not withstand the hit.

Fragments scattered. When the dust settled, Ethan peered into the opening. Wires of many colors lay exposed. He crouched and followed them toward the cage until he spotted a thick black conduit running beneath the floor and up to its base. A hum came from inside it, as though energy were rushing through.

"There it is! Haunter, break that conduit!"

"Got it!"

Haunter launched another Shadow Ball. The moment it struck the conduit and the energy inside, a violent explosion went off.

Bang!

Electricity crackled and flames burst upward. A wave of heat drove Ethan several steps back. He barely noticed; his eyes were on Moltres. Its cage flickered, lost power, and fell apart onto the floor.

"You did it, Ethan!" Misty cried.

Freed, Moltres spread its wings and gave a piercing call. It fired a huge burst of flame at the cage holding Zapdos. When the fire cleared, the cage that had resisted all their Pokemon's attacks was broken.

The group stared. Moltres's strength had done with one attack what their combined efforts could not.

They thought it had released Zapdos to help another captive. Instead, the moment both birds were free, they attacked each other. Fire and lightning met beneath the ceiling. Everyone ducked to keep out of the blast.

"They heard what the collector said," Melody cried. "Why are they fighting each other now, of all times?"

Ethan gave a rueful smile. "You call them gods, but they're Pokemon. They don't necessarily think about consequences far in the future. To them, a rivalry centuries old matters more than changes to the environment."

"But their islands could be destroyed too!" Melody said, dismayed.

Before Ethan could answer, Tracey spoke as a Pokemon Watcher. "People depend on their homes. Bird Pokemon like Moltres can move elsewhere if their nests are destroyed. And, as Zapdos showed us, they may even be able to change the conditions of a new habitat."

"Then... can we no longer stop them?" The battle was growing fiercer. Melody wondered whether she had made the wrong choice.

A stray attack smashed through the airship's wall. Moltres and Zapdos spread their wings and flew out through the opening.

The group went back to the window. Outside, the birds chased and attacked each other. They had not forgotten what the airship had done to them, though. They wheeled around it, striking it again and again.

Explosions shook the vessel. Fire and smoke spread through its interior. After a dozen seconds, something critical seemed to take a hit. The airship tilted sharply, and warning lights flashed throughout the hold.

"It's going to crash!" Misty shouted.

Before anyone could get away, the floor slid beneath their feet and they fell backward. The whole airship was descending at an angle toward Lightning Island below.

"Sabrina!" Ethan called.

She understood at once. Her psychic power surrounded everyone, shielding them as the airship fell.

Chapter 212: An Ethereal Voice

Bang...

The airship struck Lightning Island and slid dozens of meters farther before coming to a halt near the volcano's crater.

Even with Sabrina's psychic power shielding them, the violent impact left everyone dizzy. Ethan helped her up, rubbed his temples, and looked around.

"Is everyone all right?"

"I'm fine!" Misty answered first.

"Me too!" Melody said.

Ash, Tracey, and Team Rocket appeared unharmed as well. Ethan pointed toward the hole the birds had made in the hull.

"After a fall like that, I don't know if this thing might explode. Let's get out of here."

Team Rocket needed no second warning. Jessie, James, and Meowth bolted for the opening before anyone else moved. Jessie and James even glanced back to pull faces at the others as they ran. Their Pokemon might be weaker, but when it came to fleeing, they considered themselves experts.

Ethan found their antics ridiculous. He said nothing and followed with Ash and the others.

Only after escaping through the torn hull did they grasp the airship's size. Its body, studded with propellers and wind-turbine blades, was more than five hundred meters across. Beside it, the people looked like ants. An explosion from something so vast could destroy them all.

They ran along the length of the hull for several minutes before they finally reached solid ground. Everyone was breathless except Sabrina, who had flown with her psychic power and looked as composed as ever.

A cold wind carrying snowflakes swept over them. They shivered. Melody looked around in disbelief.

Lightning Island had been green. Now it was a frozen world. Hard ice covered the ground, the trees, and the rocks. Fire Island and Ice Island, the surrounding sea, and even distant Shamouti Island appeared sealed in it. White stretched as far as they could see, like a warning that the end of the world had begun.

"How did it become like this?" Melody murmured.

"The God of Ice must have noticed the other two were gone," Ethan said. "Just as Zapdos tried to claim Fire Island, Articuno seems to be using its power to turn all four islands, Shamouti included, into its territory."

His breath fogged in the cold. The others were still staring, so he urged them on. "Don't stop. It isn't safe here. Keep moving."

As soon as he said it, the airship began sliding again. Equipment broke loose from its upper surface and fell around them.

Boom!

Debris smashed into the ice, spraying shards into the air. They covered their heads and ran. A huge blade fell onto a nearby shrine and brought the stone structure down with a crash. A glowing yellow orb bounced from the ruins and rolled to Ash's feet.

"The treasure from Lightning Island!" Melody recognized it at once.

It resembled the orb from Fire Island, but golden light flowed inside it in place of flame.

Ash reached down to pick it up. Lightning flashed past him, shattered the ice at his feet, and knocked the orb away.

After wrecking the airship, Moltres and Zapdos had gone back to fighting each other. Articuno had appeared from somewhere and joined them. The three legendary birds attacked without yielding, their moves tearing across the island.

The orb rolled farther away. Ash dived after it and caught it in both hands. They now had the second treasure for the festival.

"Look over there!" Misty pointed into the distance.

The sailboat they had arrived in had fallen from the airship into a lake on the island. After everything it had endured, it still looked intact.

"Thank goodness!"

Team Rocket scrambled into its cabin as if they had found a lifeline. Ethan and the others saw that it was their best chance of leaving Lightning Island and climbed aboard too.

A huge ball of fire struck the lake, splashing water over them. Misty wiped her face and called before she had even settled into a seat. "Melody, get us moving!"

"I know!" Melody snapped. Her expression was serious, and her movements were swift and practiced. The engine started, and she turned the sailboat toward the sea.

At the lake's outlet, Articuno swept past and sealed their path with Ice Beam.

"We're going to hit it!" Melody cried.

Ethan was about to tell Haunter to break the ice when Zapdos flew overhead. Brilliant lightning burst from its body, smashing the barrier and tearing away much of the outlet itself.

The lake poured toward the sea like water through a broken dam, carrying chunks of ice with it. The current seized the sailboat, hurled it out of Lightning Island, and flung it into the air.

"Aaah!"

Misty and Melody screamed. Ethan's heart leaped into his throat as the boat began to fall. Then a thick waterspout broke through the ice on the sea below, rose dozens of meters, and caught the boat squarely.

Everyone stared. Why had a waterspout appeared at that moment, and why had it saved them?

It carried the sailboat toward Shamouti Island. Ethan was the first to react. He hurried to the rail and searched the water below, but he could see nothing beneath the thick ice.

Then they heard a low, ethereal call.

Wooo...

It sounded a little like a whale, only longer and more haunting. The loneliness in the sound made Melody's eyes widen.

"That voice... Has Lugia appeared?"

Chapter 213: The Talking Slowking

"Lugia?!"

Ash and the others hurried to the side of the sailboat and searched the sea. They could see nothing.

Ash looked back at Melody. "Are you sure it was Lugia? Could you have heard something else?"

"It has to be," Melody said. "Lugia is the God of the Sea. The prophecy says it will appear when the world faces destruction and stop the fighting. Besides, what else in these waters could wield that kind of power?"

"I suppose so..."

The waterspout carried the sailboat several kilometers at remarkable speed, toward the coast on Shamouti Island's southern side. The God of Ice had turned the area into a white, frozen landscape. A journey that should have taken more than ten minutes took only three.

At the shore, the waterspout swung the boat up to an altar on the island's cliff.

Bang!

Snow flew as the sailboat came down near the cliff's edge, pitching everyone off their feet once more. Ethan rubbed his aching back. He had lost count of how often he had fallen today; after so much battering, even his bones felt sore.

They had barely begun to feel safe when the sailboat tipped backward. Everyone scrambled up and jumped clear just before it fell over the cliff.

Boom!

The boat that had carried them through so much finally broke apart below.

"That was close!" Ash lay on the snow and let out a long breath. When he stood and turned toward the altar, he found himself face to face with a pink Pokemon wearing a shell on its head.

"Huh? What's that?"

Ash took out his Pokedex and scanned the Pokemon he did not recognize.

"Slowking, the Royal Pokemon. Water and Psychic type. It evolves from a Slowpoke holding a King's Rock. Slowking is exceptionally wise and perceptive, and stays calm no matter what happens. It spends each day puzzling over the mysteries of the world, but if the Shellder on its head is removed, it forgets everything it has learned."

"Slowking?"

It was the first one Ash and his friends had seen. Slowpoke and Slowbro could be found around many bays, but Slowking were rare, whether in the wild or in a Trainer's care.

The Pokemon stood with its hands behind its back, like a kindly grandfather, and regarded them. No one, Ethan included, had noticed it arrive. It was as though it had been standing at the center of the altar all along, silently watching. Wild Pokemon usually kept their distance from people to avoid being caught; this one seemed entirely at ease.

Before anyone spoke, Slowking raised a hand and pointed toward a stone tablet in the altar.

"Place the treasures there."

"It talked!" Tracey pointed at it. "That Slowking can speak human language?"

They had encountered talking Pokemon beforeTeam Rocket's Meowth and Ethan's Haunter, for example. They were still rare enough that no one had expected to find another one here.

Melody thought for a moment. "Carol once told me there was an unusual Slowking on Shamouti Island, but I'd never seen it. So this is where it's been living."

Ash was still staring. Slowking repeated, "Place the treasures there."

"Oh. Right." Ash went to the tablet and set the orbs from Fire Island and Lightning Island into a recess.

Slowking stepped closer. Seeing only two, it shook its head. "Two? That won't do. The ceremony cannot be completed without all three treasures."

While they spoke, Ethan studied the area. The altar stood on the opposite side of Shamouti Island from the town, facing the three smaller islands. Built entirely of rock, with hibiscus growing nearby, it looked ancient.

He turned to say something to Sabrina and found her watching Slowking with interest.

"Thinking of catching it?" he asked.

She nodded. "It's a rare Psychic-type Pokemon."

Ethan understood why she wanted it. As a Gym Leader traveling for so long, she could use another Pokemon on her team. But he could not remember exactly what part this Slowking played in the story, so he leaned close and whispered, "The timing's wrong. Let's leave it alone for now. When this is over, you can try to catch it."

Slowking turned toward them as soon as he finished speaking. It looked into their eyes and felt a sudden, inexplicable unease about the pair.

Ethan cleared his throat. To keep Slowking from growing suspicious and fleeing, he led Sabrina a little farther away.

Only then did he notice how quiet it was. He looked around. Team Rocket had vanished. They must have used the confusion after landing to slip away.

Those three never miss a chance.

Over Lightning Island, the God of Thunder, the God of Fire, and the God of Ice continued to fight. Somehow their battle had drifted closer to Shamouti Island.

Everyone frowned. If the birds came any nearer, their attacks would reach the town. The people living there would be in danger, and those at the altar would be caught in the fighting first.

Just then, the waterspout appeared again. It burst through the ice and rose in the birds' path, forcing all three back.

The waterspout broke apart into a spray of droplets. High above them, a strange white Pokemon emerged.

Chapter 214: Lugia

"It's Lugia!"

Melody called out its name, caught between relief and dread. Lugia had finally appeared in their hour of need and saved them. But the battle had already gone so far. Would events unfold as the prophecy warned?

Ash stared at the Pokemon he had never seen before. "So that's Lugia... the God of the Sea?"

Lugia was one of the rarest legendary Pokemon in the world. It normally slept at the bottom of deep ocean trenches. People said a single beat of its wings could raise a storm that lasted forty days. Its power was so great that it could also calm a raging sea. Whenever a violent storm arose, people thought of Lugia.

Whatever happened next, the group had seen the legendary Pokemon with their own eyes. That alone made the journey unforgettable.

Above them, the Gods of Ice, Fire, and Thunder stopped fighting each other for the moment. They fixed their attention on the newcomer.

Lugia gave another high, haunting call. It seemed to be trying to end the battle, but the three birds neither answered nor showed any sign of stopping.

For a moment, all four Pokemon were still. Their power seemed to clash in the air before any of them moved. It crackled like an invisible struggle.

Tracey swallowed as he watched from the altar.

After two or three tense seconds, the impatient God of Thunder attacked first. Zapdos beat its wings and sent lightning at Lugia. The God of Ice and the God of Fire followed at once, striking together.

Lugia twisted and climbed to avoid the lightning, fire, and ice. The powerful moves passed close by, but it managed to answer with attacks of its own.

Alone against three opponents, it was still at a severe disadvantage. Lugia was stronger than any one of the birds, yet together they pressed it hard.

"This is troubling," Slowking said after watching for a while. "The God of the Sea appears only when the world faces destruction. But it seems unable to stop that destruction."

Ethan narrowed his eyes at the battle and thought quickly. At last he made a decision.

"We can't wait any longer. Ash, let's divide the work. Go to Ice Island and get the last treasure as fast as you can. If the legend is true and Melody's flute can calm them, we'll need it to stop this."

"I understand." Ash nodded, then noticed Ethan was not moving. "What about you? Aren't you coming?"

"No." Ethan took three Great Balls from his waist. "I'll stay and hold them off. That will take some pressure off Lugia and give you time."

"Hold them off?" Melody shook her head. "They're too powerful. Ordinary Pokemon can't join a battle like this!"

"Ordinary Pokemon..." Ethan gave a quiet laugh but did not argue. He looked straight at Ash. "Go. Your job matters. Bring that treasure back safely."

Ash understood how serious this was. "Leave it to me." He turned his cap backward and hurried away with Pikachu.

In Ethan's hand, the three Great Balls stirred. His Pokemon had been eager to emerge for some time. There were no outsiders here he needed to hide them from, and he saw no reason to make them wait longer.

Now's my chance to show offno, to show what I can do.

He threw the Great Balls high. "Come out, all of you!"

Three flashes of blue light appeared in the sky. To everyone's astonishment except Sabrina's, three more legendary birds emerged above them.

"Moltres? Zapdos? Articuno?" Misty cried.

"I can't believe it! Another set of the three legendary birds?" Tracey stared upward.

The others could hardly believe what they were seeing. During the giant Tentacruel incident, Misty had suspected that the Zapdos which appeared might have something to do with Ethan. She had never imagined he had caught Moltres and Articuno as well.

Ash had not gone far. Hearing the commotion, he looked back and stopped in his tracks. Ethan had captured all three legendary birds? When had that happened? The distance between them had grown greater without Ash even realizing it.

Melody was more stunned than anyone. To the people of Shamouti Island, the Gods of Thunder, Fire, and Ice were sacred and untouchable. Ethan owned others of their kind. She stared at him, unable to understand.

Who is he?

Ethan knew nothing of their thoughts. Lugia was taking hit after hit. He swept an arm toward the battle.

"Moltres, Articuno, Zapdosgo! Let's shake things up!"

The three birds cried out, circled once, and flew straight toward their counterparts.

Melody finally recovered enough to grab Ethan's sleeve. "Wait! If the fighting gets worse, won't it do even more damage to the environment in other regions? That's what you said!"

"I know," Ethan said with a rueful smile. "But should we stand by because we're afraid of damaging it? I'd rather end this quickly than let it drag on. Nature can recover in remarkable ways. We have to respect its power, and trust its ability to heal."

Chapter 215: Seven Legendary Pokemon Clash

The four legendary Pokemon already fighting noticed the three birds entering the sky above them.

The island's three gods stopped first. They watched their counterparts warily, unable to tell where the newcomers had come from or what they wanted. Lugia used the pause to catch its breath.

Ethan's birds offered no greeting. As soon as they reached the others, they attacked.

The three island birds were furious. Had these strangers come to seize their territory or simply pick a fight? Either way, they were fiercely territorial. They left Lugia alone and met Ethan's birds head-on.

Fire faced fire, ice met ice, and lightning sought out lightning. Six bird Pokemon chased one another across the sky, their moves bursting into brilliant light whenever they met.

The gods of the Orange Islands were formidable. The collector had captured them with surprising ease, but their attacks were devastating. After only a few exchanges, Ethan's Moltres was holding its own while his Articuno and Zapdos were slowly being pushed back.

Ethan was neither surprised nor alarmed. This was no formal match. His Pokemon could work together, draw enemies away, and change tactics whenever they needed to.

He surveyed the battlefield and settled on the God of Ice. In a group fight, it made sense to concentrate on the opponent who offered the best opening.

That did not mean the island's Articuno was weak. All three gods were immensely powerful. But Ethan's Moltres was the strongest of his birds, and its Fire-type attacks gave it an advantage against an Ice-type opponent. With help from the others, they might defeat the God of Ice quickly.

Ethan released Gardevoir. The battle was too far away for his shouted commands to reach the birds, so he needed her to carry his thoughts directly to them.

Gardevoir rested one hand lightly on his arm and fixed her gaze on Ethan's three birds.

Moltres, leave the God of Fire and attack the God of Ice. Zapdos, help Moltres. Your Electric-type moves will work well against its Flying type. Articuno, hold off the other two. Don't let either of them interfere until we've beaten the God of Ice.

The birds changed targets, each taking up its assigned role.

"Connect me to Lugia too," Ethan told Gardevoir.

She nodded and turned her attention to Lugia.

Lugia, I'm the Trainer of those three birds. I need your help. Please work with my Articuno to keep the Gods of Fire and Thunder away for now.

A neutral voice answered inside Ethan's mind. Lugia was speaking through telepathy of its own.

I see. Are you the commander?

No. I'm a Trainer who happened to come to this island. The commander is probably my friend. He's gone to Ice Island for the last treasure. This isn't the time to argue over titles, though. Even an ordinary person can do something in a crisis.

Lugia was silent for a moment. You're right. I'll help hold back the Gods of Thunder and Fire and buy time for the commander's return.

With Ethan's birds in the fight, Lugia no longer faced all three opponents alone. It could turn more of its strength toward attacking.

Energy gathered in its mouth. A huge Hydro Pump surged toward the God of Fire.

Bang!

The attack was too fierce to meet directly. The island's Moltres tried to evade, but Lugia was close enough that the water clipped one side of its body. It cried out, shuddered with pain, and climbed higher.

One strike had wounded the God of Fire. Water's advantage over Fire helped, but Lugia's power clearly surpassed that of any one bird on the field.

Elsewhere, Ethan's Moltres and Zapdos had caught the God of Ice between them. Moltres fired Flamethrower again and again while Zapdos struck with repeated electric bolts. The island's Articuno had to defend in two directions at once.

It fought fiercely, but it could not watch both attackers every instant. Flame or lightning found its mark whenever its attention shifted, and its strength began to flag.

The God of Thunder tried to go to its aid. Ethan's Articuno intercepted it halfway.

Ethan glanced across the sky and nodded. If they could defeat the God of Ice first, they could take control of the battle.

He found a moment to check on Ash. Ash had taken a plank from the wrecked sailboat and was riding it like a sled toward Ice Island, pulled by Charizard. He kept having to steer around the stray attacks from the battle, so he was moving more slowly than Ethan had hoped.

Still, the fight was turning in their favor. Ethan felt less desperate for Ash to return with the last treasure immediately.

Then dozens of iron rings flew from the frozen crater on Lightning Island. They closed around Lugia, crackling with electricity, and bound it in midair within seconds.

The collector!

Ethan had not forgotten him through carelessness. Everyone had been focused on escaping the airship before it exploded, and they had assumed the collector might die in the crash. Somehow he had survived. Seeing Lugia appear, he had made his move.

Lugia screamed and struggled, but it could not break free of the rings.

Ethan's brow tightened. With Lugia unable to move, the battle could reverse in an instant. He already knew how tough the rings were. Sending his birds to destroy them would mean surrendering their advantage over the God of Ice, and the three island birds would hardly stand by while they tried.

Possibilities flashed through his mind. There was no time to hesitate.

Zapdos! Articuno! Moltres! We have to defeat at least one of them before the three gods regroup. It's time to stop holding back. Use that big move!

Chapter 216: A Vision of the End

Ethan's three birds abandoned their opponents and climbed together into the clouds.

The island's three gods stared after them, baffled that their counterparts had all broken away at once. They did not give chase. Instead, they watched the sky warily.

Dark clouds gathered and thickened at a visible pace. Within moments they had covered the whole skynot merely over one island, but across more than two hundred kilometers of surrounding sea.

The fighting had already darkened the day. Now no sunlight reached them at all. A breeze stirred by the shifting air pressure lifted everyone's hair.

Misty pulled her clothes tighter around herself. The oppressive sky made it hard to breathe. "Ethan, what exactly are you doing?"

Melody and Tracey looked at him too.

Ethan patted Misty's shoulder. "I'm trying a combination move with a lot of power. The birds have practiced it a few times, but they're still working on it. They've only succeeded once. I wouldn't use it lightly if this weren't urgent, so you'd better protect yourselves."

That was hardly reassuring. Misty and the others sent out Pokemon to stand guard. Seeing that Melody had none, Misty brought out Starmie to protect her.

Snowflakes began to fall. A blizzard was coming. Thunder rumbled overhead without striking; terrible amounts of electricity seemed to be building inside the clouds.

A blizzard and Thunder together? Misty had seen powerful Pokemon moves alter the weather, but never over an area so vast.

Snow settled on the three island birds. Their body heat melted it into water that soaked their feathers.

Crack! Crack!

Lightning tore across the sky as the gathered power reached its peak. Instead of one bolt, more than a dozen smaller streaks fell at once toward all four legendary Pokemon on the battlefield.

"Here it comes," Ethan said quietly.

The three birds tried to evade, but they were exposed in the air, their feathers wet. The lightning found them as if guided to its targets.

The Gods of Ice and Fire cried out. The attack was divided among many bolts, so it was less devastating than it looked, but it still sent them falling toward the sea. The God of Thunder resisted the Electric-type strike and remained in better shape.

The lightning had barely faded when a fierce blizzard engulfed them. This time the God of Ice resisted the attack, while the Gods of Fire and Thunder cried out in pain.

They were legendary Pokemon with immense endurance. Though struck twice, all three held on. They were wounded, panting, and disheveled, seemingly near their limits.

"Too bad," Misty said with a sigh. She had hoped the two attacks would defeat them outright.

"Don't worry. There's one more strike."

Misty looked up. A huge ball of fire, almost like the sun, broke through the cloud cover and dropped straight toward the battlefield.

"What is that?" Melody asked. "A meteor?"

"No. It's Moltres using Burn Up. It started high above us, so the descent makes it look like a meteor."

By then Ethan's Moltres was only dozens of meters from the ice below. The injured island birds sensed the threat and tried to leave. They had barely begun to beat their wings when Moltres struck the God of Thunder.

Boom!

Flames exploded outward. Heat cracked and melted the ice, and a storm of steam, water droplets, and wind burst from the center.

The gale nearly knocked Ethan and the others down. Blown ice chips stung their faces and forced their eyes shut. Their Pokemon blocked the larger chunks and debris before anyone was hurt.

The wind reached far across the ice. When it finally eased, they looked toward the battlefield.

A gap several hundred meters wide had burned through the frozen sea. The God of Thunder floated on the water, unconscious and unable to fight. Ethan's Moltres hovered nearby, breathing hard.

Burn Up did not usually exhaust it so badly. The dive from such a height had increased its speed, but the impact had taken a toll on Moltres too.

Ethan had originally imagined this three-bird combination for an opponent as formidable as Mewtwo. If every part hit, it might even defeat a Pokemon as strong as Lugia in one blow. Using it to knock out the God of Thunder felt wasteful, but he had needed to weaken the island birds quickly.

The clouds broke apart, and the wind calmed. The Gods of Ice and Fire had been thrown clear by the blast and were struggling to rise. They had been farther from the center, and Moltres had aimed Burn Up at Zapdos, so they had taken little damage from the final strike.

Misty scanned the sky. "Where's Lugia?"

Ethan looked around. Lugia had vanished. "Maybe it went into the sea. The lightning struck everywhere, so it may have broken the rings holding Lugia. If so, it could have escaped then."

"I hope so." Melody clasped her hands at her chest. "I hope it's safe."

Across Shamouti Island, townspeople stood at their windows, watching the sky with grave faces. The distance hid the battle itself, but thunder, blizzard, and the falling fireball had terrified them.

Was this what the end of the world looked like?

Chapter 217: Unexpected Visitors

Lugia rose from the sea. Its body bore serious electrical burns, but it still seemed strong enough to fight.

It shook water from its wings, opened its mouth, and fired a hot orange beam straight into the airship wreckage at Ice Island's crater.

Boom!

The wreckage exploded and burst into flames. Lugia had not forgotten the rings that had trapped it. This was its answer to the collector.

Ethan nodded. With the collector dealt with and the God of Thunder unable to fight, the situation was turning in their favor again.

Thrum, thrum, thrum...

Just as Ethan prepared to press the advantage, he heard a helicopter's rotors. He looked up. A helicopter wobbled toward Shamouti Island, its flight plainly unstable. While the group watched, it dropped onto a hill near the altar.

Bang!

It crashed close enough for Ethan to feel the impact. Smoke rose from the hillside.

"What's going on?" Misty asked. "Who would fly a helicopter here in weather like this?"

"Maybe it's something urgent," Ethan said. He could not leave the battle now. He turned to Sabrina. "Someone may be hurt. Would you check on them?"

"I will." She set off. Misty and Tracey knew they could do little from the altar, so they followed her.

Only Melody remained beside Ethan. The unexpected helicopter made him worry that events might change in a way he had not foreseen. He hurried his attack.

Moltres, finish the God of Ice. Zapdos and Articuno, help Lugia against the God of Fire!

It was four against two now. Ethan had a clear advantage, though defeating the remaining birds would still take work.

Time passed as the battle continued. Then Ash called from the ice. Charizard had brought him back from Ice Island in a little over ten minutes. Without the three gods blocking his way, he seemed to have collected the treasure smoothly.

Ash jumped from Charizard before it landed and came down beside Ethan.

"I'm back! I got the last treasure." He looked around. "Where are Misty and the others?"

"A helicopter crashed nearby. They went to help the people aboard. You"

Ethan broke off. Sabrina's psychic power had touched his mind with a warning. He sent a command through Gardevoir and called his three birds back to the altar.

Ash watched them arrive with mixed feelings and a hint of envy. "Ethan, when did you catch them?"

"I'll tell you another time." Ethan put a finger to his lips, then turned to Melody. "Both of you, please keep this to yourselves."

He had been willing to show his birds to Ash, Misty, and their companions. He did not want to reveal them to strangers without reason. More people would learn eventually, and the news might bring him trouble rather than disaster, but caution could keep criminals from seeking him out.

Ash and Melody understood that he trusted them with something private. They nodded without hesitation. "We won't tell anyone."

Ethan returned Moltres, Zapdos, and Articuno to their Great Balls. Victory had been close, and he regretted losing the chance to defeat the other two birds himself. But Ash had returned with the treasure. He could take over from here.

Lugia was still holding its own against the two island birds. Taken together, the three gods no longer seemed likely to overwhelm them.

Footsteps sounded behind Ethan. Sabrina, Misty, and Tracey were returning with several people. Among them were Ash's mother, Delia, Professor Oak, and a woman in a white lab coat. Two pilots and several television crew members carrying cameras came along as well.

Ash stared. "Mom? Professor Oak? Professor Ivy? What are you doing here?"

Professor Oak shook his head. "Kanto has been experiencing extreme weather. We investigated and traced it to this area, so I asked Professor Ivy to come with me and look into it."

Ethan understood why the professors had come. He was less sure why Ash's mother had joined them. Ash seemed to be wondering the same thing.

"Mom, why did you come? I heard the helicopter crashed. Are you hurt?"

"I'm all right." Delia looked at him with concern. "Professor Oak said you might be nearby. I wanted to check on you."

"It's strange," Professor Oak said. "The flight was normal until we reached Shamouti Island. Then we were suddenly caught in a fierce blizzard. That's why the helicopter failed."

Misty and the others looked toward Ethan.

He gave nothing away and redirected their attention. "It was probably caused by the three legendary birds and Lugia."

"The legendary birds? Lugia?"

Professor Oak and the others looked into the distance and saw the legendary Pokemon. The television crew immediately raised their cameras and began filming.

"Remarkable," Oak said. "So they're behind the weather disturbances."

Ash was about to speak, but Ethan stopped him. "We can catch up later. Put the treasure on the altar. Stopping the birds is what matters now."

"Oh! Right." Ash slapped his forehead. "I nearly forgot the most important thing!"

Chapter 218: The Festival Song

Ash hurried to the altar and took out the last orb, which flashed with electricity inside. He fitted it into the remaining recess of the stone tablet.

The three orbs sat in a row. Everyone watched. Several seconds passed without anything unusual happening.

"Huh? Why isn't it working?" Ash looked at Melody. "Are they just decorations? Do they really have any power to stop the fighting?"

Melody remained calm. "Wait. You'll see."

As the others wondered what she meant, brilliant light burst from the three orbs. It grew brighter, then merged into a soft green glow. A curtain of light spread over the entire altar.

A strong wind blew outward from the tablet, clearing away the nearby snow. Flowers lay everywhere, filling the air with fragrance. The place seemed to have returned to how it had been before the disaster.

Glowing green liquid flowed from beneath the tablet. It filled a circular channel at its base and crept up the stone pillars beside the altar. Only now could everyone see the strange symbols carved densely into the paving stones below.

The color made them step back. Could something that green be poisonous?

Melody walked to the center of the altar and took her unusual flute from inside her clothes. She lifted it to her lips, closed her eyes, and moved her fingers over it. The first note sounded.

Wooo...

A graceful melody carried across the altar. When its sound reached the pillars, they began to pulse with light in time to the music. They seemed to amplify the tune and send it far across the islands.

Ethan felt its calming effect at once. The tension he had carried through the battle eased from his body.

The fighting Pokemon heard it too. Their interest in battling faded, and they came down to listen. Even the God of Thunder, unconscious on the water, opened its eyes and began to stir.

Wooo, wooo...

As Melody played, green liquid sprayed up like a fountain and flowed from the shrine into the sea. It spread quickly in every direction. Ice and snow melted wherever it reached, as visibly as grease clearing when detergent touched dishwater.

The music soothed the anger of the three island birds. They circled a few times, then turned together and flew back toward their respective islands. By now they were exhausted. Even without the flute, they could hardly have kept fighting much longer.

Lugia raised a huge waterspout from the sea. It swept away the last dark clouds, revealing the sun after what felt like a long absence.

For all the danger and drama, the crisis had ended rather abruptly. Ethan took out his phone and photographed the rare sight, as he often did.

Lugia descended above the altar and spoke into their minds.

To the two commanders, and to the one who brought peace: thank you for all you have done. Thank you for protecting this world.

The two commanders were Ethan and Ash. They had shared the work: Ash sought the treasures while Ethan held off the birds. Melody was the one who brought peace. Her festival tune, Lugia's Song, had finally ended the battle.

Melody lowered her flute. Relieved that the fighting had stopped, she clasped her hands before her and looked up at Lugia.

"We should be thanking you, God of the Sea. Thank you for protecting Shamouti Island for so long and keeping the three birds in balance."

The television crew had been stunned by the legendary Pokemon. Only now did they pay attention to the people at the altar. One of them recognized the handsome young man standing there: Ethan Vale, the new Indigo League Champion from Kanto.

What was he doing here? Was he involved in this crisis?

The crew swung their cameras toward him. The Champion, Lugia, the three sacred birds, an extraordinary battle, and extreme weather across the regionsput together, it would be a sensational story.

Before they could record Ethan, Professor Oak stepped in front of the cameras.

"I'm sorry, everyone," he said. "Please don't broadcast what happened here."

"What? Why not?" one of them asked.

Oak gave a small laugh. "The weather affected other regions, but several legendary Pokemon are involved. Publicizing this could cause panic. I think it would be best to let the matter pass without a report."

"I agree," Professor Ivy said. "I live in the Orange Islands, and I don't want outsiders disturbing these Pokemon. Let this end here."

The crew exchanged looks. As journalists, leaving a story this big unreported pained them. But Professor Oak carried enormous influence in Pokemon research, and Professor Ivy was an authority in this region. They could not lightly refuse either of them.

After a hard moment, they agreed. They could only imagine how high the ratings would have been if the footage aired.

Lugia had finished thanking the group. It rose into the sky once more.

Until we meet again.

It plunged into the sea and vanished, leaving the legendary Pokemon of the Orange Islands to live on in people's stories.

Ethan watched the calm water. He would have liked to catch Lugia, but this was plainly not the time. They had just fought side by side, and he could not bring himself to act against it now.

Never mind. I'll probably meet another Lugia someday.

"Ethan," Misty said, remembering that he had never met Professor Ivy. "Let me introduce you. This is Professor Ivy. Like Professor Oak, she's a well-known Pokemon researcher."

Chapter 219: Professor Ivy

Professor Ivy's main field of study was the way Pokemon's forms varied by region. She looked about twenty-six or twenty-seven, perhaps older. She took such good care of herself that Ethan could not judge her age from her appearance.

Her loose white lab coat did little to hide her striking figure. She often looked sleepy and moved at an unhurried pace, yet she was beautiful and carried herself with an easy, mature charm.

Brock had been so taken with her when they met that he had left Ash's group of his own accord. He was still at her laboratory, cheerfully doing whatever odd jobs needed doing.

Ethan gave the professor a glance, then offered his hand. "So you're Professor Ivy. It's a pleasure to meet you. Misty has told me about you, but I didn't expect we'd meet here."

He treated researchers with considerable respect, whether it was Professor Oak, Professor Cerise, or Ivy. In truth, he had recognized her the moment he saw her. His memories of the anime had left a strong impression of her. Apart from the Crystal Onix, she was among the things he remembered most clearly from the Orange Islands story.

Ivy smiled and shook his hand. "No need to be so formal, Mr. Vale. I've heard plenty about you too. I watched several of your Indigo League matches on television. The way you handled your opponents so decisively was impressive."

"You're too kind. My opponents helped make me look good."

After a little more polite conversation, Melody warmly invited everyone back to Shamouti Town. The streets held far fewer visitors now. Ethan could not tell whether the battle had driven them off or the festival had simply run its course.

Although the crisis with Lugia was over, Professors Oak and Ivy and Delia chose to stay for the time being. The two professors had been struck by what they had seen. They planned to write a paper together on how Pokemon battles affected the surrounding environment.
