The journalists who had arrived with them would have to wait for a ship calling at Shamouti Island that evening before returning to mainland Kanto. The helicopter's two pilots would remain until their company sent people to investigate the crash and complete the necessary questioning.

It was nearly dusk. Melody led the group to the banquet hall they had visited before. She had food brought in, then found Carol and the elderly man. The three spoke quietly, glancing toward Ethan and Ash as they talked.

Melody was plainly telling them what had happened. She kept her promise to Ethan, though, and did not reveal his three legendary birds.

Grrr...

Ethan's stomach brought his attention back to the table. Platters of fragrant food covered it. More than ten hours had passed since they left the hall the previous night. They had neither eaten nor rested properly, and all of them were ravenous.

They exchanged smiles and started eating without worrying about table manners. Only Sabrina remained elegant, taking small bites and chewing slowly.

Oak, Ivy, and Delia ate at a more ordinary pace. They had left Kanto around midday, only a few hours earlier, so it was simply dinner time for them.

For a while the banquet hall was quiet except for chewing and swallowing. Conversation resumed only after Ethan and the others had satisfied their hunger.

With Ash's stomach finally full, Delia had a chance to talk to her son. She watched him fondly as he told her all about his recent travels, adding a question or comment now and then.

Nearby, Oak and Ivy were discussing their proposed paper with enthusiasm. Misty listened from beside them until she could not resist mentioning Ethan's explanation of the butterfly effect.

"The butterfly effect?" Ivy looked toward him with interest. "I haven't heard of that theory. It seems you have an unusual way of looking at science as well as talent for battling, Mr. Vale."

Oak smiled at Ivy. "I almost forgot to mention it. Ethan isn't only a Trainer. He's also an assistant to Professor Cerise."

"Cerise? The one in Vermilion City?" Ivy looked surprised. "So you work with him. Does that make you something of a scholar too?"

Ethan shook his head quickly. "I don't have the education or experience to call myself that. I just run errands for Professor Cerise. I'd be embarrassed to claim to be a scholar in front of you two."

Ivy took that for modesty and plainly did not believe him.

The elderly man approached the table with Carol and Melody. He thanked Ethan and the others for helping Shamouti Island through its crisis, then welcomed the two professors.

"Commanders, thank you! Without you, the world might have been destroyed!"

Ethan knew the entire world would have survived even if the island had exploded or frozen over. The belief was deeply rooted among the islanders, though, and he had no desire to argue. He accepted the man's thanks.

Only now did Ethan learn that the elder who had played up the prophecy earlier was Shamouti Town's mayor.

Yet looking at Carol, he felt he had forgotten something. She turned to him.

"Mr. Vale, didn't Maren come back with you?"

"Oh no." Ethan gave a rueful smile. "We left her on Fire Island. I'm sorry. She should still be there; her boat ran aground, so she can't get back alone."

"I see." Carol smiled helplessly. "It isn't your fault. Don't worry. I'll go and bring her back myself in a little while."

Chapter 220: The Moon Is Beautiful Tonight

"One more thing," Ethan said as he remembered it. "That collector started all this, and we don't know whether he survived. Could you go to Lightning Island and check on him too?"

"Of course. Leave it to us," Carol replied.

After she left, the mayor brought in musicians to thank Ethan and the others. Melody took the stage as well and played a tune on her flute.

She had changed back into her festival clothes. Beneath her white veil, she looked graceful and beautiful. After everything she had experienced with Ethan's group, her feelings toward them had changed. At the least, she now considered them friends.

The tune was nothing like Lugia's Song. Its quicker rhythm lifted everyone's spirits.

In such a festive mood, Professor Ivy set aside discussion of her paper and picked up a glass of wine. She could drink quite well. After several glasses on her own, her expression had not changed. Seeing Ethan and Sabrina sipping nearby, she joined them for a chat.

Ethan took a mouthful and considered the taste. It was not made from grain. Shamouti Island must brew this fruit wine locally. It was mild rather than harsh, with a sweet fruit flavor.

Sabrina tried a few sips too, but before she finished even half a glass, her cheeks had begun to flush. She rarely drankperhaps she never hadand was clearly unused to the taste of alcohol.

"You two are drinking so slowly," Ivy said with a laugh. "Don't tell me you can't hold your wine. There'll be plenty more occasions like this. If a little wine gets you drunk, you'll miss half the fun. Come on, cheers!"

Without waiting for an answer, she clinked her glass against Ethan's. He drank his wine in one go. Before he could put the glass down, Ivy filled it again.

"Professor Ivy..." Ethan shook his head with a wry smile. "I really can't drink much. This is too fast for me."

"That's why you need practice. Another toast!" She tapped her glass against his again.

Ethan sighed. She wanted someone to drink with her, and he could hardly escape. He emptied his glass, then covered its rim with his hand. Ivy gave him a sidelong look but did not press him further.

At the other end of the table, Ash watched the adults with longing. When he heard the fruit wine was weak, he tried to sneak a taste. Delia caught him immediately and forbade it. He put the glass down in defeat.

"Little boys should listen to their mothers," Misty teased him. Then, when she thought no one was looking, she stole a few sips herself. Her face turned red as an apple.

Melody happened to see her. Before long, they were bickering again, this time over how old someone ought to be before drinking.

The hall was full of noisy conversation. Ethan liked it. He surveyed the table and found that Tracey was the only one apart from the revelry.

Tracey admired Professor Oak so much that he sat beside him like a diligent student, notebook in hand, asking one question after another. From Oak's expression, Ethan suspected he wanted to try the fruit wine too. But with Tracey looking up to him, he was determined to maintain his image and patiently answered every question.

The burden of being famous. Ethan felt a little sorry for him. Perhaps he would face the same predicament one day.

Dinner continued as stars appeared outside. Once everyone was full, fatigue struck all at once. Fortunately, Melody had arranged rooms for them.

Ethan left first. In his room, he took a cool shower to wash away the day's exhaustion. He had changed into sleepwear and was about to go to bed when someone knocked.

"Ethan, are you asleep?"

It was Misty.

"Not yet. What is it?" He opened the door and found her waiting in the hall.

"Ash and I talked it over," she said hopefully. "We're planning to leave around noon tomorrow and head to the next island. When are you going? Would you like to travel with us?"

"I don't think so." Ethan considered it, then shook his head. "We're going to different places. Let's each follow our own route. Knowing our luck, we'll run into one another again soon."

"...All right." Misty sighed, unable to hide her disappointment. She turned and walked away.

Ethan was about to close his door when he noticed Sabrina leaning against the wall farther down the hall. It was dim enough that he had missed her before. She had surely heard his conversation with Misty.

Sabrina watched Misty disappear around the corner. "That girl really likes you," she said softly.

Ethan paused, choosing his words. "Maybe. I think of her as a little sister, though. Nothing more."

"I see."

Sabrina looked at him and began opening the door to her room. Before she went in, Ethan spoke on impulse.

"Would you like to come in and sit for a while?"

She turned back, unsure why he was inviting her into his room so late. Given their closeness, she saw no reason to refuse.

"All right."

When Sabrina entered, Ethan closed the door almost without thinking. Her hair was still damp from a shower. A faint trace of wine remained about her.

In the dim room, she looked beautiful. Ethan found himself watching her.

After a moment, Sabrina smiled. "What are you looking at?"

"I... The moon is beautiful tonight."

Sabrina glanced out the window. A cloud hid the moon completely. There was nothing to see. Ethan had plainly reached for the first thing that came to mind.

Her psychic senses told her the mood was different tonight. Ethan, too, seemed unlike himself. Perhaps something was about to happen. She fell quiet.

He kept looking at her. Though he had drunk water only a short while ago, his mouth felt dry. Perhaps the wine had made him less cautious. He moved closer.

Sabrina neither retreated nor pushed him away. She watched him calmly, and her stillness gave him courage.

Ethan stopped before her, close enough that she could feel his breath. He held her gaze for a long moment. Then he lowered his head and gently kissed her.

Chapter 221: The Morning After

Ethan slept until the sun was high before he opened his eyes.

He rubbed his temples. The fruit wine had a stronger aftereffect than he had expected. He had thought a night's sleep would clear his head, but even mild wine could leave him dizzy if he drank enough.

Half awake, he reached across the bed. The space beside him was empty. Sabrina was gone.

Where did she go?

He yawned, sat up, and looked around the room. She was nowhere in sight.

Looking back, he had been impulsive the night before. With wine clouding his judgment, he had gone much further with Sabrina than he had planned.

After washing, Ethan folded the bedsheet and put it in his bag. He would find somewhere to dispose of it after they left. He did not want to leave evidence of last night behind for anyone to find and laugh about.

He went to Sabrina's room and knocked. No one answered. Puzzled, he headed for the banquet hall.

Ash, Misty, Tracey, Delia, and the others were eating a leisurely lunch. They greeted him as he entered.

"You're up late, Ethan!" Ash said around a mouthful of food. "We've been awake for ages. We're leaving after lunch to challenge the next Gym. Misty said that if you didn't get up soon, we should pull your blanket off!"

Misty's face flushed. "Don't make things up!" She glanced shyly at Ethan, then gave Ash a sharp knock on the head, Delia's presence notwithstanding. "You're the one who said that. Don't blame me!"

Their bags were packed and bulging beside Ash's feet.

Ethan smiled. "I've been exhausted these last few days. I didn't want to get up. And I wouldn't recommend barging into my room."

"Why not?" Ash asked.

"I might be sleeping without pajamas," Ethan said with a grin. "You'd both regret the surprise."

Ash and Tracey fell silent. Ethan let the joke stand and approached the table.

Several people were missing. "Where are Professor Oak and Professor Ivy?"

Misty studied him. Perhaps she was imagining it, but something about Ethan seemed different today, as though he had matured overnight.

"They took diving equipment and went to the three islands with Carol," she said. "They're studying the undersea currents for their paper. I think they want to test a theory."

"That sounds like hard work," Ethan said. "I thought professors like them spent all their time in laboratories. I didn't expect them to go into the field themselves."

Tracey remembered something and lowered his voice. "Carol said that when she went to bring Maren back last night, she also searched Lightning Island. She found wreckage everywhere, but no sign of the collector or his body. Maybe he slipped away."

The collector had survived Lugia's attack and escaped? Ethan raised an eyebrow. He was surprised, though the man had never struck him as someone who would die easily.

Had the ordeal changed the collector's mind? Would he still try to capture Lugia? Ethan could not know. Once he left the Orange Islands, he doubted they would ever meet again.

He still had not found Sabrina. As he looked around, ready to ask about her, footsteps sounded behind him.

She entered the hall with Maren just behind her. Sabrina's hair was damp from a recent shower. Maren yawned and looked as though she, too, had only just woken.

Maren spotted Ethan first and widened her eyes. "You have some nerve! I kindly took you to Fire Island, and you left me there without a word. I waited all night in the cold!"

She had heard the full story from Carol and did not truly blame them. The complaint was meant as a joke to ease the awkwardness. Her voice was hoarse, though, and she sounded congested. Ethan felt guilty.

"I'm sorry. The collector captured us, and we couldn't get word to you."

Misty and Tracey apologized too. Seeing their serious expressions, Maren realized her joke had misfired.

"It's all right," she said with a wave. "It's just a cold. I've taken medicine, and a few days' rest will see me right."

Maren sat down at the long table. Ethan finally turned his full attention to Sabrina. Her face and manner seemed normal. He knew her too well to assume that meant he could read how she felt.

"Sabrina..." He cleared his throat. "Are you all right?"

She understood what he meant and gave him an amused look. There was a new warmth in her eyes.

"I'm fine. The bed was hard, and I worked up a sweat last night, so I took a shower."

The others heard an odd exchange without making anything of it. Ethan was relieved she did not seem upset with him. They could talk properly in private later.

There was still something else on his mind. In the heat of the moment, he had given no thought to restraint or protection. Remembering it now made him uneasy.

Chapter 222: Catching Slowking

After lunch, Ash and his friends left Shamouti Island in Maren's repaired speedboat, reluctant to go.

Ash's mother, Delia, stayed behind, to no one's surprise. With her son always traveling, she lived alone and must have felt lonely at times. She planned to return to Pallet Town with Professor Oak when he finished his research here.

Ethan did not want to view their friendship through a narrow lens, but it was hard not to wonder whether there was something more between them.

He had no time to dwell on that gossip. He had concerns of his own.

Once he and Sabrina had packed, they left their luggage in their rooms rather than depart at once. They headed for the altar on the other side of Shamouti Island. A rare Pokemon still waited there for Sabrina to catch.

They walked through dense forest. Ethan looked at the trees around them. The freezing caused by the God of Thunder had damaged them to varying degrees. Their leaves and branches looked wilted, but the trees had survived and were still full of life.

It was quiet now. With no one else around, Ethan and Sabrina finally had a chance to be alone.

He had wanted to say something on the way. Yet Sabrina treated him exactly as she always had, and he found that puzzling. After what had happened between them, shouldn't she want to talk about it? She seemed almost unmoved. She was the one who had the most at stake.

Ethan could not make sense of it. In the end, he kept quiet. Sabrina was hard to read. She must have her own thoughts about last night; he would wait for her to raise the subject.

They crossed the forest and climbed over a broken cliff before reaching the altar. The talking Slowking was there, standing with its hands behind its back and gazing out to sea like an old man at peace with the world.

Hearing them approach, it turned. "Ah. It's you."

It seemed a little surprised to see them again. "The end of the world has been averted. The God of the Sea has gone back beneath the waves, and this year's festival is over. You have no reason to come to the altar anymore."

"You're wrong about that," Ethan said. "We have another reason."

He gave Slowking a pitying look, shook his head, and stepped back to let Sabrina forward. This was her capture to make. He did not need to take part; the altar was her stage.

Slowking had no idea what awaited it. It watched Sabrina approach, looking confused. Only when she took out a Poke Ball did it sigh.

"Oh, dear. I don't want to fight you, and I don't want to leave this island. Could you please go away and pretend you never saw me?"

If Ethan had been the one trying to catch it, he would have respected that wish and given up. Unfortunately for Slowking, it was facing Sabrina: the coldhearted girl who was all too willing to use her psychic power against someone weaker.

She regarded it coolly. Its wishes did not change her mind. She wanted this talking Pokemon, and she sent out her own.

A flash of blue light revealed Kadabra on the altar.

Slowking tried to reason with Sabrina again. She paid no attention. Through telepathy, she ordered Kadabra to attack.

Kadabra raised its spoon and sent a burst of Confusion straight at Slowking.

Slowking might look vacant, but unlike its unevolved form, it was remarkably intelligent. It had been watching Kadabra closely. It thrust out a hand and answered with psychic power of its own.

Bang!

The two attacks met in midair and canceled each other out. Neither Pokemon gained ground.

Ethan looked around, found a flat rock, and sat down to watch. He even had time to let several of his Pokemon out to watch with him.

Kadabra was a pure Psychic type. Slowking was Psychic type too, but it was also a Water type, which gave it another option in battle. Kadabra had Sabrina on its side, though. Her commands made better use of its moves, and she could watch for openings it might miss.

The two Pokemon traded attacks for a while. Ethan turned to Gardevoir with a smile. "You're not the only one getting stronger. Sabrina's Kadabra has grown a lot too. Do you remember fighting it when we challenged Saffron Gym? It feels like yesterday."

Gardevoir looked at Kadabra and nodded slightly. As another Psychic-type Pokemon, it could feel the two strong powers clashing.

A Water Gun shot past Ethan, drawing his attention back to the fight. He had no idea how old this Slowking was, but its moves were powerful. It did not seem used to battling, though. It was more of a thinker than a fighter and apparently got little exercise; after such a short fight, it was already panting.

Its intelligence was another distraction. Slowking kept glancing at Ethan's Pokemon, especially Haunter, which wore a mischievous grin. It worried they might rush into the fight at any moment.

Sabrina heard Ethan's praise of Kadabra and glanced at him without showing what she felt. In truth, she minded that Kadabra had not defeated Slowking quickly in front of him. Everyone wanted to look their best before someone they liked, even a girl with psychic powers.

She told Kadabra to stop, then walked toward Slowking herself.

Slowking watched in confusion. Did she mean to end the fight?

Instead, Sabrina raised a hand toward it. A frightening surge of psychic power enveloped Slowking and knocked it down before it could react.

For all its intelligence, Slowking had never faced anything like this. It had not imagined that a human could wield psychic power, much less that this woman would be stronger than Kadabra.

Ethan was stunned too. Sabrina had entered the battle herself. Had watching Kadabra made her eager to fight? Or did she think a Pokemon she caught with her own hands would obey her more readily?

He scratched his head, unable to understand it.

Slowking struggled to its feet. Before it could fight back, a second wave of psychic power struck it, then a third. It went down again.

Pain ran through its body, and it mourned its freedom. It had spent much of its strength fighting Kadabra. It could not keep battling this terrifying woman.

When Sabrina saw that Slowking had stopped resisting, she promptly took out a Poke Ball and threw it at the Pokemon.

Chapter 223: Melody's Feelings

When Ethan and Sabrina returned to Shamouti Town, Professors Oak and Ivy were arriving at the banquet hall too. They exchanged friendly nods.

The festival was over, and the islanders had returned to their ordinary lives. The crowds had scattered. With Ash and his friends gone as well, the town felt quiet.

Inside the hall, Ivy took a laptop from her bag, opened a document, and showed it to Ethan.

"Have a look. This is what we've gathered from yesterday's and today's investigation. It's only a first draft, but the overall direction is settled. We still need to revise some details."

"Me?" Ethan raised his eyebrows. "It's your research. I'm not sure someone outside the project should be reading it."

"It's fine," Oak said. "We included your thoughts on the butterfly effect without asking first. Strictly speaking, you've contributed to the paper."

Ethan felt embarrassed. The butterfly effect was not his idea. It had been easy enough to mention it in conversation. Seeing it in a research paper made him feel as though he had taken credit for someone else's work.

He could hardly explain the truth without talking about the world he had come from and its history. So he moved closer to the screen.

With both professors watching, he pressed the down arrow and began to read. Within a few lines, he found formulas and technical terms he did not understand. Long blocks of academic prose made his eyes swim.

Oak and Ivy were respected Pokemon researchers. Ethan knew better than to invent an opinion about work he could not follow. Fortunately, they seemed to want to include him, not test him.

He scrolled down, trying to look thoughtful. He found the passage based on his butterfly effect explanation. At the end, after the two professors' names, he saw his own.

"You've put me down as an author? Is that really appropriate?"

"You were directly involved in what happened," Ivy said. "Your account matters. Professor Oak agreed, so don't worry."

Oak nodded. "We only did the thinking afterward. Without you and Ash taking action, we wouldn't be here investigating at all. And you're a field investigator for Professor Cerise's laboratory. Including your name makes sense."

"All right." Ethan sighed softly. Once the paper was published, other researchers would know his name. He did not intend to become a Pokemon scholar, so he expected little practical benefit beyond more attention. But the professors meant well. Arguing further would only dismiss their kindness.

...

After so many delays, it was finally time to leave Shamouti Island. Ethan collected his luggage and went outside with Sabrina. He called Charizard, ready to fly to the island of the next Gym.

The professors still had research to do, so they stayed. Delia joined them to see the travelers off. Melody and Carol came too when they heard Ethan was leaving.

On the steps, Melody watched him with mixed feelings. Their time together had been brief, but she liked him. The moments aboard the collector's airship had stayed with her. He was handsome, and he had three legendary birds besides. Yet once he flew away, she might never see him again.

Her feelings had barely begun to grow, and already she had to let them go. She wished he could stay.

Melody lifted her flute and played a gentle, lonely tune. It drifted over the town. However much she wished otherwise, she could not change his plans.

Ethan did not know what she was thinking. He only noticed that the girl who had been so cheerful earlier seemed subdued at their parting.

He waved farewell and helped Sabrina onto Charizard's back. As he prepared to climb up after her, Melody called out.

"Ethan, wait!"

It was the first time she had used his name without an honorific. He turned. She hurried over and gave him a great hug, surprising the others. Then she stood on tiptoe and kissed his cheek.

Tears shone in her eyes. "Don't read anything into it. It's the customary farewell kiss."

"Melody..." Carol watched her sister from a distance. She had once been the festival maiden herself. She knew perfectly well that there was no such custom.

Melody had made it up. Carol understood her sister's feelings, and her heart sank. After Ethan and Sabrina checked out, Carol had cleaned his room and found used tissues in the wastebasket. She knew the two travelers were close. Melody was younger than Ethan, and he already had someone beside him. Her sister was hoping for something that could not happen.

Ethan, remembering the greeting Melody had given him when they arrived, took her explanation at face value. He returned the hug briefly.

"Take care. Maybe we'll meet again someday."

He said a proper goodbye, let go, and climbed onto Charizard. A light nudge from his feet sent it beating its wings and rising into the sky.

The wind from its wings made the people below shield their eyes and scattered Melody's hair. Only Carol saw the tears running down her sister's cheeks.

For Ethan, Shamouti Island had been one episode in a longer journey. His ambitions lay across a much wider world, where countless adventures still waited.

Chapter 224: Rudy

Trovita Island lay in the southern Orange Islands, one of the few major islands in the region.

From the island's earliest days, powerful undercurrents had swept through the waters to its north, making the sea treacherous. Over thousands of years, the currents had claimed countless lives and torn countless families apart.

More than a century ago, hoping to prevent so many ships from sinking, the islanders had driven sixty or seventy enormous stone pillars deep into the seabed. The pillars created dozens of whirlpools of different sizes. They changed the course of the water and slowed the undercurrents, while becoming Trovita Island's most distinctive sight.

Ethan and Sabrina had set out at noon, so it was already dusk when they reached the island. Sunset spread a layer of gold across it.

They landed outside the town. Ethan followed his navigation straight to Trovita Gym without delay.

The Gym stood well away from town in a four-story building. From the outside, it looked more like an ordinary Pokemon Center than a Gym. As at the previous two Gyms, no one waited by its entrance. It seemed few Trainers came to challenge it.

Ethan climbed the high steps and went inside. He asked a staff member to announce their arrival. Before long, a red-haired man in a black shirt and white vest approached with a group of identically dressed attendants.

His bearing and confident expression suggested that he was the Gym Leader. His clothes, though, made Ethan think of a Latin dancer.

Ethan stepped forward to explain why he had come. The man glanced at him, walked straight past, and stopped before Sabrina.

"Beautiful lady, might I have the pleasure of knowing your name?"

He gave a showy little bow, as though inviting a dance partner onto the floor.

Ethan was at a loss for words. Danny had behaved like this too. Why were the male Orange League Gym Leaders so frivolous? Could none of them take their eyes off a pretty woman?

Sabrina's brows drew together. Ethan could see her annoyance, and before she could use her powers on the man, he moved between them.

"Judging by your outfit, you must be a waiter," Ethan said with a smile. "I'm here to challenge the Gym. Is the Leader available? And please stop bothering my companion. If you upset her, things could get very unpleasant."

"A waiter?" The man heard the mockery in Ethan's voice and took a step back. He studied Ethan as though noticing him for the first time. "Ah, forgive me. Seeing such a beautiful lady made me forget myself. But you've got the wrong idea. I'm no waiter. I forgot to introduce myself: I'm Rudy, the Gym Leader here, and the Orange Crew's Star of the South!"

He finished with a few dance steps. He was plainly proud of his title.

Ethan merely waved a hand. "Sorry. I'm from Kanto, so I'm not familiar with the titles you use in a little place like this. Can we get started?"

A little place?

Rudy felt his blood pressure soar. Ethan's indifferent tone seemed to shatter all his pride. In the Orange League, the Gym Leaders were the center of attention. The public followed their every move. When had anyone ever looked down on them like this?

And seeing a beautiful woman at Ethan's side made him even harder to bear. Rudy drew a deep breath and calmed himself.

He often considered himself handsome. Now he had to admit that the man before him was perhaps just a tiny bit better looking. What an irritating fellow.

Rudy pursed his lips and folded his arms. "It doesn't matter if you've never heard of us. My time is valuable, and I won't battle just anyone who wanders in. If you want to challenge me, you'll have to prove you're worthy of being my opponent."

"Prove it?" Ethan thought for a moment, then took his badge case from his bag and opened it. Inside were two Orange League badges.

"I've already beaten two Gyms before yours. Will these do?"

Rudy gave the badges a brief glance and snorted. "Even Gym Leaders vary in skill. Those two don't understand battling at all. Don't compare me to them."

"Really?" Ethan feigned surprise. "That's wonderful. To be honest, I was worried your match would be as childish as the challenges at those other Gyms. What a relief. It's been so long since I've had a proper Gym battle that I'm starting to forget what one feels like."

Beside them, Sabrina could feel the sparks flying as the two men traded barbs.

When someone approached her like this in the past, she had always dealt with him herself, using her psychic powers to "remind" him to keep his distance. She had not expected Ethan to stand up for her and quarrel with another man out of jealousy.

After last night, she could sense that Ethan had begun to think of her as someone no outsider was allowed to touch, almost as his private possession. She had never felt anything like it before. She ought to have disliked being thought of that way, yet a strange warmth filled her instead.

Rudy led them behind the Gym to a river. Steep cliffs lined both banks, their sides thick with shrubs. A small dock stood on the shore, with a motorboat moored beside it.

Rudy pointed to the boat. "Proving yourself is simple. Ride this motorboat down the river and have your Pokemon use its moves to knock down every target along the way. Do that, and you'll have earned the right to battle me."

His smile turned thin. "Oh, and it's getting dark. Normally I wouldn't run the test at this hour, but since you're in such a hurry, I'm sure you won't mind if it goes on into the night?"

"Of course not." Ethan knew Rudy was making the test harder, but his expression did not change. "Day or night, it's all the same to me."

"Confident, aren't you?" Rudy chuckled. "Then I look forward to seeing how you do."

Chapter 225: Target Practice

They untied the motorboat, and Ethan, Sabrina, and Rudy climbed aboard. Rudy would drive and supervise Ethan's attempt, so he had to come along. His attendants had gone ahead to take up positions and operate the targets.

After a short wait, Rudy heard over his radio that everything was ready. He started the boat.

The diesel engine rumbled and belched smoke as it carried the three of them upriver, toward the forest in Trovita Island's interior. The boat sped across the water, leaving ripples in its wake. Fortunately, the river was calm, and the ride was less rough than Ethan had expected.

The banks flashed past. After a little over a minute of silence, Rudy spoke again. "Another hundred meters and we'll reach the course I prepared. Are you ready?"

Ethan nodded. After a moment's thought, he opened a Poke Ball and sent out Jolteon.

Jolteon rubbed affectionately against him. Ethan explained the task, and it gave a soft cry, turned around, and fixed its attention on the river ahead, alert to the slightest movement.

"Jolteon?" Rudy sounded surprised as he looked at the spiky Pokemon. "On the water, with the boat moving underneath you, I thought you'd choose a Water-type like the other challengers."

"Don't worry," Ethan said, his face expressionless. "My Jolteon is strong. It can handle a small test like this."

In fact, he had considered using Blastoise. As Rudy said, Blastoise had spent so much time in the water that the rocking of a boat would hardly bother it. Its Water Gun was fast and reliable, and powerful enough to smash an ordinary wooden target.

But Blastoise had one obvious drawback: its size and weight. Summoning it onto the motorboat would surely sink them.

A round target suddenly dropped from the trunk of a large tree. Ethan turned toward it. At the same instant, Jolteon's stiff fur bristled, and a bright current shot out.

Bang!

The electricity struck dead center. The target burst into countless fragments that fell into the river.

Ethan gauged the strength of the attack from the wreckage, then crouched beside Jolteon. "Nice work. But save your energy from here on. That was more power than you needed. Ease up a little on the next one."

Jolteon cried in reply. Another target rose from behind a boulder, and without waiting for Ethan's command, it fired a second current. The target shattered too, though the damage was clearly less extensive this time.

"Good!" Ethan rubbed its head. "Keep it there."

The boat continued forward. Targets appeared more and more often. Some moved, and at times two or three appeared together. Despite the rising difficulty, Jolteon performed brilliantly, sending out controlled bursts of electricity and destroying every target.

Bang! Bang! Bang, bang!

The cracks echoed over the quiet river.

At the back of the boat, Rudy watched Jolteon in astonishment. The sun had set, though the sky was not yet completely dark. In the fading twilight, visibility was getting worse by the minute.

The Pokemon brought by previous challengers had varied in ability, so Rudy had set a passing mark: a hit rate of at least eighty percent. He knew Ethan must have some skill to have beaten the first two Gym Leaders. Still, he had expected the light to throw Ethan's Pokemon off enough to miss a few targets and perform below its usual standard.

Then Rudy could make a few cutting remarks while Ethan squirmed, before making an "exception" and graciously allowing the challenge anyway. It would have repaid Ethan for the insults earlier.

Instead, Jolteon had done so well. How disappointing.

On second thought, it had to be Ethan's strongest Pokemon. Of course it was! He had exposed his trump card just to pass the test. How naive.

The more Rudy thought about it, the more certain he became.

After completing a circuit, the motorboat returned to the dock. Once it had stopped, Rudy acknowledged Ethan's skill, disappointed though he was.

"You passed," he said haughtily, wearing the look of a man who had seen through everything. "Under the rules, you may come to the Gym and challenge me tomorrow. But now that you've shown me your best Pokemon so early, what will you do in the actual match?"

Ethan stared at him, baffled. His best Pokemon? Did Rudy mean Jolteon?

When Rudy had spoken of a course he had carefully prepared, Ethan had expected something difficult. One circuit had proved surprisingly easy. Perhaps Rudy's view of the world was simply too narrow. Having never ventured farther afield, he probably assumed every Trainer was on the level of those in the Orange League.

Rudy mistook Ethan's silence for embarrassment at having his plan exposed. He smiled. "Tomorrow, I'll use Pokemon whose types match yours. We'll fight three one-on-one rounds. Win two, and you'll have cleared my Gym challenge."

Then he approached Sabrina again and offered his hand with exaggerated courtesy. He evidently had not given up. "Since it's so late, beautiful lady, would you care to have dinner with me?"

Sabrina ignored him. She looked at Ethan and tilted her head, as though asking whether she could teach this arrogant man a lesson.

Ethan gave her a small smile and turned to leave. Sabrina followed without sparing Rudy another glance.

Rudy stood there, embarrassed, and slowly lowered the hand he had held out to her.

Chapter 226: Another Move

After leaving the Gym, Ethan and Sabrina returned to Trovita Island's town. They chose an upscale restaurant for a generous dinner, then looked for somewhere to spend the night.

They passed the Pokemon Center, but Ethan suggested they would sleep more comfortably elsewhere. He took Sabrina to a nearby hotel and booked two rooms with large beds.

The Pokemon Center offered free food and lodging, but its men's and women's dormitories were plain and offered little privacy. They were convenient when a Trainer needed a bed at a moment's notice, yet they did not suit him tonight.

In a larger group, he would have been happy to stay there. Sharing stories and travel experiences before bed could deepen friendships and broaden everyone's horizons. With only the two of them, though, it would feel too quiet.

More to the point, after getting closer to Sabrina the night before, Ethan hoped to go a little further. The Pokemon Center's separate dormitories would be an obstacle.

The bustle of the day faded, and moonlight covered the island. Late at night, the streetlamps glowed dimly. Life moved slowly here. Shops had closed early, there was little entertainment, and hardly anyone walked the streets. The whole world seemed to have fallen silent.

In her room, Sabrina washed up and changed into her pajamas. Just as she was about to get into bed, someone knocked.

Knock, knock, knock.

She did not need her psychic powers to know who would dare come to her door at this hour. When she opened it, Ethan stood outside, smiling broadly.

She gave him a sidelong look. "What is it?"

His smile grew brighter. "Nothing. I couldn't sleep, so I thought I'd come by and see you."

Come by? Sabrina almost laughed. Whatever he called it, she knew perfectly well what he wanted.

She stayed in the doorway, deliberately giving him no room to enter. Would he take the hint and leave? Instead, Ethan brazenly squeezed past her and sat down on the sofa. She watched him with an amused half smile.

There was no stopping him. Shaking her head, she closed the door and left him alone.

Neither spoke. The silence grew peculiar. Sabrina climbed into bed while Ethan sat on the sofa, watching her every movement with bright eyes. When she actually closed her eyes, he endured the wait for a long while, then rose and walked to the bedside.

"So... who do you think will win tomorrow? Me or Rudy?"

It was an absurdly obvious question. Ethan only wanted to break the silence and get her talking. Several seconds passed. Sabrina kept her eyes shut and breathed evenly, as though asleep.

No answer? Ethan raised an eyebrow. She could not really be asleep. Even an ordinary person would hardly lower their guard with someone else still in the room, much less a psychic like Sabrina.

Since she would not acknowledge him, Ethan took off his shoes and lay down beside her. The mattress creaked and dipped slightly beneath their combined weight.

Quiet returned. He could hear their hearts beating hard.

He waited a little longer. Sabrina still did not move. Ethan gave a small cough and slowly reached toward her. His hand edged beneath the warm covers, and the moment his fingers touched her pajamas, she opened her eyes.

She turned to look at him. Ethan thought she would stop him. Instead, she said softly, "Turn off the light..."

...

At noon the next day, Rudy sat inside Trovita Gym with one leg crossed over the other, tapping his fingers impatiently on a table.

He had expected Ethan to take his challenge seriously and arrive early in the morning. Rudy had waited three or four hours and drunk several cups of coffee, yet Ethan was nowhere to be seen.

Rudy was not especially invested in the battle or in Ethan himself. He wanted to show Sabrina what a gentleman he could be and make an impression on her. He had risen early, put on his favorite clothes, styled his hair, and used hair gel, all so she would be dazzled when they met.

He checked the clock. It would be twelve in a few minutes, and Ethan still had not appeared. Had he decided he could not win and slipped away rather than face Rudy?

As Rudy brooded, a little girl of five or six came over. "Big brother, is the person you're waiting for still not here? Mahri's hungry. Let's go have lunch."

"All right." Rudy doted on his younger sister and would never let her go hungry. "Let's go to the dining room. Today's main dish is your favorite."

He patted her head fondly and stood to leave with her. Then the Gym doors opened. Ethan came in slowly, yawning, with Sabrina beside him.

Rudy frowned. Of all times to arrive, they had chosen this one.

But when he looked at Sabrina, he paused. Her complexion was rosy, and she somehow seemed even more beautiful than the day before. Her usually cool bearing now held a hint of allure.

"What?" Ethan chuckled at Rudy's stunned expression. "Did you know I was coming? Is that why you're here to welcome me?"

Rudy nearly exploded with anger. As if he would stand there waiting to welcome Ethan!

"Hmph. You finally showed up? I thought you'd run away before the match."

"You were waiting for me all this time?" Ethan looked surprised, then spread his hands. "Sorry. I went to sleep late last night, so I couldn't get here earlier."

"Wow!" Mahri stared at Sabrina. "You're so pretty!"

Most people would have been pleased to hear a compliment from such a young child, however little they cared about praise. Sabrina's expression did not change at all.

Ethan knew that was simply her nature. He bent down to the girl and answered for her. "You're very cute yourself. Do you live here at the Gym?"

"Mm-hmm!" The girl nodded vigorously and lifted her chin. "I'm Mahri. He's my big brother, and I live here!"

Chapter 227: Dance Training

Now that Ethan had arrived, Rudy had to abandon his plans for lunch and take him to the battlefield.

Before they left, Ethan heard lively music coming from somewhere inside the Gym. He looked toward a room with an open door. A dozen or so Pokemon of different species were moving to the beat, twisting their bodies in strange steps that looked like a dance.

"What are they doing?"

Rudy could not be bothered to explain, but his sister answered eagerly, as though showing off. "They're dancing!"

"Dancing?" Ethan paused. He had assumed they were doing some sort of special training, but apparently they really were just dancing. They looked happy, though human dance steps performed by Pokemon had an odd, comical quality.

Mahri nodded hard. "That's right! Every Pokemon at our Gym learns to dance. It's what makes this place special!"

Perhaps Mahri's explanation was too simple, or perhaps calling it a specialty made Rudy's method sound less serious than he liked. Although he did not want to speak to Ethan, he added, "Hmph. The days of teaching Pokemon nothing but moves are over. I invented this training method myself. It might not look related to battling, but it greatly improves a Pokemon's natural abilities!"

Greatly improves them? Ethan watched the Pokemon more closely, but saw nothing unusual in their movements. He supposed dance could be another way to develop coordination and flexibility.

He could understand making Hitmonchan, Electabuzz, or even Ninetales dance. But what was Starmie or Golem supposed to gain from it? One had a body as hard as a gem, and the other was heavy and awkward. Asking them to dance and bend their bodies seemed rather unreasonable.

Still, Ethan kept his thoughts to himself. Everyone had a training method of their own. He had no reason to interfere with Rudy's.

Rudy and Mahri never did get their lunch. Each grabbed a piece of bread to tide them over.

They left the Gym, with Ethan and Sabrina following Rudy. Surrounded by his many attendants and Mahri, the group took a path along the coast and came to a cliff.

The clifftop was barren, with hardly any vegetation. Marks left in the ground showed where, centuries earlier, the islanders had cut stone for the pillars. To protect their descendants, they had quarried away more than half a hill right where they stood. It must have been an enormous undertaking.

Ethan looked beyond the cliff. There stood the remarkable formation of dozens of huge stone pillars. Water rushed and roared below. When he stepped to the edge and looked down, he could see scores of whirlpools spinning at once, drawing seawater into them.

Anyone swimming there, or traveling in an ordinary small boat, would be in grave danger if caught in those currents. The whirlpools could wreck a vessel and drag its passengers into the depths.

Rudy pointed among the stone pillars. "That's where we'll battle."

Ethan followed his finger and saw what looked like a small mountain rising out of the sea, its top cut flat. The surface was about the size of a soccer field, and a regulation battlefield had been marked on it.

He had not fought a formal match in a long time. The sight of the field stirred an excitement he had missed.

Rudy cast him a sidelong glance and snorted. "First you have to get there. Consider that the final test. It isn't mandatory, though. If you can't make it on your own, just say so. My hot-air balloon can take you across."

He raised his chin toward a clearing in the distance. A balloon with a hanging basket stood there, ready to fly.

The battlefield was at least three hundred meters from the cliff in a straight line. Without a large Flying-type Pokemon, reaching it safely would be difficult. Crossing the water was out of the question. Challengers with no other way across would have to accept a ride in Rudy's balloon, putting them at a psychological disadvantage before the match even began.

Ethan had no such problem. He took a Poke Ball from his belt, released Charizard, and climbed onto its back. Rudy watched with a trace of disappointment.

Sabrina was about to join Ethan, but Rudy stopped her.

"I'm sorry, miss," he said, shaking his head. "Only the Trainers taking part in the match may go onto the field. You'll have to watch with my sister and the others."

With no choice, Sabrina boarded the hot-air balloon with Mahri and Rudy's attendants to watch from above.

Charizard beat its wings and carried Ethan to the battlefield first. Rudy released a Pidgeot and followed close behind.

Standing on the platform, buffeted by cold wind and listening to the unending roar of the sea below, Ethan could finally feel how dangerous this place was, both for him and for the Pokemon.

The two Trainers took their places on opposite sides.

Rudy brushed back his bangs. "All right. Beat me and you'll earn the Spike Shell Badge. Which Pokemon will you send out first?"

By custom, the Gym Leader should send out the first Pokemon. Trovita Gym, however, required opponents of matching types, so the challenger had to choose first. Rudy would then select a Pokemon of the same type. It might make the battle a little less varied, but it also prevented either side from gaining an advantage through type matchups.

Ethan did not need to think long. Charizard was already out, so it might as well take the first round.

"Charizard, you're up first!"

"Roar!"

Charizard landed, spread its wings, and roared at Rudy. Rudy took out a Poke Ball and released his first Pokemon in answer.

Chapter 228: Charizard vs. Ninetales

A flash of blue light brought Rudy's first Pokemon onto the battlefield. Ethan looked over and found himself facing a Ninetales.

It resembled a fox, with gleaming golden fur and nine long tails. Its eyes were a vivid red, but they made it look noble and elegant rather than frightening.

Ethan recognized it at once. Ninetales had been among the Pokemon dancing at the Gym. It looked no different from any other member of its species. He wondered whether Rudy's training method would produce any unusual results in battle. Rudy must have confidence in it, at least, to send it against Charizard.

Still, after all Rudy's talk, Ethan had half expected him to send out a Charizard of his own. Charizard was both Fire and Flying type, while Ninetales was only Fire type. Their types matched in one respect but not the other. Apparently Rudy did not have enough Pokemon to make every matchup identical.

It was hardly a serious problem. Battles between Pokemon with exactly the same types could show which was stronger and which Trainer commanded better, but they also lost some variety and suspense.

One of Rudy's attendants served as referee from the hot-air balloon. Sabrina and Mahri leaned forward to watch the battlefield below. From up there, they had a clear view of the entire field.

Sabrina had absolute faith in Ethan. Mahri adored her brother just as unreservedly. Neither believed the person she supported could lose.

When everyone was ready, the referee blew a whistle. Ethan gave the first command.

"Charizard, Wing Attack!"

Charizard beat its wings and climbed, circling to build speed.

Ethan had two reasons not to open with a Fire-type move. Ninetales would take only half the usual damage from fire, and he first needed to work out which Ability it had.

Ninetales generally had Flash Fire or Drought. Flash Fire was troublesome: a Fire-type attack would be absorbed and strengthen Ninetales's own Fire-type moves. Drought, on the other hand, would turn the weather harshly sunny when Ninetales entered the field.

A few clouds still drifted across the sky, briefly blocking the sun. If this Ninetales had Drought, Ethan expected them to clear and the sky around them to brighten. Nothing had changed, so its Ability had to be Flash Fire.

Across the field, Rudy's expression did not change when Ethan refrained from using fire. He had not expected it, yet it made sense. His Ninetales did indeed have Flash Fire, giving it an advantage against Fire-type opponents.

At least Ethan understood Abilities. He was not one of those beginners who ordered move after move without adapting their tactics to an opponent's strengths.

"Ninetales, Flamethrower!"

Rudy had fewer reservations. Whether Charizard had Blaze or Solar Power, he judged that the first offered Ethan no help here and the second was hampered by the conditions. Ninetales's flames would deal only half damage, but it could keep firing from a distance and wear Charizard down.

Charizard was already diving. As the flames rushed toward it, it turned slightly and slipped past them with ease. It swept toward Ninetales like the wind.

"Ninetales, dodge!" Rudy shouted.

Years of dance practice had at least made Ninetales nimble. It sprang to the side and avoided the attack. As the two Pokemon passed, Charizard's back was exposed for an instant. Rudy seized the opening.

"Now! Quick Attack!"

Ninetales launched itself like an arrow and slammed into Charizard. Charizard staggered back and swung a claw at it, but Ninetales nimbly evaded that too.

"Good! Use Confuse Ray!" Rudy called.

Confuse Ray was a Ghost-type move that could leave its target confused for a time. Like many Pokemon, Ninetales could use moves beyond its own type. Alongside common Normal-type moves, it had a few Ghost-type techniques.

Ethan knew that already. He also knew Ninetales could learn Psychic-type moves. The hit Charizard had taken did not shake him.

"Charizard, Dragon Rage!"

Before Confuse Ray reached it, Charizard fired a shock wave from its mouth.

Boom!

The two attacks met in a fierce explosion. Because it happened so close, smoke swallowed Charizard completely. Rudy strained to see whether the blast had caught it.

Then Charizard emerged from the smoke, and several crescent blades of air struck Ninetales hard. Air Slash!

Ninetales cried out in pain and fell. Rudy's expression changed.

"Ninetales! Get up!"

Charizard flew clear of the smoke and headed straight for Ninetales as it struggled to rise.

"Well done! Charizard, Seismic Toss!" Ethan shouted.

Charizard closed in and wrapped its thick forelimbs around Ninetales, pinning its joints so it could not move. Beating its wings, it carried Ninetales high into the air.

They soon rose level with the hot-air balloon. Mahri could clearly see the panic in Ninetales's eyes.

Charizard turned and plunged toward the ground. Ninetales seemed to realize what was coming and struggled desperately, but it could not break free.

"It's over," Sabrina said quietly. Mahri heard her and covered her eyes in fear.

Charizard spun faster as it descended. The hard stone rushed closer. Rudy could do nothing but watch as Ninetales was driven into the mountaintop.

Boom!

The ground shook. For a moment, the entire small mountain seemed to tremble.

Chapter 229: An Easy Victory

Ninetales lay limp on the ground, its eyes rolled back. It had lost consciousness.

Charizard stood nearby, breathing a little hard. Apart from a few superficial injuries, Ninetales had done it no real harm.

Rudy stared in disbelief. Had his Ninetales truly been defeated so easily? Even the referee had not expected the battle to end so quickly. As one of Rudy's attendants, he knew how troublesome Ninetales could be. It had helped Rudy beat countless challengers and was among the Gym's best Pokemon. Never before had it been beaten so cleanly.

The referee favored Rudy, but he still had to call the result fairly. "Ninetales is unable to battle! Ethan wins the first round!"

Fine. Rudy recalled Ninetales, admitting to himself that he had underestimated Ethan. He had thought yesterday's Jolteon was Ethan's trump card. Now it seemed Charizard was his real ace.

There were still two rounds. If Rudy won both, the match would be his. He let out a long breath and steadied himself.

"All right, let's get on with the second round." Then he seemed to remember something. He smiled slyly. "Oh, I forgot to tell you: our rules don't allow you to use the same Pokemon twice. Charizard can't battle again."

It sounded as though Rudy had made the rule to stop Charizard, but Ethan did not mind. All his Pokemon could fight. Whichever he chose would do.

He called Charizard back to his side and patted its shoulder. "Good work. You earned a rest."

Without returning it to its ball yet, Ethan took another Poke Ball from his belt and threw it onto the field.

"Now it's your turn. Go, Jolteon!"

Blue light flashed, and Jolteon appeared in the middle of the battlefield. Rudy looked smug. Just as he'd expected! Ever since Jolteon had passed the test yesterday, he'd been certain Ethan would use it in the match. Prepared for it, he confidently threw his own Poke Ball.

Up in the hot-air balloon, Mahri brightened at the sight of the familiar Pokemon. Her disappointment over Ninetales vanished.

"Great, it's Electabuzz! That's one of my brother's strongest Pokemon too! He won't lose this time!"

"Oh?" Sabrina glanced at her. "You trust your brother that much?"

"Of course!" Mahri nodded vigorously. "He's the strongest person in the world! Your friend can't possibly beat Electabuzz!"

The strongest in the world? Couldn't possibly? Sabrina frowned. The Trainers who came to challenge this remote Gym were probably weak on the whole. Rudy's frequent wins had given Mahri a false impression.

She ought not to get angry with such a young child. Misty or Chloe would likely have laughed off the innocent boast. But after growing so close to Ethan, Sabrina could hardly hear someone belittle him and say nothing.

This was Sabrina, after all: a psychic who had once used her formidable powers to toy with other people's bodies and minds. Sometimes even her parents, and even Ethan, could not guess what she was thinking. It was a mistake to expect her to react like anyone else.

"No. You're wrong," Sabrina said. Her expression did not change, but her words cut through Mahri's certainty with the same merciless directness she had once shown at Saffron Gym. "I don't know who the strongest person in the world is. It certainly isn't your brother. My companion is about to beat him."

For a moment, the beautiful woman seemed hateful to Mahri. She shook her head. "That's not true! I don't believe you! My brother won't lose!"

"You don't believe me?" Sabrina gave a cold snort and raised her chin. "Then watch the field."

Alarmed, Mahri grabbed the railing with both hands, rose onto her toes, and looked down.

While they had been talking, the two Pokemon had already clashed fiercely. Mahri caught the battle at the moment it turned. Under Jolteon's relentless attack, Electabuzz could not hold on. It rolled several times and fell to the ground. It tried to struggle back up, but its strength was spent, and it collapsed.

"What?" Mahri's eyes widened. "How could that happen? My brother lost again?"

To Mahri it was unbelievable. To Sabrina, the outcome had never been in doubt. Ethan was the newly crowned Indigo League Champion, his victory still fresh in everyone's minds. Rudy was the Gym Leader of a small league on a remote island. There was a considerable gap between their abilities.

Before Mahri could recover, the referee announced the result from the balloon. "Electabuzz is unable to battle! The challenger wins!"

On the field, Ethan saw how embarrassed Rudy was. He did not press the advantage. "Well? Are we continuing? If this is best of three, I believe I've already won."

Rudy's mouth moved, but no answer came at first. He could not bear to lose so easily. He wanted to take at least one round. It was not only the loss of face before Sabrina. More than anything, he did not want to retreat in disgrace before the little sister he adored.

Besides, he was convinced Ethan could have no more formidable Pokemon after Charizard and Jolteon.

"You won," Rudy said at last. He took a badge from his clothes and affected an easy manner. "Under the rules, you can take my badge now. But my strongest Pokemon hasn't appeared yet. It would be a shame to leave it at that. What do you say? Do you dare fight the last round?"

Afraid Ethan might simply take the badge and go, he hurried on. "Of course, if you lack confidence and want to skip it, I understand. It was my mistake not to lead with my strongest Pokemon. But the rules are the rules. Since you managed to win two rounds, I can't stop you leaving."

Ethan recognized the taunt and saw exactly what Rudy wanted. He had already lost, yet he still wanted to save face. He could not admit defeat without another word.

Still, Ethan rarely turned down a battle that would give his Pokemon experience.

"All right." He sighed as though reluctant, hiding a smile. "If you insist, I'll play along for one more round."

Chapter 230: The Spike Shell Badge

When Ethan accepted the third round, Rudy drew a deep breath and sent out his Pokemon first. Blue light flashed, and a Starmie appeared on the battlefield.

So eager to win back a round, Rudy had abandoned even his own rule requiring the challenger to choose first.

Starmie was a Water- and Psychic-type Pokemon. Ethan could have matched its Water type with Blastoise, but after a moment's thought, he chose Gardevoir.

Rudy wanted to win, but Ethan had no intention of losing. He would give him no such opening. And if Starmie really was Rudy's strongest Pokemon, he had every reason to take it seriously.

Rudy frowned at Gardevoir. He had clearly never seen one, and the unknown made him uneasy.

"What Pokemon is that?"

"Gardevoir," Ethan said. "Don't worry. I'm still following the rules. They do share a type."

That told Rudy the graceful, humanlike Pokemon had at least a Psychic typing. He ignored Ethan's jab at his own breach of the rules. Winning this round mattered more than anything now. If he wanted to save face, he had to give it everything he had.

Both Pokemon were ready. Rudy decided to strike first.

"Starmie, Water Gun!"

A jet of water shot from one of Starmie's hard points. Ethan did not move or speak, but Gardevoir understood his command through their mental link. A barrier formed from psychic power appeared before it.

Splish, splash!

The water struck the invisible barrier, broke into spray, and ran across the ground.

A Psychic type after all. Rudy clicked his tongue. What a nuisance.

Seeing that he could not break through Gardevoir's defense, he tossed his head and snapped his fingers.

"Then I won't hold anything back! Come on, let's dance to the music!"

Dance? Ethan stared. One of Rudy's attendants in the hot-air balloon switched on a speaker. Fast, rhythmic music poured out, filling the battlefield.

Mahri swayed to the beat. "It's my brother's music! Great, he's finally using that move!"

On the field, Rudy lost himself in the rhythm and performed a short modern dance. Ethan watched in embarrassment and instinctively stepped away. A man who could dance like that without a trace of self-consciousness was frightening. Better to keep his distance.

Worse, Starmie began spinning to the music too.

After his dance, Rudy raised his voice. "This is our dance of passion! Starmie, give them a Thunderbolt full of passion!"

Ethan blinked. He had not expected Starmie to know an Electric-type move. Then his eyes fell on the puddle Water Gun had left in front of Gardevoir. He understood Rudy's plan and hurriedly told Gardevoir to move.

Electricity burst from the spinning Starmie in every direction. Gardevoir used Teleport to appear elsewhere. An instant after it vanished, a current struck the puddle. It crackled sharply, and white vapor billowed upward.

That was close. Rudy did have a tactic after all.

Water Gun could soak an opponent, setting it up for an Electric-type attack. Without advance warning, who would expect a Water-type Starmie to use Thunderbolt? A Trainer caught off guard could suffer badly.

But surprise was what made the combination work. Once it had failed, it had lost its advantage. Starmie had no other unexpected trick to reveal.

Ethan could also see what Rudy's dance training had achieved. It made his Pokemon more agile, but had given them no extra power.

The result was soon clear. The music seemed to make Starmie faster still, but Gardevoir found its opening. Magical Leaf staggered Starmie; Disarming Voice followed, and Dazzling Gleam finally knocked it flat.

Thud!

Starmie toppled over and lay motionless. Rudy felt his hands and feet go cold. He had lost completely.

Three rounds, three defeats. However much he hated admitting it, he now understood the gulf between himself and Ethan.

With a helpless sigh, Rudy recalled the unconscious Starmie. He took a badge from his clothes, walked over, and tossed it to Ethan.

"You won. By the rules, this badge is yours!"

Ethan caught it easily. He had been about to say something polite, but Rudy turned and left without looking back. He seemed unwilling to remain there another second. He had lost face, and there was already bad blood between them. Perhaps he had nothing to say. At least Ethan was spared the trouble of trying to console him.

He shook his head and studied the badge. The Trovita Gym's Spike Shell Badge was flat, shaped like a spiny shell with more than a dozen points. An unidentified pink stone was set in the center of its spiral. Ethan did not find it as attractive as his first two Orange League badges.

He had just placed it in his badge case when startled cries came from above. With the match over, Sabrina had climbed over the balloon's railing and jumped before it could descend.

"What?"

Mahri and Rudy's attendants stared in shock. Even Rudy looked up at the sound and widened his eyes. Was she trying to get herself killed?

Ethan whistled sharply. Gardevoir teleported to his side, and he quickly returned it to its Poke Ball. He leaped onto Charizard and flew toward Sabrina.

Sabrina had intended to use her psychic powers to land safely. But when she saw Ethan coming for her on Charizard, she smiled faintly, let her powers rest, and watched him with a gentle gaze.

Charizard swept beneath her like the wind. Ethan caught her securely in his arms.

Cradled like a princess, Sabrina looked up at him and smiled. "Now that we have the badge, shall we leave?"

"Of course. There's nothing here to keep us." Ethan looked at her face and could not resist kissing her. Then he lightly nudged Charizard's side with one foot. "Charizard, don't land. Let's head straight for the next Gym!"

Charizard roared in answer and beat its wings powerfully. With Rudy watching in mixed feelings and Mahri looking on in admiration, it flew into the distance and disappeared.

Chapter 231: An Island of Bug Pokemon

Ethan and Sabrina left Trovita Island and continued north. Their destination was Kumquat Gym, the last of the Orange League's four Gyms.

The Gym stood on Kumquat Island, a long way from Trovita Island. One lay in the south of the archipelago, the other in the north. Even on Charizard, the trip would take at least a day and a night, and their route would carry them past Shamouti Island again.

The sea breeze brushed their faces as Charizard bore them onward. They flew for hours. When the sky began to darken and Ethan's legs had grown numb from sitting, he decided not to push on. He patted Charizard's neck and guided it toward a nearby island.

There was still a long distance to cover, but nothing urgent awaited them. They could take their time.

As they descended, they saw thick trees covering most of the small island. Beyond a broad forest, a busy town finally came into view by the sea. Ethan felt relieved. At least it was not a deserted island where they would have to camp for the night.

"Shall we stop here?" Sabrina asked softly.

"Mm-hmm. Sleeping in a tent has its charms, but honestly, a soft bed and hot water whenever you want it are much better after a day of travel."

Sabrina agreed. If a bed was available, who would choose to sleep in a tent every night?

They noticed something unusual as soon as they entered the town. Almost every Trainer they passed had a Bug-type Pokemon at their side. Even wild Bug types poked their heads out around town, apparently unafraid of the people living there.

After walking a few hundred meters, Ethan had seen Caterpie, Weedle, Pinsir, Venonat, Paras, Butterfree, Beedrill, and Scyther. Bug Pokemon seemed to be everywhere. The island felt like a haven for them, and the residents took their presence for granted.

"There are so many Bug-type Pokemon," Sabrina said, frowning. She had never liked insects.

"There certainly are." Ethan heard the distaste in her voice and rubbed his chin. "It might have something to do with that forest. It looked dense from the air. It's smaller than Viridian Forest, but still large enough to be a good place for Bug types to live and breed."

They followed the street to a hotel and went to the front desk. The young receptionist rose when she saw them and gave a slight bow.

"Welcome."

In the conversation that followed, Ethan learned that they had landed on Murcott Island. As he had guessed, it was famous for its many Bug-type Pokemon. According to the receptionist, at least ten thousand lived in the forest outside town.

She looked from Ethan to Sabrina and smiled. "How many rooms would you like?"

"Two," Sabrina answered simply, as she usually did.

"Of course." The receptionist was about to register them when Ethan stopped her with a smile.

"Wait. Make it one room."

Sabrina looked at him curiously. He shrugged. "Two rooms would be a waste. We can spend the money we save on a more comfortable one."

She rolled her eyes at the excuse. Ethan was pressing his luck again, and he was hardly concealing what he wanted. She knew his reason, yet by now she found she could not refuse him.

Seeing that Sabrina did not object, the receptionist booked a single room. Ethan paid and took the key card. In a fine mood, he walked ahead, humming an unfamiliar tune. Sabrina followed with her arms folded, shaking her head helplessly, and entered the room after him.

Moonlight shone again that night. Ethan got what he had hoped for, and the two enjoyed each other's company.

...

Ethan slept until late the following morning. The other side of the bed was empty. He found Sabrina already washed and dressed, sitting with perfect poise at the table and eating breakfast. Her phone screen was still lit beside her, as though she had just ended a call.

When he opened his eyes, she spoke gently. "Did I wake you?"

He shook his head, got dressed, and sat beside her. "What's wrong? You look serious. Did someone call you?"

Sabrina tipped her chin toward the phone. "It was President Goodshow of the Pokemon League. He wants me to return to Saffron City and take charge of Saffron Gym again."

Ethan frowned. "Didn't you say your father was looking after the Gym for you? Why do they need you back?"

She sighed. "It seems they only feel comfortable when I'm there myself."

"Your father was the previous Gym Leader, wasn't he? Isn't that enough? Do those League people doubt his ability?"

"I'm the current Leader, and I defeated him. They clearly value me more than they do my father." Sabrina gave a cold snort. She disliked the League's attempt to dictate where she should be. But Ethan's concern warmed her, and her voice softened. "Don't worry about them. I didn't agree. Even if I don't go back, they won't dare do anything to me."

Despite her assurance, the news troubled Ethan. They had grown so close that he wanted to be with her every night. He did not want her to leave.

Yet being a Gym Leader was her job. If she wanted to keep it, sooner or later they would have to part for a while. What a headache. Perhaps there was no use worrying now. They would deal with it when the time came.

After Ethan ate breakfast, they left the hotel. Instead of departing Murcott Island, they headed for the forest beside the town. Since they had come this far, they might as well explore and see whether any Pokemon there were worth catching.

Chapter 232: A Scyther Duel

A herd of Pinsir was fighting a group of Heracross for territory. Hard shells slammed together, pincers met horns, and more than a hundred Pokemon were caught in the fierce struggle.

Above a lake, a large, menacing swarm of Beedrill swept past. Ethan could only wonder whom they were going after.

The deeper he and Sabrina walked into the forest, the more obvious its dangers became. Even the air smelled different, thick with rotting wood and a mixture of fungi. They kept quiet and stepped lightly, doing their best not to attract the attention of the fiercely territorial Bug Pokemon.

They followed their instincts along winding paths, moving steadily farther in. After half a day, though, their hopes of finding a rare species had come to nothing. They had seen only common Pokemon, none that Ethan wanted to catch.

"Let's go," he said with a sigh. It felt as though they had wasted their time. He was about to call Charizard when Sabrina suddenly looked deeper into the trees.

"What is it?"

Despite the layers of forest between them and whatever lay ahead, Sabrina had sensed something. "There's a battle going on. Both Pokemon are stronger than the ones we've seen so far. One of them might be what you're looking for."

She expected Ethan to head off at once. When he did not move, she turned and found him staring intently at her neck. She touched it, but felt nothing unusual.

"What are you looking at?"

"Ahem. Nothing." Ethan guiltily looked away.

Only now had he noticed the marks he had left on the pale side of her neck. Several reddish bruises were visible there, enough to suggest what they had been doing the night before.

He had missed them because Sabrina usually wore her hair loose. It covered the marks until she moved. No wonder people in town had given them knowing looks and meaningful smiles earlier. Sabrina had been puzzled then, but Ethan understood now.

He absolutely could not let her find out. If he did, she might keep him out of her bed for days.

Rather than explain why he had been staring, he hurried in the direction Sabrina had indicated. She frowned at his strange behavior but followed him.

They went around several thick trees and walked another few dozen meters. The forest opened into a large clearing. Dozens of Scyther stood around a small pond, watching something in silence.

Ethan and Sabrina exchanged a glance and moved closer. Through the gaps in the crowd, they saw two Scyther fighting a duel. Wings beating, blade-like forearms clashing, they attacked with no sign of holding back. This was no ordinary game. Both looked intent on winning at any cost.

The other Scyther watched with solemn expressions, as though observing a ritual.

The scene reminded Ethan of nature documentaries from his old world, with their harsh contests in which the strongest survived. A winner would lead the group onward through the forest. A loser might have to drag its wounded body away alone and wait for death. Perhaps these fierce Scyther lived by the same rule here.

"Were those the two strong Pokemon you sensed?"

"Yes," Sabrina said softly. "They're both strong, but the one with the scar on its forehead is clearly stronger."

Ethan looked again. One of the Scyther was more muscular, with an X-shaped scar across its forehead. Apart from that, the two looked much alike. But the scarred one held a decisive advantage, pressing its opponent back with a relentless series of attacks.

"It is strong," Ethan agreed. "Unless something changes, the other one is going to lose. Its reactions, speed, and strength are all far behind."

Sure enough, the other Scyther soon fell. Wounds covered its body, and it could no longer move.

The scarred Scyther looked down at its rival with a proud glint in its eyes. It raised its scythe-like forearms in triumph. The others burst into celebration. They showed no sympathy for the loser, only delight in their new leader.

Survival of the fittest, Ethan thought.

After a while, the fallen Scyther regained a little strength. It struggled upright and gave the victor and its companions one lingering, resentful look. Then it staggered away without turning back.

As the two Scyther drew farther apart, Sabrina asked, "Which one do you want? The injured one can hardly resist now. You could probably catch it with a single Poke Ball. The winner still looks full of energy. Catching it would take more effort."

"If I'm catching one, it has to be the stronger one. But..." Ethan hesitated.

His collection of Pokemon kept growing, and he was already struggling to give them all enough attention. After considering it, he made up his mind. Endless second-guessing was not like him.

Besides, Scyther was a Bug- and Flying-type Pokemon, a combination he did not yet have. Catching it would give his team more options. Its evolved form, Scizor, looked impressive too, and it could later Mega Evolve into an even stronger Pokemon.

His hesitation gone, Ethan strode toward the Scyther with the scar on its forehead.

Chapter 233: Scythes Against Stone

Ethan's arrival caught the Scyther group's attention. Led by the one with the scar, they turned hostile eyes on him. As he looked around, they seemed ready to rush forward and tear him apart.

"Easy, easy." He waved a hand to calm them, then fixed his gaze on the scarred Scyther. "Let me introduce myself. I'm Ethan, a Trainer who travels around. I've come with bad intentionsuh, I mean, good intentions. First, congratulations on becoming the leader. There's also something important I'd like to discuss with you."

At another time, entering Scyther territory so boldly would have brought the whole group down on him. But their new leader had just won its duel. It was riding a wave of confidence, and a challenge now would give it a chance to establish its authority. Ethan had realized that, which was why he dared approach.

Sure enough, the scarred Scyther barked at its companions to stand back. It stepped forward and stared hard at Ethan.

"Scyther, Scyther!"

Ethan raised an eyebrow. He could not understand Pokemon speech, but fortunately he had an interpreter close by.

"Hey, Haunter. Stop sleeping and come do some work. What did it say?"

Haunter drifted lazily out of Ethan's shadow. "You only think of me when you need something." It rolled its eyes, but Ethan urged it on. "Fine. It said, 'You sneaky human. Get off my turf before I lose my temper, or one word from me and my boys will cut you in half!'"

Ethan hesitated and looked at Haunter. "Are you sure those were its exact words? It only made a few sounds. And even if Scyther are fierce, I doubt one talks like a street thug."

"What's so hard to understand?" Haunter spread its hands. "What did you expect from a Scyther? All muscle, no brains. I polished it up and filled in the context. The wording might be rough, but the meaning's about right. Don't worry about such a small detail."

Ethan stared at it. That explained the swagger. He had wondered why a Scyther living far from humans would call the others its "boys." Haunter had added its own flourishes.

After a long pause, Ethan sighed. "I see. Thank you. How thoughtful of you."

"You think so?" Haunter looked pleased. "I think so too!"

The Scyther in front of them was growing impatient. It rubbed its sharp forearms together, making a clack like blades striking. Ethan shook his head, ignored Haunter, and turned back to it.

"What I wanted to discuss is a partnership between strong allies. We could take on the Pokemon League together. Interested in defeating powerful opponents and making it to the very top?"

Scyther tilted its head. With its limited experience, it knew nothing of the Pokemon League and had no idea why Ethan suddenly wanted to climb a mountain.

Ethan reconsidered. "Let me put it another way. I challenge you to a battle. If I lose, I'll give you human food. If you lose, you'll become my partner and travel with me. How about it? Do you accept?"

That, Scyther understood. It looked down on Ethan. This human thought he could challenge it? How foolish.

It gave a loud cry. The other Scyther immediately moved aside, clearing a wide space. Ethan needed no translation this time. Just as he had expected, it had accepted.

He was about to take out a Poke Ball when something came to mind. He glanced toward the forest's edge. The Scyther defeated in the earlier duel had staggered almost to the trees.

As Ethan watched it go, he wondered whether this wounded Scyther might be the one Ash and his friends would encounter on their journey, the one Tracey would eventually catch. The more he thought about it, the likelier it seemed. There could hardly be so many coincidences.

But it had nothing to do with him, and he had no time to pursue it. He returned his attention to the scarred leader, took out the Poke Ball he had already chosen, and threw it into the clearing.

"I choose you! Go, Crystal Onix!"

Blue light flashed, and Crystal Onix appeared. It was even larger than Gyarados, though its entrance was calm rather than imposing.

This was its first formal battle since Ethan had caught it. In the meantime, it had trained and fought in private for a long while, so he was confident in its strength.

Most of all, he had the type advantage. Ground-type moves could not hurt Scyther, but Rock-type attacks would deal four times the usual damage. That put Crystal Onix in a strong position.

The sight of such a huge opponent did not make Scyther retreat. It roared and charged first. Even against a powerful foe, it was eager to fight.

Scyther moved fast. It closed the distance in a single rush, raised its gleaming scythes, and swung hard at Crystal Onix. Ethan recognized the Normal-type move Slash.
