"Crystal Onix, don't let it intimidate you. Use Harden!"

A sheen passed over Crystal Onix's body. Every part of its surface grew tougher.

Clang!

Scyther's blade struck with a metallic ring. Crystal Onix did not even have a scratch. Its stone-like body truly was hard. Onix were naturally well defended, and Harden only made that advantage greater. The impact left both of Scyther's forearms numb.

Chapter 234: The Thirteenth Pokemon

"Crystal Onix, our turn to fight back! Use Rock Throw!"

Crystal Onix roared. A huge rock formed in midair and hurtled toward Scyther.

At the last moment, Scyther used its speed to dodge. Its blades flashed more sharply than before, and it struck Crystal Onix in the belly again. But just like the first attack, this one failed to break through Crystal Onix's defenses. It did only slight damage.

The watching Scyther stirred. Even the scarred leader had not expected its opponent to be this difficult. Their new leader appeared to be on the attack, but the others could see the problem: if its blades could not get through Crystal Onix's hide, attacking again and again would accomplish nothing.

Scyther had come close once more. Ethan seized the chance.

"Don't let it get away! Use Bind!"

Crystal Onix swept its thick tail toward Scyther, trying to coil around it and hold it by force.

"Scyther!"

Scyther answered at once with Double Team. Four images of it appeared before Crystal Onix. Its tail stopped in midair. It looked from one Scyther to another, unable to tell which was real.

Ethan could not pick out the real one either, but there was a simple way to deal with so few copies.

"Crystal Onix, don't let them confuse you. Hit them all with Rock Tomb!"

Crystal Onix roared again. More than a dozen huge rocks fell into the clearing, smashing the false Scyther and forming a rough ring of stone that blocked every route around it.

Bang!

Scyther had nowhere to dodge. Rock Tomb caught it too and knocked it to the ground. Rock was strong against both of Scyther's types, Bug and Flying. The attack dealt four times the usual damage.

Scyther had landed several hits while Crystal Onix remained in good condition. Crystal Onix had needed only one solid blow to badly hurt Scyther. The type advantage was decisive, and to Ethan it confirmed the value of the battle strategy he had chosen.

Scyther rolled several times, coming to a stop covered in dirt. It looked a mess.

Ethan held off on another attack. He wanted to see what Scyther could still do.

After a moment, it shook its head and struggled upright. Panting, it leaped onto a rock, fixed its eyes on Crystal Onix, and raised its arms in an unusual stance.

Swords Dance. Ethan recognized the move immediately. Scyther knew its attacks were not getting through and meant to raise their power.

Its limbs moved in a solemn dance. A faint golden light gathered around it. With fresh confidence, Scyther charged again.

The rocks scattered across the clearing slowed it down, though. It had to leap from one to another to approach Crystal Onix.

Ethan followed it with his eyes and gave his next command calmly. "Crystal Onix, use Stone Edge!"

Crystal Onix slammed its tail against the ground. After a slight tremor, a line of sharp stone spikes burst upward toward Scyther.

Bang! Bang! Bang!

The pointed stones tore through the earth. Just as they reached it, Scyther surged forward with Quick Attack. It avoided Stone Edge and arrived in front of Crystal Onix.

The two Pokemon crossed paths in an instant. Scyther's blades came down harder than before. Crystal Onix gave a muffled grunt, and its body rocked slightly.

Swords Dance had finally made Scyther's attack count. Even so, it had not done much damage.

Before Scyther could feel any relief, Ethan's steady voice reached it.

"Crystal Onix, bind it!"

Scyther wanted to dodge, but the rocks around it limited its movement. It was running out of strength, and the burst of speed from Quick Attack had left it unable to move as quickly as before. Crystal Onix's tail wound tightly around it.

"Nowhere to run," Ethan said. "Crystal Onix, finish it!"

Scyther struggled as the coils tightened. Crystal Onix was too strong to break free from. Its eyes rolled back, and it slipped into a daze. Crystal Onix lifted it high, then slammed it down against a rock.

Thud!

Scyther lay still for a long while. Ethan thought the battle was over and reached for a Poke Ball. To his surprise, Scyther rose with great difficulty.

He raised an eyebrow. It had lost consciousness, yet its stubborn fighting spirit kept its body facing Crystal Onix, blades held ready. What a remarkable warrior.

Ethan would not stop now. He threw the Poke Ball. Red light drew Scyther inside, and the ball shook violently. After rocking a few times, it released Scyther.

The capture had failed. Scyther stood in the same place, still holding its fighting stance.

"Crystal Onix, one last hit."

Crystal Onix swung its tail in an Iron Tail. The blow sent Scyther falling backward. This time, it did not rise.

Ethan retrieved the Poke Ball he had thrown. He studied the Scyther with the scar across its forehead for a long moment, then gently touched the ball to its head.

Brave warrior, become my partner.

Red light flashed again. This time the ball shook only once before the capture was complete.

The surrounding Scyther erupted in angry cries. Several looked ready to rush forward and rescue their leader.

Crystal Onix sensed their intent and moved in front of Ethan, throwing back its head with a roar. Beside it, Ethan narrowed his eyes and turned toward the group.

Faced with the two of them, the Scyther took a step back together. Shaken, none dared come any closer.

Ethan picked up the Poke Ball, shook his head, and walked back to Sabrina with Crystal Onix.

She was watching him with a gentle expression.

"Thanks to you, I caught a fine Pokemon," Ethan said with a smile. "I really appreciate it. We're done here now. It's time to leave."

Chapter 235: The Paper Is Published

As Ethan came back to her, Sabrina glanced at the Scyther still standing where he had left them.

"Are we just going to leave? They've lost their leader. What will they do now?"

Ethan looked at her in surprise. That did not sound like the sort of question she would once have asked. It sounded more like something he might say. Then again, they had spent so much time together and been through so much. As they grew closer, her outlook had gradually changed. They were finding more in common, and perhaps, with time, they would come to see even more things alike.

"Don't worry about them. They won't die out," Ethan said with a shrug. "The world can be harsh and unpredictable. They might fight for a new leader tonight. Once that defeated Scyther recovers and hears what happened, it might even sneak back to compete for the position again."

Sabrina nodded. She had asked out of curiosity, not sympathy for the Scyther. Ethan's explanation answered her question, and her interest faded.

They pushed through the branches and left the forest.

Ethan took a Poke Ball from his belt. Without any further action from him, it vanished in an instant.

Capturing Scyther had given him a thirteenth Pokemon. Under the Pokemon League's rules and the settings of his belt, a ball beyond his carrying limit had to be transferred back to Cerise Laboratory. After some thought, he had decided to send Blastoise.

He already had Gyarados among his Water types, so their roles overlapped somewhat. Weighing his options, he chose to leave Blastoise in the laboratory's care for now.

His phone regained its signal as they emerged. It vibrated, and he saw a message from Professor Ivy. She had also tried calling half an hour earlier, while he was in the forest. What did she want?

Ethan did not call her back immediately. First he messaged Chloe to tell her about Blastoise and ask her to look after his Pokemon.

Her answer came almost at once: "Don't worry, I'll take good care of it. I'll go straight to Cerise Park after school~ :)"

Her quick reply warmed him and made him smile. Wasn't Chloe supposed to be a good student? She had answered him during class.

He shook his head. When he looked up, Sabrina was watching him with a meaningful expression. Ethan's smile disappeared. He bent over his phone with an air of seriousness and opened Ivy's message.

Since their relationship had deepened, Sabrina had become especially attentive to him. She would not welcome any hint that he was flirting with someone else. Ethan was pleased by her concern. The more she cared, the more important he must be to her.

When she saw nothing further to object to, Sabrina looked away. Ethan let out a quiet breath and turned his attention to the message.

It was fairly long. He skimmed it and understood that Ivy had written after her call failed. The news was simple: she and Professor Oak had finished their research on Shamouti Island and published the final paper. She had wanted him to know.

At the bottom, she had included a link. Ethan opened it and landed on a respected scientific website, directly on the paper Oak and Ivy had published.

Reading more carefully, he found much new material compared with the draft they had shown him. His butterfly effect explanation remained, as did his name among the authors.

He was excited, certainly, but felt little beyond that. It gave him a title as a research contributor, perhaps, without changing his everyday life.

Still, he decided to call both professors.

Professor Oak exchanged a few polite words with him. They did not know each other well, so there was little else to discuss. Even in their brief conversation, though, Ethan could hear that Oak wanted to encourage his future work.

Oak likely knew that the new Indigo League Champion had a promising future if he continued to grow. At Oak's standing, he had no need to flatter Ethan, but being on friendly terms with him could do no harm.

Professor Ivy was much warmer. She spoke with an easy attentiveness that put Ethan at ease and even made a few jokes. Before hanging up, she invited him to visit her laboratory whenever he had the time.

Sabrina was standing nearby. Ethan did not dare say much in response, and offered a few noncommittal words.

After the call, he saw that Sabrina was growing impatient. He put an arm around her shoulders and smiled. "Sorry to keep you waiting. We should get moving."

He was about to summon Charizard when she stopped him. "Wait. Let's use something else this time. I'm tired of seeing everything from the air."

Ethan considered it, then pointed toward town. "I saw a passenger ship at the harbor earlier. Let's find out whether it has a route we can use."

Sabrina agreed, and they retraced their steps to the town on Murcott Island. They crossed it and reached a small harbor.

Even before they arrived, they could see a medium-sized passenger ship, three or four stories high, moored in the bay. They asked about its route. Its next stop was Hamlin Island, separated by only a stretch of sea from their destination, Kumquat Island.

Chapter 236: A Battle on the Deck

Ethan bought two tickets. He and Sabrina passed through security and boarded the passenger ship.

They had arrived just in time. Murcott Island was neither a commercial center nor heavily populated, so few large ships stopped there each day. Ten or fifteen minutes later and they would have missed this one. The next ship on a similar route toward Kumquat Island might not pass through until the following evening.

Several bird Pokemon flew overhead toward the Bug Pokemon forest. Ethan could not tell whether they were going there to feed or to hunt the Pokemon among the trees.

He and Sabrina climbed the gangway to the deck. The vessel was not especially large, and it appeared to make short trips between the Orange Islands. Its fittings, though, were unexpectedly luxurious.

Crystal chandeliers hung overhead. Ornaments of all shapes lined the passageways, and a thick red carpet ran from the middle of the deck into the cabin. The place gleamed.

Ethan took his eyes off a finely made handle shaped like a Horsea. "The Orange Islands really do know how to cater to tourists. I didn't expect a ship we boarded on a whim to be this extravagant." He smiled at Sabrina. "Looks like choosing this over Charizard was a good idea."

Sabrina rolled her eyes. She had been the one to suggest a different way of traveling. He did not have to sound so delighted with himself.

They had booked a cabin with one large bed. After finding it and putting away their luggage, they went to the restaurant.

After several intimate encounters, neither needed to spell out what their relationship had become. They could share a room and sleep in the same bed without treating it as a question.

As Ethan looked around the ship, he had to admit that sailing was slower than flying on Charizard but far more relaxing. They could sit on comfortable sofas, eat, watch television, and enjoy the journey. There was no sea wind striking their faces and no fear of being caught in a downpour.

Still, he should enjoy this sort of comfort only now and then. Too much of it might wear down his resolve, and he would lose the chance to train along the way.

The lavish ship reminded him of the S.S. Anne. That was hardly a reassuring comparison. Better to put it out of his mind and hope this voyage ended safely.

When Ethan and Sabrina entered the restaurant, the handsome pair drew plenty of attention. To the other passengers, they looked well matched in both appearance and bearing, and more than a few stole glances at them.

They chose a table by the window. Ethan pulled out Sabrina's chair, then called a server and ordered a generous, fairly expensive lunch.

The main course was prime beef flown in from Paldea. Seared to perfection and served with local side dishes, it tasted every bit as good as its price suggested. On a distant stage, a pianist played soft music while they ate at an unhurried pace and enjoyed the elegant room.

When they had finished, Ethan leaned back and looked through the window. On the aft deck, a group of Trainers had organized an impromptu Pokemon battle tournament in a simple battle area. Their table gave them a clear view.

Watching the Trainers work up a sweat brought back memories.

"Time goes quickly," Ethan said. "When I first came from Kalos to Kanto, I was traveling alone with only Ralts. I used to battle on ships like this to get stronger. Looking back, those were good days. I still remember them clearly."

Ethan seldom spoke about his past, so Sabrina listened carefully. She imagined him crossing the sea alone to challenge himself in an unfamiliar place. It could not have been easy.

A cheer rose from the deck. They turned to see that a strong young Trainer had just defeated another opponent. He had won five battles in a row. That alone marked him as better than most of the people taking part.

Ethan studied him. He looked eighteen or nineteen and wore practical fighting clothes. His untidy hair fell past his ears, with a green ribbon tied around his head. He looked less like a Trainer than a fighter from a contest with no rules.

Sabrina glanced from the young man back to Ethan. She could see the interest in his eyes.

"Does he remind you of yourself?" she asked softly.

"A little," Ethan said. Then he grinned. "But he's nowhere near as handsome as I am."

"How vain." Sabrina rolled her eyes again, though she laughed. "Are you going to try your hand?"

"I think I will. A little exercise after lunch. Want to come?"

She answered by getting up to go with him.

They left the restaurant for the aft deck. By the time they arrived, the young Trainer had defeated another opponent.

"That's six wins in a row!"

"He's on another level. No one here can beat him!"

The spectators talked excitedly while the Trainer scanned the crowd with wide eyes, looking for his next challenger.

"Another one down! Well? Anybody else think they can take me on?"

No one answered. People looked at one another, but none stepped forward.

"How disappointing," he muttered.

He was beginning to think nobody would accept when a calm man's voice came from behind the crowd.

"If there's no one else, I'll give it a try."

Chapter 237: Mugsy

At the sound of the voice, the spectators all turned at once. As they parted to make a path, a handsome man and a cool-looking beauty with long hair came slowly through the crowd.

Every eye was on the man, but his stride remained steady and his gaze fixed ahead. It was, of course, Ethan.

He stopped before the young Trainer and repeated himself. "Since no one else wants to battle you, I'll be your opponent."

The Trainer glanced at Sabrina. Her beauty caught his attention, but he looked away almost immediately, his eyes clear. However beautiful she was, Pokemon interested him more, and she would not interrupt his winning streak.

Ethan could not know the young man's thoughts, but he was impressed by his composure. It was natural to look at someone attractive. Yet this Trainer had shown more restraint than either Danny or Rudy, the two Orange Crew Gym Leaders Ethan had met. He was more disciplined than Ethan himself, who would certainly have stolen another glance or two.

The young Trainer studied Ethan in turn. Beneath his handsome appearance, Ethan seemed extraordinarily calm. Anyone willing to step forward now could not be an ordinary opponent. He must have confidence in his ability.

Out of respect, the Trainer introduced himself. "I'm Mugsy. What's your name?"

"Ethan. From your accent, I'd guess you aren't from the Orange Islands."

"That's right. I came from Johto specifically to challenge the Orange League." Mugsy lifted his chin with some pride. "To be honest, the Gym Leaders here aren't much. I've been here only four or five days, and I already have two badges. I thought the Orange League would be tougher."

People around them frowned. Most of the passengers were islanders. They did not enjoy hearing someone disparage their home Gyms to their faces. But Mugsy had just beaten several Trainers in succession. Before arguing, each of them had to consider whether they could back up their words in battle.

"On that, we agree," Ethan said, adding to their discomfort. "Compared with Kanto and other regions, the Gyms here aren't very professional. Especially those challenges that have nothing to do with battling. They're a headache."

He glanced at Mugsy's Pokemon. It was a Quagsire with a dopey, endearing look, the one that had defeated five opponents in a row.

"Anyway, back to the match. Will you keep using Quagsire?"

"Better not." Mugsy folded his arms and shook his head. "You don't look like an easy opponent, and this one is getting tired. I'll use another Pokemon against you."

Ethan had no objection. Since they were both choosing anew, he proposed something else. "Let's make it fair. We can each pick a Poke Ball and send our Pokemon out at the same time, without seeing what the other chose."

"A blind matchup? Fine by me."

Mugsy recalled Quagsire and took another ball from his belt. Ethan walked to the opposite Trainer's position, scanning the makeshift battlefield before he made his choice.

The field had been set up on a ship's deck. It could not be as sturdy as one built on solid ground. A particularly heavy Pokemon might damage it. He would have to choose someone agile.

After a moment's thought, he settled on his third Poke Ball. The two Trainers exchanged a glance, then released their Pokemon almost together.

"Come out, Jolteon!"

"Go, Poliwrath!"

Two flashes of blue light revealed Jolteon on Ethan's side and Poliwrath on Mugsy's.

Ethan raised an eyebrow. "Electric against Water. Even with a blind pick, I've ended up with the advantage."

"You have," Mugsy said with a smile. "I didn't expect Jolteon. Looks like I drew an unlucky matchup."

His voice held no alarm. The type disadvantage hardly seemed to bother him.

"Would you like to change Pokemon?" Ethan asked politely.

"Of course not." Mugsy waved the offer away. "Don't underestimate me. Poliwrath is my ace, and I'm good at fighting through a type disadvantage."

Ethan noticed a gleaming gold belt around Poliwrath's waist. "What's that?"

Mugsy grinned proudly. "A championship belt. Poliwrath and I won it at a Pokemon fighting tournament back home. We beat one strong opponent after another. It's a rare keepsake."

A fighting tournament. Ethan admired the belt. Unfortunately, he had no Fighting-type Pokemon, so he could not enter a competition like that for now.

While the Trainers talked, Jolteon and Poliwrath took their stances. The battle looked ready to begin at any moment. The spectators whispered among themselves, guessing who would win.

Mugsy had offended many of them, but he had a winning streak behind him, and most thought he would extend it. A few noticed Ethan's composed bearing and suspected he might be strong enough to end Mugsy's run.

Chapter 238: Poliwrath Presses On

"Begin!"

A sailor acting as referee swept down his arm. Before either Trainer spoke, Jolteon and Poliwrath both ran forward to meet each other.

The battle had barely started, but Ethan was already forming a plan. Poliwrath was both Water and Fighting type. Electric attacks would be twice as effective against it, yet its victory in a fighting tournament proved it was no ordinary opponent. Better to avoid close combat.

"Jolteon, don't get any closer! Keep your distance and use Thunder Shock!"

Jolteon braked sharply. It circled Poliwrath, adjusting its position to stay a safe distance away. Electricity crackled through its stiff fur and shot toward its opponent.

Poliwrath was not fast enough to dodge the bolt. To avoid taking a direct hit, it stopped and countered with a move of its own. An icy beam erupted from its mouth and drove through the middle of Jolteon's attack.

Ice Beam. It was a common Ice-type move among Water Pokemon, and Ethan recognized it immediately.

The attacks were unevenly matched. Ice Beam shattered Thunder Shock into fading sparks and kept going straight toward Jolteon. Jolteon darted aside just in time, letting the beam pass close by its body.

With the exchange turned around, Jolteon shook its fur and fired another Thunder Shock.

Poliwrath's breathing had grown slightly ragged. A powerful move took more energy and more time to recover from. It could not fire Ice Beam over and over without pause. This time it was still gathering itself and had no beam ready to block the attack.

The electricity came too quickly for Poliwrath to dodge. Thunder Shock struck it squarely, crackling across its body in golden arcs. Pain showed on its face.

Still, Poliwrath gritted its teeth and pressed toward Jolteon. It clearly wanted to close the gap. Jolteon was too agile, always moving away before Poliwrath could reach it.

Mugsy's brow gradually furrowed. The type disadvantage itself did not frighten him. What worried him was Ethan's refusal to let Poliwrath fight up close.

Ethan had guessed right. Poliwrath's fighting ability was exceptional, even stronger than one might expect from its Water typing. It had used that strength more than once to turn around matches against opponents who assumed they had already won. Now Ethan seemed to have seen through Mugsy's best chance.

He had to find a way to bring Poliwrath closer.

"Poliwrath! Aim at the ground beneath Jolteon. Ice Beam again!"

Poliwrath had recovered enough to use the move. Its new beam crossed the path of the first and spread ice across half the field. The deck beneath Jolteon's feet became slippery. To avoid falling, it had to slow down.

"Now! Body Slam!" Mugsy called.

Poliwrath closed in, leaped high, and spread its arms and legs as it dropped toward Jolteon with all its weight.

Ethan had understood Mugsy's intention the moment Poliwrath aimed at the deck. If Mugsy was determined to get close, Ethan would meet him head-on and see which Pokemon could take the harder hit.

Jolteon started to move away. "Don't dodge! Use Discharge right there!"

Jolteon obeyed without hesitation. It stood its ground and gathered power. A stronger burst of electricity erupted from its body just as Poliwrath dropped to within a few inches. Neither Pokemon could avoid the other now.

Crackle!

Bang!

Blinding gold light filled the deck. The spectators raised their arms over their eyes. They could not see the impact, but the sounds suggested that Body Slam had hit Jolteon and Discharge had struck Poliwrath.

Had they knocked each other out?

As the light faded, Jolteon shook its head, dazed by the blow. After a moment it recovered enough to climb back to its feet.

Poliwrath lay on the deck, smoke rising from its body. It looked scorched inside and out. Just as everyone thought the match was over, it coughed out a puff of black smoke and unsteadily stood again.

What determination! Ethan and the spectators were stunned. Poliwrath was badly hurt, yet it was still trying to fight for the win.

Was Mugsy not going to concede? Ethan waited, then looked toward him. Mugsy was frowning, but he made no move to stop the match.

Ethan could not understand it. In a fight for one's life, or a contest that mattered deeply, a Trainer might have reason to keep going even at terrible risk. This was only an informal match between travelers. Pushing a Pokemon so hard here was wrong. Mugsy was gambling with its life.

Ethan did not try to lecture him. Mugsy would have to live with his own decisions. All Ethan could do was end Poliwrath's suffering as quickly as possible.

"Jolteon, finish it. Thunder Fang!"

Jolteon nodded gravely. It too was impressed by Poliwrath's resolve. But as its opponent, the greatest respect it could offer was to defeat it without holding back.

Chapter 239: Kumquat Island

With hundreds of people watching, Poliwrath seemed to find a sudden last reserve of strength. It attacked Jolteon of its own accord with Mega Punch, its fists blurring as it struck.

The attack looked fierce, but everyone could see it was a final struggle. Jolteon followed the punches and dodged them nimbly.

Discharge and Body Slam had broken the ice left beneath Jolteon's feet by Ice Beam. With no danger of slipping, it could run freely again. As its speed returned and Poliwrath's strength ebbed, not one of the punches connected.

After dodging more than a dozen, Jolteon saw an opening and opened its jaws. Lightning flashed between its closely set fangs as it bit down on Poliwrath.

Crackle!

Electricity was strong against Poliwrath's Water type. The super effective Thunder Fang sent another surge through its body. This time Poliwrath could not even cry out. It collapsed and could no longer fight.

The referee declared Ethan the winner.

Applause rose from the crowd. Many had expected Mugsy to win, or at least to fight an even match. Instead, the handsome newcomer had beaten the Trainer on a winning streak with surprising ease. More than a few women among the spectators looked at Ethan with fresh interest.

Once the result was certain, Mugsy recalled his badly hurt Poliwrath, his face dark. Without another word or a backward glance, he pushed into the crowd and disappeared.

Ethan guessed the defeat had embarrassed him too much to offer even a polite farewell. His manner was a world away from his confidence before the match. Yet Poliwrath needed urgent care, and tending to it ought to be Mugsy's first concern.

Poliwrath had been unlucky. It had formidable fighting skills, but Ethan's type advantage had worn it down before it could make full use of them. Pokemon battles could change in an instant. No Trainer knew what an opponent would send out next, and luck played its part in every victory or defeat. Mugsy had drawn a poor matchup. Ethan might have struggled just as much in his place.

Jolteon returned to Ethan's feet. The deck quieted. Spectators looked around and murmured to one another, but after a long wait no one came forward to challenge him.

"Never mind." Without another opponent, Ethan had no reason to keep battling. He recalled Jolteon and left with Sabrina as the crowd watched.

...

Hooooonk!

With a deafening blast of its horn, the passenger ship eased toward a harbor. After more than twenty hours at sea and stops at three islands, it reached Hamlin Island at dusk the following day.

Ethan had not battled again since his match with Mugsy. The other Trainers aboard were generally weaker. Although they organized several more matches, none interested him as Mugsy had. He was the Indigo League Champion now; he no longer needed to accept every casual challenge as he once might have.

He had also used the voyage to spend time with Sabrina. It felt like a small holiday for the two of them.

As it happened, Sabrina's period had begun. Ethan fetched warm water and helped keep her abdomen warm, attending to her with care. Perhaps no one had ever looked after her so tenderly. His efforts touched her deeply, and throughout the day she regarded him with unusual warmth.

Ethan did not know what she was thinking. To him, caring for the woman he loved when she felt unwell was simply the right thing to do.

One thing did puzzle him: Mugsy had vanished after their match. Ethan had expected him to recover his confidence and ask for a rematch, but it seemed he had left the ship at one of the stops.

Perhaps Poliwrath's injuries had forced him to seek treatment at a town's Pokemon Center. Or perhaps losing with his strongest Pokemon convinced him he could not win, and he would rather leave than face Ethan again as the loser. Whatever the reason, Ethan doubted they would meet again.

"Where to now?" Sabrina asked gently, interrupting his thoughts.

They stood at the harbor, with Hamlin Island's town ahead of them.

"Let's keep going," Ethan suggested, looking up at the sky. "Kumquat Island isn't far. It has the final Gym. If we leave before sunset, we can find somewhere to stay when we arrive."

Sabrina agreed. These days, when they had a decision to make, she generally went along with Ethan's plans even if she had ideas of her own.

Ethan called Charizard, and they climbed onto its back for the flight to Kumquat Island.

They soared above the sea. Perhaps because night was approaching, the wind felt colder than before. Sabrina knew she was still not feeling her best, so she wrapped them both in psychic power to shield them from the chill.

They flew for more than two hours. By the time Kumquat Island appeared beneath them, night had fallen. The rest of the island was too dark to see clearly, so Ethan directed Charizard toward the harbor.

Even at this hour, the docks were brightly lit. A large passenger liner from another region was moored in the bay, and crowds of tourists streamed off it. Their chatter filled the lighted waterfront. Kumquat Island seemed to be another popular destination for travelers.

Ethan patted Charizard's neck, signaling it to descend gently. Even with its speed reduced, the sudden arrival startled several people below. Ethan apologized, recalled Charizard, and joined the flow of people walking with Sabrina toward the island's town.

Chapter 240: The Red-Haired Woman and the Hotel

They had gone only a few steps when Ethan spotted several groups of hotel employees stationed around the harbor. Dressed in distinctive uniforms, waving flags, and calling through megaphones, they competed for the arriving passengers.

"Welcome! Come stay at the Green Tangerine Hotel!"

"Still need a room? Check in at the Seven Oranges Express Hotel!"

"The Little Kumquat Inn has plenty of vacancies! Clean rooms and low prices!"

The calls came from every direction. A large crowd was disembarking, and the hotel staff had come to the port instead of waiting for guests to find them. They pressed forward like sharks drawn by the scent of blood. Competition on Kumquat Island was clearly fierce.

Among them, one curvy red-haired woman stood out. It was less her looks than her fiery manner and powerful voice. She could drown out everyone around her without a megaphone.

"Travelers, over here! If you're choosing a hotel, choose the Kumquat Hotel! It's the best in the whole Orange Archipelago. Rooms and food alike, I guarantee you'll be satisfied!"

Ethan smiled at the bold claim. The best hotel in the entire archipelago? Probably the most expensive too.

He and Sabrina walked past her toward the other hotels. The woman noticed him and stepped into their path.

"Wait a moment, you two!" She smiled at Ethan's puzzled look. "You're Trainers, aren't you?"

When he nodded, she went on. "I saw your Charizard land. You've raised it well. Our hotel is perfect for traveling Trainers. We have all sorts of Pokemon training equipment and even dance fitness classes for them. You can stay and train in one place!"

Ethan blinked. "Dance fitness classes just for Pokemon?"

"Exactly!" She grinned. "The dancing gets their blood flowing, loosens their joints, and helps them regulate their breathing. It's a wonderful training method. I bet you haven't tried it. So what are you waiting for? Come stay with us!"

Without waiting for an answer, she waved over a server to look after them, then turned back to recruit more tourists.

Ethan raised an eyebrow. Pokemon dancing sounded remarkably like Rudy's approach at Trovita Gym. The woman had been so enthusiastic, though, that he had not found a moment to refuse. He looked at Sabrina. She gave a slight nod, so they followed the server out of the harbor.

He led them several hundred meters along the coastal road to a high-rise building. The sign identified it as the Kumquat Hotel. Lights shone against its facade in the dark, making it look grand. At more than twenty stories tall, it was substantial enough to support the woman's boast, at least from the outside.

"Please come in." The server bowed slightly and gestured toward the entrance.

Ethan and Sabrina entered. The lobby surprised them at once. More than twenty male attendants stood in two lines and bowed together.

"Welcome!"

While Ethan was still taking in the reception, a man and a woman approached with friendly smiles.

"Welcome. We're the managers here. Would you like to check in?"

Managers greeting guests for an ordinary room? Ethan could hardly believe it. Like the red-haired woman, everyone was almost excessively welcoming. Still, it made a good first impression. Whatever the rooms were like, treating ordinary travelers as honored guests was a pleasant way to start.

At the desk, Ethan booked one room for them both. Sabrina looked surprised. Since her period had begun and they would not be intimate tonight, she had expected him to sleep separately. Ethan simply shrugged at her expression. They cared about each other; sharing a room was hardly only about sex.

He had expected the rate to be high, but the price was unexpectedly reasonable. He had braced himself to be overcharged. Perhaps he had judged the place unfairly.

They took the elevator to the seventeenth floor, entered their room, put away their luggage, and looked through a small booklet the staff had given them. It was a visitor's guide to the island.

Kumquat Island was a well-known tourist destination. Its specialties, Ethan learned, were seafood and hot springs said to be good for the skin. Unfortunately, Sabrina could not use the hot springs while she was on her period, so she would miss one of the island's attractions. He wondered whether she could at least enjoy the seafood. If she had to avoid that too, the trip would have lost both of its local pleasures for her.

The night passed without incident.

The next day, they first visited the dance class. A woman in exercise clothes was teaching a group of Pokemon. The steps looked much like those at Trovita Gym. Ethan quickly lost interest.

After breakfast at the hotel, he asked a server how to reach the Gym. To his surprise, Kumquat Gym was inside the hotel itself.

He had never heard of a Gym built into a hotel. At the very least, the hotel and the Gym Leader must have some sort of partnership, if they were not owned by the same person.

The server led them through the lobby to an arena in a rear hall. So this was Kumquat Gym. It was lavishly equipped, though its lack of a separate entrance or exterior still felt strange to Ethan.

A woman stood on the far Trainer's side of the field. Ethan looked at her and recognized the red-haired woman from the harbor. She must be the Gym Leader.

He sighed to himself. He had forgotten much of the story of the Orange Islands. If he remembered it better, he would have recognized her at once. Then again, even if he had, he would still have had to pretend they were meeting for the first time.

Chapter 241: A Double Battle

The woman put a hand on her hip and looked at Ethan. "Hello again! Sorry, things were so hurried last night that I never introduced myself. I'm Luana. I own this hotel, and I'm also the Leader of Kumquat Gym!"

She owned the hotel? Ethan had assumed she merely worked with it. Was being a Gym Leader really so profitable? A building like this was enough to make him wonder whether he should open a Gym of his own after traveling the world and challenging its Leagues.

"Pardon me." She was watching him, so he put the thought aside and introduced himself. "I'm Ethan, a Trainer from another region. I came specifically to challenge you, Miss Luana."

Luana nodded. Most Trainers who came here wanted a Gym battle. That was no surprise. But her eyes shifted from Ethan to Sabrina. Even though she was a woman who had seen many beauties, Sabrina caught her attention. Her gaze, however, was cold and carried a faint pressure. She did not look easy to approach.

"Such sharp eyes. Are you here to challenge the Gym too, miss?"

Sabrina stood with her arms folded and said nothing. Ethan answered for her. "She's my companion. She's a Trainer as well, but she's only here with me, not to challenge you."

"I see." Luana turned back to him. "How many badges do you have? You haven't come to me for your first one, have you?"

Ethan took his badge case from his bag and opened it. Three badges of different shapes lay inside. "I've beaten the other three Gyms. Kumquat Gym is my final stop."

"Well, well. Three badges already!" Luana sounded impressed. "Beat me and you'll qualify for the Orange League Winner's Cup."

Sabrina considered the name. Ethan had explained the Winner's Cup to her before. Once a Trainer beat all four Gym Leaders, they could challenge the Orange League's strongest Trainer. Defeat that person as well, and they would claim the League's highest honor.

"That's right," Ethan said, thinking back over the past days. "I'm only one step away from the Winner's Cup."

Luana laughed heartily and explained her Gym's rules without further delay. "We each use two Pokemon at the same time. One battle decides it. As soon as either Pokemon on a side goes down, that side loses."

A Double Battle. Ethan had never encountered one in a Gym before. But unlike an ordinary Double Battle, this match would end as soon as a single Pokemon fell. If his two Pokemon focused their attacks on one of Luana's, could they win almost at once?

The rule seemed strange and rather rough to him. Still, it had been in place for some time. Luana must have her reasons, and there was no point debating it now. He would follow it.

"No problem. I'm ready when you are."

A referee came forward and stood between them. "The official badge match between Kumquat Gym Leader Luana and challenger Ethan is about to begin! Both sides, send out your Pokemon!"

This was the most formal Gym battle Ethan had seen since arriving in the Orange Islands. A professional referee presided over it, and both the size of the venue and the field itself were far beyond the previous three Gyms. Each detail must cost money. By comparison, those other facilities seemed decidedly rustic.

His impression of Luana and Kumquat Gym rose sharply.

Footsteps sounded around the hall. Ethan glanced toward the stairways and saw groups of people entering the spectator seats. They were Trainers staying at the hotel. The managers had told them a Gym match was about to start, and they had come to watch.

Because the hotel and Gym shared a building, its guests could observe challenges and learn from them. As far as Ethan knew, no other Pokemon League Gym let unrelated tourists watch a challenger's match in quite this way. It was one of the Kumquat Hotel's distinctive features.

The spectators were considerate. They found seats and watched quietly, making little noise.

Luana's voice drew Ethan's attention back to the field. "Have you decided which Pokemon to use?"

She took two Poke Balls from her pockets, enlarged them in her hands, and threw them onto the battlefield. "These are my partners!"

Two flashes of blue light revealed Alakazam and Marowak. Alakazam was a Psychic type; Marowak was a Ground type. Ethan saw no obvious synergy between them and wondered what Luana had in mind. He did not underestimate her.

He had considered his own pair as soon as he heard the rules. Seeing Luana's Pokemon did not change his plan. Two more flashes of blue light brought Charizard and the Scyther with the scar on its forehead before her.

Two Flying types? Luana narrowed her eyes but could not work out his strategy.

In truth, Ethan had no special tactic behind the choice. He wanted the experienced Charizard to help the newly caught Scyther through its first Gym battle. As it happened, Scyther's Bug typing also gave it an advantage against Alakazam.

With all four Pokemon ready, the referee stepped back and brought down his arm. "The Kumquat Gym challenge begins now!"

Chapter 242: A Change of Tactics

As soon as the match began, Luana's smile disappeared. Her whole bearing changed.

"Alakazam, Marowak! Show them what we can do!" she called sharply.

Alakazam and Marowak rushed toward Charizard and Scyther. One carried spoons and the other a bone club. Both came armed, as though each had an extra weapon from the moment it entered the field.

Ethan would not give ground. He waved his hand, sending Charizard and Scyther forward together.

As the teams closed in, Alakazam stopped first. It raised a spoon toward Charizard and used Confusion. Ethan had watched the opponents closely from the start and saw the movement.

"Charizard, watch Alakazam! Dodge!"

Charizard beat its wings and rose into the air, weaving from side to side to avoid Alakazam's aim.

Meanwhile, Scyther and Marowak collided. Marowak swung its club in a Bone Rush, and Scyther met it with both scythe-like forearms.

Clang! Clang!

Marowak's bone was remarkably hard. Sparks flew where it struck Scyther's blades, and each clash rang like metal on metal.

Alakazam, having lost Charizard as a target, turned on Scyther. A Psychic attack knocked it to the ground. Charizard took advantage of the opening to breathe fire down from above and burn Alakazam.

Both sides had taken damage in their first exchange. Ethan, however, was not pleased. Their Double Battle was turning into an uncontrolled melee. He began adjusting his plan.

"Switch opponents! Scyther, take Alakazam. Charizard, hold off Marowak!"

To his surprise, Scyther ignored him. As soon as it got up, it charged Marowak again.

Ethan sighed. Scyther had once led its group and had a proud streak. He had caught it only recently, and it was not yet used to taking his orders or working with his other Pokemon.

He had spent the past few days talking to it, feeding it, and caring for it. It had begun to follow his instructions, and he had thought they were making progress. This battle showed that the problem remained. Scyther had let its temper take over.

He would have to shift his priority from Alakazam to Marowak. "Scyther, keep fighting Marowak. Charizard, leave Alakazam and help it!"

Luana heard him. She watched Scyther for a moment, then smiled at Ethan. "What, you can't command your own Pokemon?"

"I only caught it recently," Ethan said with a rueful smile. "It was a leader before that. It isn't used to taking orders from someone else. Getting it to listen every time may take a while."

"Oh? You brought a newly caught Pokemon to challenge my Gym?" Luana sounded surprised. "Did you have no one else available, or are you that confident in its strength?"

Ethan shook his head and did not explain. Losing a Gym match would not be the end of anything. The Gym was not going anywhere; a challenger could rest, prepare, and try again. Ash, throughout his journey, had needed a second attempt at many Gyms. Ethan could afford the same if necessary.

While they spoke, all four Pokemon clashed again. For now, Ethan could not rely on Scyther to cooperate. The best he could hope for was that it would beat Marowak by itself. He still had no intention of abandoning it.

"Charizard, Smokescreen!"

Charizard breathed a thick cloud of black smoke toward the ground. It spread across nearly half the field. Marowak and Scyther had been fighting fiercely when the cloud reached them. They looked around for danger, then turned back and found that neither could see the other, even though they stood only a short distance apart.

Outside the smoke, Alakazam held back. It could not attack the cloud at random without risking a hit on Marowak, and it would not enter blindly. It waited for the smoke to clear.

Ethan did not wait. "Charizard, Dragon Rage into the smoke!"

Charizard paused in midair and fired a wave of energy from its mouth. It blew the smoke apart and caught both Marowak and Scyther.

Bang! Bang!

Both Pokemon rocked backward from the hit.

"What...?" Luana stared. Ethan had attacked his own Pokemon along with hers.

He ignored her surprise and spoke coldly to Scyther. "Have you calmed down now?"

Scyther had disobeyed him at a critical moment. He needed to bring it back to its senses. The unexpected hit from behind had stunned it. It turned toward Ethan, the frenzy fading from its eyes, and finally nodded.

"Good." Ethan let out a quiet breath. "Then stop standing there. Switch targets and go after Alakazam!"

Scyther rushed toward Alakazam. Marowak tried to follow and keep its opponent engaged, but Charizard dived between them and blocked its path.

Alakazam used Confusion again, trying to stop Scyther from a distance. At the crucial moment, Scyther burst forward with Quick Attack. It avoided the psychic force and closed in at once.

Both blades gleamed. Scyther raised them high, crossed them, and brought them down hard on Alakazam.

X-Scissor!

Chapter 243: Luana's Request

Bang!

Two flashes crossed, and Scyther's X-Scissor struck Alakazam. Bug-type moves were strong against Psychic types. The hit dealt twice the usual damage.

"Ahh!" Alakazam cried out in pain.

Luana frowned. "Alakazam, get away from there!"

Alakazam looked up to see Scyther raising its blades again. Alarmed, it used Teleport and reappeared on the other side of the field.

It had no time to catch its breath. Scyther had already turned and was rushing at it once more.

Alakazam used Confusion to keep its attacker at a distance, but Scyther repeated its earlier move. Quick Attack carried it around the psychic force and rapidly closed the gap.

Scyther's stubborn fighting spirit had impressed Ethan back in the Bug Pokemon forest. Now Alakazam was learning what that determination felt like from the other side.

Luana grew frustrated as Alakazam struggled. Scyther just would not let it go. She was not about to stand by and watch it lose.

"Alakazam, use Reflect!"

Alakazam raised both hands. A barrier of psychic power formed before it just as Scyther's X-Scissor came down again.

Despite its name, Reflect did not send attacks back at an opponent. It raised a protective wall that weakened physical blows. Scyther's blades passed through the barrier and struck Alakazam. Reflect blunted the attack, but the type advantage still made it hurt.

Luana sighed. Only moments ago, she had mocked Ethan for being unable to command Scyther. After he had hit his own Pokemon, the battle had turned sharply against her. Did Scyther need a beating to make it behave? She could not understand it.

Whatever the reason, she had to find a way to recover. She turned toward her other Pokemon.

"Marowak, help Alakazam!"

Marowak broke away from its fight with Charizard and ran toward Alakazam. It had gone only a few steps when Charizard beat its wings, flew ahead of it, and blocked the way. Its fierce grin made its meaning plain: Marowak was not getting past.

"Marowak, we can't waste time! Use Double-Edge!"

Marowak accelerated and drove its hard skull straight at Charizard. As a Ground-type Pokemon, it was skilled at Ground-type moves, but those would not affect a Flying type like Charizard. Luana had chosen a Normal-type attack instead.

Neither of Luana's Pokemon was in a good position.

Marowak charged without holding back. Charizard gave one light flap and rose into the air, avoiding the blow. Marowak tried to use the opening to continue toward Alakazam, but Charizard swooped down with Wing Attack. Marowak had to dodge.

"Good! Keep it there!" Ethan was more at ease now that Charizard had the upper hand. He called to Scyther, "Finish Alakazam! Use Fury Cutter!"

Scyther swung its arms in a rapid series of five or six slashes. Alakazam evaded two, but the remaining Fury Cutter strikes landed. It could take no more. It fell onto its back, unable to battle.

The referee raised a hand. Under Kumquat Gym's rules, a side lost as soon as one of its Pokemon went down. With Alakazam defeated, the result was clear.

"Alakazam is unable to battle! The winner is the challenger, Ethan!"

At last it was over. Ethan breathed out. He was happy to win, but this match had tired him more than an ordinary Gym battle. In a Double Battle at this level, he had to keep track of two opponents and two of his own Pokemon at once. That took far more concentration than the uneven battles he sometimes found on the street.

Still, he had won. He called Charizard and Scyther back to him, then looked at Alakazam with a little sympathy. For the next few nights, it might have the same nightmare over and over: a scarred Scyther pursuing it with gleaming blades and refusing to stop.

It had lost the battle, and its imagination might go on punishing it. Poor Alakazam.

Luana came over. With the match finished, she had regained the booming manner Ethan had seen when they first met.

"Congratulations!" She seemed entirely untroubled by her loss and gave his shoulder a hearty slap. "You beat me with a Scyther that wouldn't even listen to you. That's impressive!"

Ethan had heard plenty of compliments from Gym Leaders and could answer politely without thinking. Luana's hand was another matter. She had almost knocked the breath out of him.

"Not at all. Your Alakazam and Marowak are well trained. I was lucky to win."

"You're modest too. Here, take your reward. You've earned the Jade Star Badge."

Luana took a small badge from inside her clothes and handed it to him. Sabrina came over as Ethan looked down at it. Like the other Orange League badges, it was made from a shell. This one resembled a gray conch marked with a six-pointed star.

Ethan thanked Luana and started to leave with Sabrina. They had gone only a few steps when she called after them.

"Please wait!"

He turned. "Is there something else?"

Luana hesitated. "Once you've finished your Orange League challenge, are you going to travel to another region?"

"Yes. After I defeat the Supreme Gym Leader here, I plan to go to Johto and challenge its League."

"That's wonderful!" Luana smiled. "In that case, could I ask the two of you for a favor?"

"A favor?" Ethan and Sabrina exchanged a glance. "Tell us what it is. If we can help, of course we will."

Chapter 244: The Final Destination

"It's like this," Luana said, looking a little embarrassed. "My son Travis set out with a Pikachu last year. He's been traveling for nearly a year, but I haven't heard a thing from him. Not one message. I can't help worrying."

Ethan raised an eyebrow. "You want me to find your son?"

"Yes." Luana gave a rueful smile. "I have to run both the Gym and the hotel. I can't spare the time to go looking. If you happen to meet him on your travels, could you please tell him to come home soon?"

No word at all? Had Travis run away, or simply set off on a journey? Ethan thought it over. Whatever the reason, he ought to have sent at least a message or made a call. Something about the family's relationship might have gone wrong, or perhaps Travis had quarreled with them before he left.

"His name is Travis, right? If I meet him, I'll encourage him to come back."

"Thank you. He's stubborn and likes to make up his own mind. If he won't come home, please at least ask him to call me."

"I will, but don't count on me finding him. It's a big world."

Luana did not seem too worried about that. "You're both going to Johto to challenge the Silver League. When the Silver Conference begins, there's a good chance you'll meet."

"He's challenging the Silver League too?" Ethan was surprised. "That is a coincidence. All right, I'll keep an eye out."

He did not promise more. Travis might never enter the Conference, or something might delay him along the way. Ethan did not want to give Luana false hope.

Delighted that he had agreed, she bowed slightly. "I'm counting on you. Thank you!"

...

Ethan and Sabrina left Kumquat Gym, climbed onto Charizard, and headed for their final destination: Pummelo Island.

It was the Orange League's headquarters. Drake, the League's strongest Trainer and the pride of the islands, waited at Pummelo Stadium for challengers.

Charizard carried them through the wind. High above the sea, watching clouds drift past, Ethan said, "Time goes quickly. Before I knew it, our journey through the Orange Islands was almost over."

One arm rested around Sabrina. After sitting so long, he grew bored, and his hand began to wander. Sabrina gave it a light pat. Ethan grinned and behaved himself.

"You sound as though you'll miss it here," she said dryly.

"Of course I will. The weather's warm, the scenery's beautiful, and it's a wonderful place to travel. Most of all, I'd like more time with you."

His sudden, rather corny declaration brought a small smile to Sabrina's face. She said nothing, only leaned back against him.

The Orange Islands held happy memories for her too. Here, she and Ethan had become lovers. They enjoyed the quiet closeness for several minutes before she suddenly looked down at a spot on the sea.

"I think that's Ash down there."

"Ash?" Ethan looked and saw a small dark shape moving over the water. He guided Charizard lower. It was a Lapras carrying Ash and his friends in the direction Ethan and Sabrina had just left.

"Hey, Ash! Misty!" Ethan called.

They stood and looked up at his voice. "Ethan!" Misty waved excitedly.

Once Charizard was level with Lapras, Ethan leaped onto its back. Lapras dipped a little beneath the weight of four passengers.

"Where are you headed?" Ethan asked.

"To Kumquat Island, of course! We're going to challenge Kumquat Gym," Ash said, rubbing his nose.

"So you already have three badges? You've made good time."

Ash could hardly wait to ask, "What about you, Ethan? Have you got your fourth?"

"Of course he has, you idiot," Misty said before Ethan could answer. She rolled her eyes at Ash, her voice full of admiration. "Ethan's much stronger than you. And didn't you see that he came from the direction of Kumquat Island?"

Ash scratched his head and laughed awkwardly. Misty had pointed out the obvious.

"She's right," Ethan said with a wave of his hand. "I've reached the end of the course. Next comes the final boss."

"Aw." Ash sighed. "I hurried as fast as I could, and I'm still a step behind you."

"Don't worry about it. Whether you get there a little earlier or later makes no real difference. It's only a few days."

As they spoke, they noticed Tracey leaning over the side with a glass bottle. He lowered its opening into the sea and filled it.

"What are you doing?" Misty asked.

"Collecting a sample," Tracey said as he screwed on the cap. "I'm a Pokemon Watcher, remember? Comparing seawater and studying the habitat here is part of my work."

Then Ash's Pikachu seemed to spot something. It cried out toward the water beneath them.

They looked down. A huge dark shape was approaching quickly below the surface. Its outline resembled a whale or some other large sea creature. Before anyone could react, it passed beneath Lapras and surfaced ahead of them in a burst of spray.

It was a roughly built submarine.

Chapter 245: A Wave of Magikarp

The submarine's hatch opened. Under everyone's gaze, an elderly man emerged. He had a thick handlebar mustache, small round glasses, and dark skin. He was short and looked at least sixty or seventy, yet he was remarkably lively. His eyes shone with intelligence.

As soon as he climbed out, he produced an assortment of strange little devices. He measured the wind, received a signal with a small satellite dish, and kept himself so busy that he seemed not to notice anything else.

Then he glanced aside and saw the group nearby. "Oh! You young people over there, you'd better be careful!"

The warning came without explanation. Ethan and the others stared at him.

"Careful of what?" Ash asked.

The old man ignored the question. He straightened and spoke to himself. "Wind conditions good. Water temperature acceptable. No change in the current. Solar spectrum normal. They'll be here soon!"

None of it made sense to the others. They looked at one another, puzzled.

"They? Who's coming? Who is this man?" Misty whispered.

"I don't know," Tracey said. "It looks like he's doing scientific research."

Before they could ask more, the sea around them seemed to boil. Bubbles and spray burst from the surface. The waves grew rougher, and tens of thousands of Magikarp suddenly surged up from below.

"M-Magikarp?"

"Where did so many come from?"

Ash and his friends gaped. The Magikarp paid them no attention. Every one of them swam in the same direction, as though following an unseen command.

Only then did Ethan understand the old man's warning. The Magikarp formed a dense red wave. They meant no harm, but anyone who fell into the water now could be battered badly by the sheer mass of fish.

Lapras shivered beneath the group. The sight had frightened it, and it stopped swimming, bobbing with the current. Ash hurried to soothe it.

Ethan steadied himself. Once the first shock passed, he noticed that the old man on the submarine was not surprised at all. He looked excited.

Tracey had kept his attention on the Magikarp. After studying them for a while, he spotted something too. "What are those rings around their tails?"

Ethan looked away from the old man. Many Magikarp had colored bands around the base of their tails. Some were red, some yellow, and some blue. Apart from their colors, the rings looked alike. Who had put them there?

The old man watched through binoculars for a time, then spoke to himself in a voice far more excited than his years suggested. "Targets confirmed! Speed and direction exactly as predicted. Excellent. Emergency dive! The pursuit begins!"

He closed the hatch and started the submarine again, following the Magikarp. They watched it move away until it disappeared from view.

"What was that about?" Misty said. "He rushed in and rushed off. I don't understand any of it!"

Tracey frowned, as though something had come back to him. "Wait. That direction..."

He took a map from his bag and checked the coordinates. His finger stopped on a small island. "That's it! The Magikarp are headed this way, and Rind Island lies right along their route."

"Rind Island?"

The name was new to Ethan, Ash, and Misty. Tracey, who came from the Orange Islands, explained. "As far as I remember, it's small and uninhabited. Hardly anyone knows about it, much less visits. I only heard my grandparents mention it a few times when I was little."

Tens of thousands of Magikarp and a mysterious old man in a submarine had caught Ash's interest. "Something must be happening. Let's go and see!"

Misty and Tracey agreed. They wanted to know what was going on too.

Ethan thought for a moment and decided his own journey could wait. He told Sabrina what they were doing and asked her to follow on Charizard. He stayed on Lapras with Ash and the others as they headed toward Rind Island.

Spray flew around them. Lapras avoided the Magikarp and fought to keep its balance in the disturbed water. On its back, Ash and his friends argued excitedly over why so many Magikarp had gathered. Their guesses grew less convincing by the minute.

Ethan listened without joining in. He had known that meeting Ash made it likely he would stumble into another adventure. It seemed to have happened again.

He did not mind. To Ethan, travel was made of unfamiliar experiences. Taking part in strange events was how people tested themselves and grew.

The constant splashing interrupted his thoughts. Magikarp were hardly impressive in an ordinary battle, but in the sea they could swim at a respectable speed. Even carrying so many passengers, Lapras worked hard to keep up. One by one, the Magikarp overtook it until they were following at the rear of the vast school.

Fortunately, after ten or fifteen minutes they could already see Rind Island in the distance. They would not lose their way now.

Chapter 246: Swimming Upstream

From a distance, Rind Island looked covered in forest. Cliffs of varying heights ringed its shores, forming a natural barrier between its interior and the open sea. A low mountain rose in the middle.

Tracey had been right: it appeared to be uninhabited. At least from outside, Ethan could see no sign of human activity.

The Magikarp did not swim past the island. They poured into a river mouth and headed inland. Ethan looked around and spotted the submarine they had seen earlier, moored in a sheltered bay. Its hatch was closed and it sat motionless. The old man must have gone into the island after the Magikarp.

"What now?" Ash asked.

"What else?" As Lapras approached the shore, Ethan was the first to jump off. "We've come this far. Let's go in and see."

Once everyone had landed, Ash recalled Lapras. Sabrina brought Charizard down and joined Ethan. The group set out on foot along the riverbank.

The river was packed with Magikarp as far as they could see. The fish fought their way upstream, their red bodies filling the water in a spectacular procession.

"Wow. You don't see a sight like this every day," Misty said over the constant splashing.

Ethan nodded. It reminded him of stories from his old home about fish returning from the sea each year to lay their eggs farther inland. Perhaps the Pokemon here had a similar habit. He took out his phone and recorded the scene.

While they watched the river, the old man's voice came from behind them. "What are you young people standing around for?"

They turned. The submarine's pilot was approaching with more than a dozen boxes and bags piled on his back. They had been following his trail, yet somehow he had come up behind them. His luggage stood higher than he did. How could such a small old man carry it all and still move so easily?

Seeing them stare, he began distributing the boxes among them without asking. In a moment, even Sabrina and Pikachu had a small box apiece.

Ethan and the others were caught off guard. The stranger had evidently decided they were free porters.

"Hey, sir, what are you doing?" Ash asked, holding a large box in his arms.

The old man did not answer. Now carrying only a small backpack, he strolled along the river toward the forest. After a few steps, he noticed they had not followed and beckoned them on.

"Come along. Don't just stand there!"

Come along to where? They exchanged baffled looks, then followed him.

"Wait a minute! Will you tell us what you're doing?" Ash called.

The old man did not even slow down. Misty looked down at her box and sighed helplessly. "What is going on?"

"Let's see where he's taking us," Ethan said. "We'll probably understand when we get there."

He glanced at Sabrina. She still held her box and looked calm. In a low voice, he asked, "You're not angry, are you?"

She shook her head. In the past, she would have used her psychic powers to send a stranger this rude flying through the air. Now such a small imposition hardly mattered to her.

They continued beside the river, deeper into the forest. The ground rose from a gentle slope to a steep climb. They were ascending the mountain they had seen from the sea.

The change in height made the current stronger, yet the Magikarp kept swimming against it toward higher ground. Ethan was beginning to understand. If they were not going to spawn, something else must be drawing them.

After several more minutes, Misty's curiosity overcame her. She hurried up beside the old man. "Who are you, sir?"

He looked at her friendly face and smiled. "I'm Quincy T. Quackenpoker, a Pokemon Watcher."

A Pokemon Watcher? The others all looked at Tracey. He shook his head. Despite sharing the profession, he had never heard Quackenpoker's name.

Misty lifted the box in her hands. "What's in all these?"

"Isn't it obvious? Equipment for observing Magikarp!"

"Equipment to observe Magikarp?"

"Of course." Perhaps Misty reminded him of his granddaughter, for he explained a little more. "I prepared every piece especially for watching these Magikarp."

Tracey's interest flared. He hurried to the river's edge and pulled out his sketchbook. "So I was right! These Magikarp must have some special ability or behavior. I'll observe them too."

He had stepped too close. A Magikarp leaped from the water, startling him and splashing him in the face. His enthusiasm cooled at once.

"Foolish boy," the old man snorted. "Those are ordinary Magikarp. There's nothing worth observing about them."

"Huh?" Tracey asked, ignoring the water on his clothes. "Didn't you just say all this equipment was for Magikarp? If they're ordinary, why bring it?"

Chapter 247: The Lake of Birth and Evolution

Quackenpoker did not explain further. He checked the time, frowned at how much had passed, and said, "We can't waste any more time here. You'll understand when we arrive. Come on, hurry."

He turned and quickened his pace toward the summit.

After spending a little time with him, Ethan and the others knew he meant no harm. He was simply an eccentric researcher who paid little attention to ordinary manners. They still had plenty of questions, but with Quackenpoker walking away, they followed.

Beside them, the Magikarp continued swimming tirelessly against the current. Soon the school reached a cluster of three small waterfalls. They tried to climb, but the water rushed straight down. Even at full effort, many Magikarp were swept off partway up. Only a few thrashed hard enough to overcome the force and reach the water above.

Ethan stopped again. For the Magikarp, this was an enormous obstacle. He could hardly believe he was seeing such a sight. They were working too hard for this to look like a simple spawning migration. It reminded him of the old tale of carp leaping the Dragon Gate.

In this world, though, a Magikarp that made it over the falls would not become a dragon soaring through the clouds. It would become a fierce and terrifying Gyarados.

He took out his phone to film. In the short time he watched, hundreds of Magikarp failed, exhausted, and were washed downstream. The others kept coming, one wave after another, throwing themselves at the falls.

Tracey turned to watch too. "Mr. Quackenpoker, what's at the top that makes them work so hard?"

"Only an ordinary lake," Quackenpoker answered without stopping. "It doesn't look like much. I call it the Lake of Birth and Evolution."

"The Lake of Birth and Evolution?" the others repeated.

"That's where these Magikarp were born. Every year at this time, they come back to complete their evolution."

Evolution? Ethan stared. An ordinary Magikarp did not evolve easily. It often required a Trainer's careful raising and training. Was Quackenpoker saying that a Magikarp only had to return to this lake to evolve?

If that was true, the place would seem magical to anyone raising Magikarp. Ethan thought like a Trainer, though, and his interests were not quite the same as everyone else's. Tracey, as a Pokemon Watcher, saw a different question.

"Mr. Quackenpoker, how many Magikarp make it up each year?" he asked eagerly.

Quackenpoker gave him a thoughtful look, glanced back at the falls, and shook his head. "It varies. From what I've seen in past years, at most one in ten gets over the waterfalls and reaches the lake."

Only one in ten? The number surprised them, though the force of the water made it understandable. Given the size of the school, that could still mean nearly a thousand Magikarp reached the summit.

"Do all the ones that reach the lake evolve?" Ash asked, astonished.

"Don't be ridiculous." Quackenpoker rolled his eyes, amused and annoyed at once. "Getting there doesn't guarantee anything. Only one in ten of those Magikarp evolves into Gyarados."

One tenth of one tenth. That would still produce around a hundred new Gyarados, hardly a small number.

Ash scratched his head, about to ask more. Quackenpoker waved him off. "Enough questions. If we don't move, we'll miss their mass evolution!"

Ethan and the others fell silent. Now that they had come this far, they wanted to see how the Magikarp evolved together. They hurried on.

None of them noticed a clump of bushes moving shortly after they left.

"Hey, did you hear all that?" a voice asked from inside. It sounded almost human, though a little raspy. Team Rocket's trio poked their heads out.

"Of course I did!" James's eyes widened with excitement. "A lake of evolution! What an amazing discovery!"

"Idiot," Jessie said, folding her arms and giving him a sidelong look. "It's an opportunity."

"Oh? How?"

Jessie lifted her chin. "If it's a lake of evolution, we catch enough Magikarp and throw them in. Then we'll have lots of powerful Gyarados! With those, we can build a magnificent, earthshaking, unbeatable, greatest-ever Gyarados army!"

"A Gyarados army?" James pictured himself commanding a whole force of them. "The Boss would finally take notice of us! We could even hold our heads high in front of that woman with the watermelon-rind glasses!"

"Exactly!" Jessie clenched her fists, eyes blazing. "With a Gyarados army, Team Rocket will be unstoppable. No one will stand a chance against us!"

"But..." James hesitated. "Will it really be that easy? Gyarados and we have never gotten along."

"Don't worry. I've thought of that," Jessie said with a confident laugh. "We catch them while they're still Magikarp. Then they can't disobey us after they evolve!"

Chapter 248: The Magikarp's Home Island

Ethan and the others followed the river up through the forest. After another ten minutes or more, they finally reached the summit of Rind Island's mountain.

The ground leveled out. Dense forest surrounded a broad, clear lake in the center, and water spilled from it toward the slopes below.

"Here we are. The Lake of Birth and Evolution!"

Quackenpoker set down his backpack. Only a few Magikarp were swimming around the lake, and the mass evolution had not begun. Relieved that they had made it in time, he relaxed.

Misty dropped onto a large rock with a long breath. She had climbed all this way carrying a heavy box. At last she could rest.

Everyone looked at the lake. As Quackenpoker had said, it appeared perfectly ordinary. Had they come upon it by themselves, they would never have suspected that anything remarkable happened here.

Sabrina glanced around. "It's strange. Why can't I see any other Pokemon?" she asked Ethan quietly.

He noticed the silence then. A forest this large ought to have wild Pokemon, and a lake should attract other Water types. Yet the entire summit was quiet.

"Maybe the Magikarp migration disturbed them," he suggested. "They might have moved away or hidden until it's over."

Sabrina nodded without committing herself. They had too little information to be sure.

Quackenpoker was already taking equipment from the boxes and setting it up beside the lake. There were devices shaped like cameras, a laptop, and something resembling a satellite receiver. Ethan and his friends gathered around. None of them knew enough to identify every instrument from its appearance.

"Mr. Quackenpoker," Tracey asked politely, "what do these machines do?"

Quackenpoker seemed pleased by the question. With no sign of evolution yet, he had time to answer. "They automatically analyze all sorts of data on the Magikarp: weight, growth patterns, bone density, muscle fatigue, and more. They're powerful tools."

Ten-year-old Ash was lost among the technical terms. He scratched his head. "That's amazing. I don't understand what you said, but it sounds impressive!"

Quackenpoker laughed. Praise from a child pleased him.

Splash! Splash!

Several red shapes broke the surface as more Magikarp came up the river and entered the lake. The group watched until the water settled. Then Ethan turned back to the old man, who handled his equipment with practiced ease.

"How long have you been a Pokemon Watcher?"

Quackenpoker thought for a moment. "I've forgotten the exact number of years. It must be fifty by now."

"Fifty?" Misty stuck out her tongue in amazement. To stay with a profession for fifty years took dedication. Even if he had started at fifteen or sixteen, as Tracey had, he would be in his sixties or older now.

It seemed hard to be out in the sun and wind at that age. What if he died on some deserted island? Misty caught herself and hurriedly shook away the disrespectful thought.

Quackenpoker was busy with his instruments and noticed nothing. Even if he had, his manner suggested he would have ignored it.

Tracey admired the weathered veteran. "That's incredible. How long have you been observing Magikarp?"

"Not long. Only three years," Quackenpoker replied lightly, as though the time were nothing more than a number.

Ethan looked at him with fresh respect. At Quackenpoker's age, many people would choose to stay home and enjoy their families' company. Few would still travel to remote islands to study Pokemon. A person's life held only so many years, and he had devoted three of them to these Magikarp. His love of Pokemon was plain.

Misty trained Water-type Pokemon, but even she could not imagine giving Magikarp that much attention. She admired him while still wondering whether such an ordinary species really needed three years of study.

Tracey remembered the colored rings. "What about the bands we saw on some of their tails?"

"I put them there," Quackenpoker said. "They identify individuals by the year they left the island. Red means one year ago, blue means two, and yellow means three."

"All these Magikarp were born here?"

"That's right. Magikarp born on this island swim out to sea as they grow. No matter how far they travel, they return at this time each year. Rind Island is their home."

Ethan had never known Magikarp could have a home island. They lived all over the world, though, so perhaps this migratory behavior belonged only to the ones in the Orange Islands.

The light of sunset fell across the group. More Magikarp were gathering in the lake, but none had begun to evolve.

"This isn't good," Quackenpoker said, checking the time again. "There are fewer Magikarp than I expected. It looks as though we'll have to camp here tonight."

Chapter 249: Camping by the Lake

"Mr. Quackenpoker, does the number of Magikarp affect when they evolve?" Tracey asked.

"It does," Quackenpoker said. "There's a mysterious power in this lake. The more Magikarp gather, the more likely they are to trigger it and evolve together. With too few, it can take a long time, perhaps until tomorrow."

"I see."

The sun was sinking toward the horizon. Since they would have to spend the night outdoors, everyone set to work before dark.

Quackenpoker continued operating his equipment and observing the Magikarp. Ash and Misty gathered firewood and lit a campfire. Tracey brought out the cooking gear and took charge of dinner. Ethan and Sabrina pitched their own tent and Quackenpoker's.

Tracey made curry rice with a fruit salad. The Pokemon gathered the fruit in the nearby forest; Ash and his friends had brought the curry, potatoes, rice, and other ingredients themselves. They camped and cooked far more often than Ethan, who tended to stay in hotels or eat at restaurants, so they traveled prepared.

"This is delicious!" Quackenpoker said after tasting the fragrant curry. The mild spice surprised him. "What luck! I was going to make do with instant noodles. I never expected a meal like this on an uninhabited island."

Across the folding table, Ethan took a generous spoonful of rice covered in sauce. It really was good, appealing in taste, smell, and appearance. Tracey was a capable cook, though still not quite as good as Brock, who usually handled Ash's meals.

They talked as they ate. The pleasant dinner stretched beyond an hour, and the moon was high by the time they finished. Misty brewed a pot of hot tea, and everyone gathered around the campfire.

Quackenpoker held his warm cup and looked at Tracey. "Young man, you're a Pokemon Watcher too, aren't you?"

"Huh? How did you know?"

"Never underestimate an experienced Watcher's eyes." Quackenpoker blew aside the tea leaves floating in his cup, looking pleased with himself. "I saw your sketchbook and binoculars down by the river. You look at things the way a Watcher does."

"So that's how." Tracey had never tried to hide his profession, and he did not mind Quackenpoker recognizing it.

After an evening together, the old man's manner toward all of them had softened. He smiled kindly and held out his hand. "May I look at your observation notes?"

"Of course." Tracey gave him his sketchbook and the notes he carried with it.

Quackenpoker read through them carefully. At last he nodded in approval. "Very good. You've studied all kinds of Pokemon, and your observations are detailed. Keep at it. I believe you'll become an excellent Pokemon Watcher."

The two discussed their knowledge and experiences. Tracey learned a great deal from the older Watcher. Quackenpoker did not leave Ethan and the others out. Over tea, he told them stories of unusual events from his travels. He had visited regions all over the world, and Ash and his friends listened in amazement to adventures they could hardly imagine.

More than a hundred Magikarp entered the lake during that time, but there were still too few to trigger the mass evolution.

After ten o'clock, everyone grew sleepy. Ethan and Tracey began discussing who would keep watch, but Quackenpoker refused before they could settle it.

"I'll stay up alone," he said firmly. "To be honest, I wouldn't feel comfortable leaving the Magikarp measurements to you."

He was blunt, but they understood. He had studied these Magikarp for three years and waited a long time for tonight. They might evolve at any moment. He wanted to observe them himself rather than entrust the crucial hours to anyone else.

Tracey first tried to persuade him to rest for his health. Quackenpoker would not change his mind, so the others let him sit beside the fire through the night. Ethan understood him. At a moment this important, he too would want to see it with his own eyes.

Ethan and Sabrina washed up by the river, then went into their tent. Ash and Tracey spread their sleeping bags at a comfortable distance from the fire and climbed in.
