Chikorita's body was pale green, with a dark green leaf on its head and a ring of buds around its neck. Its head was large in proportion to the rest of its body, and it had red eyes, four short legs, a single nailed toe on each foot, and a small tail.

It was the first Chikorita Ethan had seen in person. Curious, he took out his Pokedex and pointed it toward the Pokemon.

"Beep! Chikorita, the Leaf Pokemon. A Grass type. Gentle by nature, Chikorita loves basking in sunlight. It uses the leaf on its head to sense the temperature and humidity around it and find a warm place. Though it may wave its leaf to threaten an opponent, the sweet scent it releases calms them both down."

As one of Johto's three starter Pokemon, Chikorita was certainly popular. Ethan, however, had little interest in raising one.

The Sentret had already taken several hits and was running low on strength. It was searching for a way to escape when it caught sight of Ethan and Sabrina. Its eyes brightened, and it ran straight toward them without hesitation.

"Chikorita, don't let it get away! Use Tackle!" the girl shouted.

Chikorita prepared to charge, but Sentret had already darted behind Ethan and Sabrina. The little Pokemon hesitated and stopped.

That moment was enough. Using the two travelers for cover, Sentret plunged into the forest beside them and vanished.

"Ah! My second Pokemon!" The girl dropped to the ground like a deflated balloon, watching her nearly captured Sentret get away.

Ethan glanced back toward the place it had disappeared. What a clever Sentret. It had even known how to use them as a shield.

He shook his head and started to leave. He had taken only a few steps when the girl shouted behind him, "Hey! You two, don't go!"

She had scrambled back to her feet and came trotting over. Ethan turned to face her.

"What is it?"

"You have the nerve to ask?" she said angrily. "If you hadn't suddenly shown up, that Sentret never would have escaped!"

Her voice was clear, with a strong Kansai accent.

"That's my fault?" Ethan shrugged. "You're the one who couldn't catch it. If you'd knocked it out sooner, it wouldn't have had a chance to run away."

"I don't care! You got in my way!" She waved her baseball bat around, fierce in a way that was more cute than threatening.

Sabrina frowned at the girl's unreasonable behavior and was about to teach her a lesson. Ethan hurriedly caught her hand before the young Trainer could get hurt.

After a second look, he had recognized the girl from her clothes. She had appeared several times in the animated series, and he remembered her fairly well. That was one reason he was not angry. He was a grown man; there was no point in quarreling with a little girl.

He considered her for a moment, then smiled. "What would you like us to do about it?"

His smile made the girl's cheeks turn red instead.

Wow, he's handsome. And he looks even better when he smiles!

She had not meant any harm. She was frustrated that a Pokemon she had almost caught had escaped, and seeing Ethan simply walk away had annoyed her. She had rushed over without thinking. Now that he was speaking to her so politely, she felt rather embarrassed.

Seeing her stumble over her words, Ethan said, "You look like a new Trainer. Maybe that Sentret just wasn't meant to be yours. If you still want it, you could try your luck in the forest and see whether you find it again. Or you could look for a different Pokemon to catch."

"I... I'm sorry. I got carried away."

She lowered her head and fiddled with her baseball bat as though she intended to examine every inch of it.

"It's all right." Ethan waved it off. When she remained silent, he took that as the end of it. "Good luck with your catching. See you!"

He and Sabrina turned and walked away without looking back.

"Oh? G-goodbye..."

The girl answered automatically. She watched Ethan go until he disappeared. Only then did it occur to her what she had missed, and she smacked herself on the head.

"You big idiot!"

At her feet, Chikorita looked up in curiosity, its little head full of confusion. Before it could work out what its Trainer meant, the girl sighed again.

"I didn't even ask his name. Oh well. We'll probably meet again. I'll ask him next time!"

Chapter 282: Silver

New Bark Town: the town where the winds of a new beginning blow.

If Kanto's starting town, Pallet Town, was a cozy little place, New Bark Town was practically a small city by comparison. In the games it had been a remote town with few residents. Now that Ethan had set foot here, he found it far larger and more prosperous than Pallet Town.

It had tall buildings as well as shopping centers, schools, a hospital, and even an amusement park. The Johto Pokemon League had built a Pokemon Center here especially for new Trainers. Yet, like Cherrygrove City, New Bark Town was quiet. Few people were out on the streets, and those who were moved at an unhurried pace.

As Ethan and Sabrina walked toward the town center, it was clear that she was still thinking about the girl they had just met.

She glanced at him. "I've noticed you're always especially friendly to cute girls. Misty, Chloe at Cerise Laboratory, Melody in the Orange Islands, and now that girl. You don't have some peculiar preference, do you?"

The hairs on Ethan's arms stood up.

Danger. Danger. Danger!

"Hey, don't say things like that!" His heart hammered as he hurried to deny it. "They're my friends, or like little sisters to me. I've never had any improper thoughts about them. And didn't you see how well I treated Ash? I like helping younger Trainers. How does that give me a peculiar preference?"

Sabrina studied him for a long moment. "Really? I was only making an observation. Why do you look so nervous?"

"I'm not nervous!" He wiped the cold sweat from his forehead, trying to look unconcerned. "It's just a dangerous question. If someone overheard you, they might think I was a bad person!"

Fortunately, Sabrina did not press him. Ethan breathed a little easier.

Hadn't she asked something similar before? Had his behavior lately been too brazen? Had Sabrina noticed something again? A psychic girl's sixth sense was a frightening thing.

To keep her from asking more, Ethan quickly took out his phone and contacted Looker again. They agreed to meet at a coffee shop, which Ethan found easily with the map on his phone.

It was a small shop on a street corner. Evening was approaching, but it was not yet dinnertime, and hardly anyone was inside. As soon as Ethan entered, he spotted Looker skulking in a corner.

Looker wore his usual trench coat, but he had added sunglasses and a wide-brimmed hat. If he had not been the only customer, and if his build had not been so familiar, Ethan might not have recognized him.

Ethan dropped into the seat opposite him. "Long time no see. What is this? Is someone following you?"

"Keep your voice down!" Looker removed his glasses, revealing a face so haggard that he looked as though he'd been overindulging for weeks. He glanced toward the server and put a finger to his lips. "No one's following me. I'm following someone else."

"Following someone?" Ethan looked him up and down. "Come on. Dressed like that, you'd stand out in any crowd. They'll notice you at once!"

"I don't have much choice." Looker spread his hands. "Team Rocket has circulated my picture everywhere. Practically all of them know my face. Without a disguise, they'd recognize me even faster."

Looker noticed Sabrina and nodded in greeting. Before Ethan could say anything else, he raised his eyebrows at him with a leering expression.

Well done, kid. You really did win Sabrina over!

Ethan had no trouble reading him. He glanced at Sabrina, then answered with a look and a lift of his own eyebrows.

What can I say? I'm irresistible.

Oh, please. When did you get so shameless?

What would you know, you lonely old bachelor?

It was the kind of wordless understanding men shared. Sabrina could not possibly follow their exchange. She stared at them, puzzled by all the eyebrow waggling. Both looked rather indecent, and she was sure they were discussing something offensive.

Her expression grew more suspicious, and her gaze more dangerous. Ethan hastily cleared his throat and turned to Looker with a serious face.

"Back to business. Who exactly are you following here?"

Looker pointed through the window. "Look over there."

Across the street, inside a restaurant, a man was eating dinner alone with his back to the coffee shop. He wore a tight black top and dark jeans. Ethan could not identify him from behind, but one thing stood out: his medium-length red hair.

"Who is he?"

"Giovanni's son," Looker said quietly.

"Giovanni's son?"

Sabrina froze. She and Giovanni had been Gym Leaders for years, yet this was the first she had heard that he had a son. Ethan frowned too. Did Looker mean Silver?

So that was why Looker had been so secretive, and why he had traveled all the way to New Bark Town: he was tailing Silver.

"Surprised?" Looker asked when he saw their faces. "We only found out recently that Giovanni has a son. The sly old man hid him well."

Ethan tried a question. "So you're following him in the hope that he'll lead you to Team Rocket?"

Looker nodded. "His name is Silver. I intend to use him to find Giovanni's hiding place. And I suspect he has a scheme of his own for coming here."

Ethan sighed softly. Looker was bound to be disappointed. Silver did not get along well with Giovanni. Though he was Giovanni's son, he disapproved of his father's withdrawal and hated what Team Rocket did. He wanted to make his own way, had almost nothing to do with the organization, and generally acted alone.

Ethan could hardly tell Looker any of that. Besides, he wanted to see what Silver was doing in New Bark Town himself.

Chapter 283: Following Silver

Ethan doubted Silver would leave the restaurant immediately, so he waved over the server and ordered a quick meal for himself and Sabrina, with two coffees.

The server soon brought several sandwiches with different fillings, along with vegetable salad, fries, and chicken nuggets.

While they ate, Looker gave them a general account of the operation the International Police were about to carry out.

He spoke openly, apparently unworried that Ethan or Sabrina might leak his plans. The two had been a great help in Kanto, after all. The International Police could not clear out Team Rocket with their own forces alone. They needed the Pokemon League and Trainers like Ethan to help them.

It took four sandwiches to fill Ethan up. Just as he reached for a fifth, he saw Silver rise from his table across the street and leave the restaurant.

To Looker's amazement, Ethan demonstrated the rare skill of eating a sandwich in three bites. He stuffed the rest into his mouth, hurried to pay with his cheeks bulging, and left the coffee shop with Sabrina and Looker.

The evening air was cool at this time of year. A breeze passed over them, raising goose bumps on their skin. The streetlights were already on, but there were fewer and fewer pedestrians.

Silver walked northwest through town at an easy pace, seemingly unaware that anyone was following him.

As Looker made to slip after him, Ethan caught his arm.

"No. It's dark, but the streets are nearly empty. If we stay close enough to follow him, he'll spot us."

"I know," Looker said with a helpless shrug. "But if we wait, he'll be out of sight. I came in a hurry, and there are no other International Police officers nearby to work with me. I'll have to take the risk."

In Ethan's old world, they would probably use some piece of surveillance technology. Here, though...

"We have another option." Ethan smiled and looked down at his shadow. "This is exactly when you ask a Pokemon for help. Gengar, you heard us, right? I'm counting on you."

Gengar's head rose out of the shadow. It gave a wicked chuckle. "Of course. Shall I abduct him, or wait until no one's around and kill him?"

Ethan stared at it.

"Please..." He sighed, exhausted already. "Just follow him quietly and see where he's going and what he's doing. Do you have to make it sound so horrifying?"

Ethan could have sent Charizard to watch from above, or asked Sabrina to keep track of Silver with her psychic powers. After considering his options, he had decided Gengar was the best choice.

"Ha! I know," Gengar said with a laugh. "I was only joking to lighten the mood!"

"Who feels like joking now?" Ethan shook his head. "Leave markers along the way so we can find you. Above all, don't let him notice you, and don't play any pranks on your own initiative."

Gengar patted its chest. "Leave it to me!"

Before their eyes, its body slowly turned transparent, as though it were vanishing into thin air. Soon they could not see it at all. A light breeze brushed past them, and they knew Gengar had silently gone after Silver.

Looker watched the direction they had gone with envy. "You have so many powerful, dependable Pokemon. That's impressive."

"Why don't you catch a few more yourself?" Ethan asked. "I think you only have a Growlithe and a Croagunk. Isn't that dangerous for an International Police officer?"

"I can't manage it," Looker said with a smile. "My assignments keep me busy every day. I don't have time to catch more Pokemon, much less raise and care for them. I may have only two, but both are partners I can count on. They're enough for me."

The three waited until Silver was out of sight, then slowly followed the route he had taken. They saw neither Silver nor Gengar along the way, but every few dozen meters a clear arrow appeared in a conspicuous place, keeping them on course.

The sky darkened as they crossed most of the town. At last, they reached a large walled property.

Inside stood an imposing three-story building with a round tower. It looked like some kind of research facility.

Looker brightened, as though his suspicions had been confirmed. "Professor Elm's laboratory! I knew it. This is Team Rocket's target!"

He scanned the area, but no matter how carefully he looked, he could not find any other Team Rocket members.

Professor Elm... Ethan rubbed his chin, trying to recall what he knew. Elm was Johto's Pokemon professor and provided starter Pokemon to new Trainers. He also seemed to have studied under Professor Oak. Beyond that, Ethan remembered little.

Gengar's head appeared above the courtyard wall. It beckoned to Ethan and whispered, "Over here! That Silver fellow went into the grounds!"

With no one else nearby, Ethan asked Sabrina to stay outside and watch their backs. Then he and Looker climbed over the wall.

As soon as they landed, they saw Silver crouched among some distant bushes, staring fixedly at the three-story building. They hurried behind a large tree before he could spot them.

Silver clearly had no idea that someone was watching the watcher. He would never have guessed that his every move was under observation.

The grounds remained quiet. After watching for some time, Looker finally voiced his confusion. "How strange. Why is Silver alone? Where are the other Team Rocket members?"

Ethan was silent for a moment. "Perhaps there aren't any. I think he's acting on his own this time."

"On his own?"

Looker's face fell. If that was true, he had spent all this effort for nothing.

Chapter 284: The Arrest

Silver had clearly studied the grounds and Professor Elm's laboratory before coming here.

From a distance, Ethan and Looker watched him check both sides to make sure no one was around. He hurried to the building, forced open a window, and climbed inside. The whole maneuver was so smooth and efficient that Ethan could not help admiring it.

He exchanged a glance with Looker. They crept to the window and peered in, showing only the tops of their heads. Inside a research room full of delicate instruments, Silver took one of three Poke Balls lined up on a machine and slipped it into a pouch at his waist. Then he carefully returned the way he had come.

Ethan and Looker hurried back behind the tree before he emerged.

Silver did not bother hiding his tracks. He left the window open and walked straight out of Professor Elm's grounds without a pause.

Ethan turned to Looker and whispered, "What do you want to do? Should I stop him?"

Looker had frowned through the entire theft. Lost in thought, he did not answer for a long while.

Was that really it? How disappointing!

Given Team Rocket's usual methods, and the fact that Silver was Giovanni's son, Looker had assumed he must be the organization's second most important figure. He had imagined Silver arriving with Team Rocket members to carry out a spectacular raid. At the very least, he had expected them to strip the whole laboratory of Pokemon and research material.

Instead, after all the effort of following Silver here, he had witnessed a simple theft. Just one Poke Ball! When had Team Rocket become so unambitious?

You're the son of Giovanni, the Earth Gym Leader. Where are your standards? Your pride? How is this any different from the work of a petty thief?

"Let's wait," Looker said at last. He still could not let go of his theory. "I feel there's more to this. Follow him and see where he goes."

Ethan signaled for Gengar to keep tracking Silver. He and Looker went back over the wall and met Sabrina outside.

Silver walked directly out of New Bark Town toward Cherrygrove City. They followed for a long time. The moon climbed high, and stars filled the sky. When they were nearing the forest where Ethan had met the girl with the baseball bat, Looker finally ran out of patience.

"We can't let him keep going," he said with a sigh. "I read too much into it. He didn't come here to lead some major Team Rocket operation. He came to steal a Pokemon from Professor Elm, plain and simple."

Ethan looked at him. "So what now?"

Looker thought it over. "We have two choices. We can leave him alone and keep following him. Perhaps he'll eventually lead us to Team Rocket's hideout. But that could take a great deal of time and effort, and if he has other things to do, we'll be following him forever."

"The odds of that working are slim," Ethan agreed.

"Then we take the second option. Arrest him and question him. That should tell us everything we need to know."

Ethan weighed the choices and nodded. "All right. We have a Team Rocket member in front of us. No reason to let him walk away. Let's move."

They whispered over a plan. Ethan hurried ahead through the forest and caught up with Silver beside a lake, blocking his path.

"Who are you?" Silver demanded with a frown.

Before Ethan could answer, footsteps sounded behind him. Silver turned and saw a man and a woman approaching from either side. The three had him surrounded. They had plainly come for him, and their intentions were anything but friendly.

Looker held up his identification. "Silver, I'm with the International Police. You're under arrest. Come with us quietly. Don't resist and get yourself hurt."

"The International Police?" Silver did not look frightened. He sneered. "What crime have I committed that calls for you to arrest me?"

"If we're watching you, you can assume we know where you come from," Looker said. "You just left Professor Elm's laboratory. Must I tell you what you did inside?"

Damn it. When did they notice me?

They knew exactly what he had done at the laboratory. Silver realized they must have been following him for some time. If he wanted to get away, he would probably have to fight.

He took out the Poke Ball he had stolen and spun it around one finger.

"Fine. The Poke Ball is right here. If you want to take me in, let's see whether you can manage it!"

He had scarcely finished speaking when the Poke Ball lifted itself from his finger. It shot toward Sabrina, who caught it neatly in her hand.

"What?"

Silver stared at her in shock. Then recognition seemed to strike him. His pupils contracted as he looked her over.

"It's you! The woman with psychic powers!" His face paled, and he turned to Ethan. "Then you must be Ethan Vale. People in the organization keep talking about a man and a woman causing trouble for us everywhere. I never thought I'd run into you!"

A few ordinary International Police officers would not have worried him. This pair was different. They had made a fearsome reputation within Team Rocket. Elite units and even the four Executives had fallen to them. For ordinary members, merely hearing their names was enough to make their skin crawl.

What rotten luck. Why did I have to meet them?

Silver dropped any thought of underestimating his opponents. He took several Poke Balls from his belt. Three flashes of blue light followed, and three Pokemon appeared before them.

Ethan looked from one to the next: Sneasel, Zubat, and Gastly.

They were already good Pokemon. Why had Silver bothered stealing a starter from Professor Elm's laboratory?

Chapter 285: Suicune

"Just as I thought." Looker raised a hand as Silver refused to surrender. He released Growlithe and Croagunk. "I knew you wouldn't come quietly. Don't blame us when we use force to arrest you!"

Ethan was only helping Looker, not leading the arrest. As the battle began, he sent out Gardevoir to join Gengar, which was already beside him. The two guarded his side in case Silver tried to escape past him.

On the opposite side, Sabrina released Alakazam and the Slowking she had caught in the Orange Islands.

"Growlithe, Flamethrower! Croagunk, Sludge Bomb!"

Silver shouted his own orders. "Sneasel, Icy Wind! Zubat, Air Cutter! Gastly, Shadow Ball!"

Five Pokemon clashed. Attacks flew through the air, and cries of anger and pain echoed over the forest. Wild Pokemon that had been peering out from cover scattered in fright.

Silver had three Pokemon to Looker's two, giving him a slight numerical advantage. But with Ethan's and Sabrina's Pokemon guarding the sides, Looker did not have to hold back. He pressed Silver from the start.

Boom! Boom!

Explosions followed one another. Thick trees toppled under the continuing attacks.

Ethan watched for a while. When Zubat had just finished an attack, he told Gardevoir to use Confusion. Caught off guard, Zubat lost control of its flight and slammed into a rock.

With one opponent disrupted, Looker had less to contend with. His Pokemon drove Silver's back step by step.

Tch. Silver grew increasingly irritated. The International Police officer was difficult enough on his own. The other two were watching every escape route.

He could not win by staying in this fight. It was time to get away.

Gritting his teeth, Silver ordered all three Pokemon to attack together. They forced Looker back for a moment, then turned and charged toward a gap beside Ethan.

Ethan saw what he intended. He waved a hand. Gengar and Gardevoir struck together, and the difference in power sent Silver's Pokemon back.

"Damn it!"

Silver changed direction and tried Sabrina's side. Team Rocket's stories said she was formidable, but to him she was still a woman. Even with psychic abilities, surely she would be easier to overcome than a Psychic-type Pokemon.

He had chosen the most dangerous route of all.

Sabrina did not need either of her Pokemon to stop him. Her hair lifted as she released her own psychic power. An overwhelming force swept into Silver and his Pokemon and knocked them all to the ground.

Bang!

Silver's head swam. He was hurt and pale, but what shook him most was the power he had just felt. How could this woman be so strong? It seemed beyond anything a human should be able to do. Escape now looked impossible.

"Stop struggling," Looker said. He brought Growlithe and Croagunk closer. "Those two are far beyond what you can handle. Give up and come quietly."

A cornered beast might still fight, but Silver had not yet grown into the dangerous Team Rocket figure Looker had imagined. He could not break through the cage they had made around him. After the short, intense battle, Sneasel, Zubat, and Gastly were covered in injuries and had little strength left.

Looker took out handcuffs and stepped forward to arrest him.

At that moment, a brilliant light flared above the lake beside them. Everyone turned.

As the glare eased, Ethan narrowed his eyes. A Pokemon stood at its center, on a rock rising from the water. The remaining light hid its features, but he could make out a four-legged shape, flowing hair, and two long ribbon-like tails. It looked graceful even in silhouette.

"What Pokemon is that?" Looker called, shielding his eyes.

The light faded. Its shape became clear, and the sight startled them even more. There was no mistaking it.

"Suicune!" Looker cried.

The Pokemon's body was blue-green with white markings. A crest like a blue jewel adorned its head, and two pale streamers trailed behind it.

According to legend, Suicune had once been one of three unknown Pokemon that died in the fire at the Burned Tower. Ho-Oh restored them to life, and they became Suicune, Entei, and Raikou. Because of Ho-Oh, they were also known together as its guardians.

Looker still seemed unable to believe what he was seeing. "Why would the legendary Suicune appear here?"

No one answered. Ethan had seen and even caught several Legendary Pokemon, yet the sight of Suicune still thrilled him. He quickly took out his Pokedex and pointed it at the Pokemon.

"Beep. Suicune, the Aurora Pokemon. A Water type and one of the Legendary Pokemon. Gentle as a spring, it seeks places where clear water flows. It races over the land with gliding movements and can instantly purify dirty water. The north wind is said to rise when Suicune appears, and it runs away with that wind. It is called the embodiment of the north wind."

Suicune regarded them with calm eyes. When their gazes met, they understood why it had come. This powerful Pokemon valued peace. It had heard the fight and believed Ethan's group was disturbing and damaging the forest, so it had appeared to stop them.
